- `IMP-0789` **Redesign:** preserve product truth, content, function, constraints, and explicit brand commitments; replace the old visual world rather than polishing it. The old look is evidence of what the subject is, not authority over what it becomes.
- `IMP-0790` **Established world:** inherit it. A missing DESIGN.md does not erase a coherent identity already in code; document that identity instead of inventing a replacement.
- `IMP-0791` **Incomplete brand:** preserve confirmed assets and recognizable traits, then expand the system with the user for this surface.
- `IMP-0792` **No visual authority:** create a new world with the user.
- `IMP-0793` **Persuade:** who must act, what they should believe, which real proof, content, or assets earn that belief.
- `IMP-0794` **Operate:** the task, information, important states, frequency, constraints.
- `IMP-0795` **Read:** the reader's question, source material, structure, wayfinding.
- `IMP-0796` **Experience:** what leads, how exploration unfolds, which interaction or transition matters.
- `IMP-0797` Name the product's unique mechanism in one sentence, the audience's real scene, its cultural home, and what this first surface must prove. Note the page this category always ships and its predictable opposite; both are the rut, kept out of the seven-candidate list. A brief that paints its own picture, a product name, a titled artifact, a governing metaphor, adds its literal reading to the rut: spend at most one candidate on it and derive the rest from elsewhere in the audience's world.
- `IMP-0798` From that cultural world, list seven concrete visual systems, artifacts, places, or rituals the audience knows by heart, each with one line on why it resonates and can carry the mechanism, ordered by resonance. The audience's world includes its graphic and screen traditions, not only its physical objects: the notation, publications, identity programs, data graphics, and interfaces it reads daily. A nameable abstract system (a school of poster, a documentation standard) is as concrete a candidate as any artifact. What would this thing look like as a physical object; what did its world look like before the web? Near-duplicates count once. When more than three of the seven share one material family, the derivation stopped at the subject's most obvious artifact; dig until the list spans at least three families.
- `IMP-0799` Turn that material into complete directions: each joins a reusable visual world to a concrete first-surface experience.
- `IMP-0800` Run `.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable concept-seed --scope direction --mode <mode>` and follow what it prints. No substitute, no skip: on a new or replacement world, writing artifact code before this script has run and its assignment is acknowledged is a contract violation, whatever the harness, the model, or the time pressure; the roll is what keeps every run from converging on the category default. The script assigns the direction to build and deals catalog challengers. Fuse each challenger before judging it: the challenger supplies the form and its system grammar, the product supplies every fact, clarity wins conflicts. Weigh fused challengers against the assigned direction on exactly two axes, audience identification and product clarity. Losing to strong grounded material is a valid outcome; beating a thin or tool-monoculture list is the point. Close with a verdict per challenger, decided before any borrowing: wins (beats the assigned direction on both axes; becomes the build candidate), competitive (holds one axis; stays a full alternate), or declined (loses both). A declined challenger is not spent: name the one discipline of its system the assigned direction lacks, and raise the assigned direction to match before presenting it. A donation transfers ambition and system discipline (a palette's total commitment, a grid's density courage, a form's structural honesty), never the challenger's clothes; a lifted motif is a costume note, not a raise, and one world owns the page. Write each raise into the presented direction as its own line, named for its donor; a raise nobody can read did not happen.
- `IMP-0801` Present one direction, fully committed and already raised by the hand it beat, raises visible as named lines: world, first viewport, visitor path, signature interaction, cross-surface reach, honest risk. Route each challenger by verdict: winning and competitive challengers are full alternates with their QUALITY BAR cards and one-line case; declined challengers render demoted, compact and quiet, each carrying its verdict and what the direction kept from it, never full-size, never silently dropped, still adoptable on request. The verdict informs the user's choice, never pre-empts it; the demoted row is the hand's proof of judgment. A hand holds at most three full-card challengers: when the roll deals more, the three strongest join and the rest wait in the re-roll pool, noted in one line; dropping a challenger from the hand itself takes a named product-truth failure, disclosed. Add one card for your own top-ranked grounded candidate when it is not the assigned direction, kicker IMPECCABLE’S PICK, same anatomy as every card, with an honest risk line naming its familiarity when true: the strongest grounded direction is often where most runs in this category land, and the user deciding that trade is the point of showing it. Familiar and effective is a legitimate destination, not a failure of nerve; the pick card and the standing exit serve it at two depths. One pick card, never two, never a ranked list: a lineup of your candidates hands selection back to a taste function and invites the safest card. The pick never takes the lead position; when the dice assign your top candidate there is no pick card, and the assigned card notes it topped your list. Add re-roll with an optional one-line steer, in three registers: plain (a fresh hand, same spread), safer (your remaining conventional grounded candidates plus the canon against named competitors), bolder (foreign forms only, at full commitment). The register is the user's steering on the familiar-to-bold axis, never yours to pre-select; when the answer carries one, re-run the seed with `--register <value>` and the next `--reroll` round, and follow what it prints. A user saying "bolder" or "safer" while a direction round is open means these registers, never the bolder or harden commands. The two channels share this structure and differ only in richness: cards and boards on the decision page, names and one-liners through the structured tool, whose option list carries the assigned direction, the pick, the winning and competitive challengers, and the standing exit last; declined challengers fold into the assigned option's description as their kept lines, so the raise survives the text channel.
- `IMP-0802` **comps.** The comp round from [visualize.md](visualize.md): three compositional comps of the requested surface at its own viewport under `.impeccable/mocks/`, each with a prompt sidecar, put in front of the user; the chosen one's sidecar gets `"approved": true`. The gate counts them and reads the approval; a `start --comp` skips this phase because it already happened.
- `IMP-0803` **spec.** Measure the comp: `impeccable comp-spec --comp <comp> --grid` writes a coordinate grid over the comp; open it, name every salient region by grid span in a regions file (text and control regions narrow to an ink cluster only when it retains at least 95% of the span’s contrasting pixels; inspect the resulting crop, especially for compound controls and multiline text; `snap: false` keeps the span, and an explicit `box` is taken as drawn) (kind `plate` / `image` / `texture` for anything painted: every illustration, photograph, figure, product object, and material texture; `text` / `control` / `chrome` for what code draws; every region carries a `note` saying what the comp shows there, which the plate prompt and the gate messages read), and run `impeccable comp-spec --comp <comp> --regions <file>`. The spec carries each region's box, sampled palette, and medium; `impeccable comp-spec --print` is the build's reference from here on. Type is measured, not guessed: `impeccable font-match --measure <text region>` reads the comp's cap height, width class, and weight off the pixels, and `impeccable font-match --rank <region> --text "..."` takes its candidates from a fingerprint index of the Google Fonts catalog (the nearest faces to the crop's shape) plus any names you pass with `--candidates`, renders them at that cap height with the region's words, and ranks them by fingerprint distance (its `USE` line is the CSS); with no browser resolvable it records the catalog's nearest face and says the size is estimated, which is still the choice to build on. Do not install a browser to rank, and never write a `chosen` face into the spec by hand: the gate accepts only what font-match wrote. The spec gate refuses to close until the lead text region is measured and ranked. A region note that describes painted material (a diagram, drawing, photograph, texture) under a code kind is refused at the spec: reclassify it as a plate, or reword the note if code really draws it. The script refuses a regions file that leaves comp ink unnamed: what is never named can never be missing. It also refuses a `text` / `control` / `chrome` region larger than a quarter of the comp: that is a column, not an element. Name each element inside it (`container: true` only when it truly is one undivided element). Anything drawn is a plate: an inline SVG past an icon's budget (a diagram, notation, leader lines with arrows, a "quick approximation" of the artwork) is refused at the hero; icon-sized SVG (under 64px, a few paths) is fine, and a chart the page draws from data at runtime is a chart, not an illustration. Callout lines and arrows that annotate a drawing belong to that drawing's plate, with only their labels set as text. Split regions by what varies independently, as [region-map.md](region-map.md) sets out; a framed view is a frame plate over its own image region. A crop of the comp is never a plate (the plates gate refuses a resample of the comp region); the crop is the reference the plate is generated from. A plate region's box has to hold its whole artwork with a margin: the spec measures the artwork's contact with the box edges and refuses a box that cuts through it (`bleed: true` only when the page really crops it there), because a plate placed with `object-fit: cover` on such a box shows the artwork minus the side the box lost. Anything not in the spec does not exist on the page: no borders, rules, containers, or chrome the comp does not show. Only three concessions exist: fonts (the closest obtainable face), icon glyphs (close enough, exact if the user chose an icon library; this covers the pictogram only, never a control's chrome, so a chevron, an arrow, a dropdown's border and fill, a button's shape are the comp's), and genuine defects in the comp such as spelling errors.
- `IMP-0804` **plates.** Every raster region ships as a plate: an illustration, photo, or figure regenerated at asset resolution from its comp crop, UI text removed, at its `plate` path (isolated ink, figures, or objects use native transparent PNG so they sit on the page's own ground; photos and textures stay opaque); a texture (paper, cloth, grain) is a clean patch of the comp region mirror-tiled to size, generated only when no clean patch exists. `impeccable comp-spec --crop <id>` writes the reference; save `impeccable comp-spec --plate-prompt <id> --background transparent` to a prompt file for a cutout, or use `--background opaque` otherwise. Prefer the harness-native image tool with that crop and prompt, then `impeccable embed-prompt <plate> --prompt-file <prompt.txt>`. The API fallback is `impeccable generate-image --ref <crop.png> --prompt-file <prompt.txt> --out <plate.png> --size <WxH> --quality high --background transparent` (use `--background opaque` for full-frame assets); create the output directory first. Verify actual alpha, white foregrounds, fine edges, and clear holes on light and dark grounds; do not chroma-key native output. The plates gate scores the assets against the comp; also inspect placement and scale visually. With parallel subagents, spawn the shipped asset producer (`impeccable-asset-producer`; `impeccable_asset_producer` in codex; `/impeccable-asset-producer` in Cursor; on GitHub Copilot say "Use the impeccable-asset-producer agent") with the spec path and let it produce them all; without subagents, produce them here. A crop of the comp is a reference, never a shipping pixel. The gate checks every plate exists, is at least 1.5x the region's size, and reads as the region. Page code waits for this gate: a page written before its plates exist is a page that draws its material in CSS. A single-file deliverable changes nothing here: the plate is produced the same way and inlined as a data URI. `--force` exists for one case only, the user downgrading the comp's authority in words you quote in `--reason`; the script refuses every other reason. Score the plates before the review opens: once `build-phase advance` reports nothing but the pending review, run the plan and asset review in [component-review.md](component-review.md), where the user checks the plates and which regions code draws. A plate replaced after they accept needs a new round. No page code is written before they accept it; `build-phase advance` refuses until they do.
- `IMP-0805` **hero.** `impeccable build-phase scaffold` first: it writes the measured layout as CSS custom properties (`.impeccable/build/scaffold/layout.css`: `--r-<id>-x/y/w/h` in % of the comp, plus cap height, font-size, family, and weight where measured) and a reference page (`hero-reference.html`) with every region at its box and every plate placed. Bind the numbers to your own semantic structure, an element per region; the reference is a check on positions, never the page, and overlapping boxes are overlapping boxes. Then build only the first viewport, at the comp's own dimensions, the comp's words copied verbatim (the user approved that comp with those words; rewording is a stated decision after the hero passes, never a silent one inside it), every text region sized from its measured cap height and set in its ranked face, plates first: place every plate at its spec box (`object-fit: cover`, an `<img>`, a background image, or an inlined data URI named for it) before any text or control, capture into `.impeccable/review/hero-repro.png`, run `impeccable build-phase record hero` once so you see the plate regions read as match before any text exists, then lay the semantic layer over the plates from the spec's palette and boxes and advance. The gate first refuses while any plate is unreferenced by the source, then runs `impeccable comp-diff`, writes `.impeccable/review/diff/hero/` (side-by-side, heatmap, one paired crop per region, `report.json`; `raw-report.json` preserves the uninterpreted measurements). The report and crop labels use the gate's verdicts; `gate.reasons` lists the remaining blockers even when a region is called drift. An accepted plate is revalidated if its file, measured region, or comp changes. The gate passes at 72% overall with no hard veto outstanding (a missing region, a contradicted plate or text block, an SVG illustration, a clipped plate, invented ink block at any score); above the bar, the numeric readings become advisories printed with the pass, and the polish pass before responsive is where they get fixed: the gate also reads each text region's cap height, line count, weight, ink colour, and position against the comp, each chrome strip's height off its rule, and the frame for ink where the comp is calm (a kicker, an extra nav item, a divider), and says each miss as a number ("cap height 78px in the build, 103px in the comp"); those numbers are the edit. When it fails, open the region crops it lists, in order, before editing: a region scored `missing` needs its material, `contradicted` needs its structure re-derived from the spec box, `drift` is where size and spacing edits belong; repeated attempts do not clear unresolved blockers. After three failed attempts, present the first-viewport review from [component-review.md](component-review.md) instead of iterating. Once the user accepts a first viewport, the overall bar, the palette check and every numeric reading are advisories while the capture still matches it; a missing or unreferenced plate, an SVG illustration, an organic clip, a clipped plate, invented ink and failed rendered presence still block. When the capture drifts from what they accepted, restore it: that review is closed and is never requested again. This is where the run's ambition is won or lost, and a retry here costs minutes where a rebuild verdict at the finish costs the run.
- `IMP-0806` **sections.** Build the rest of the surface inside the spec's system: the same corner language, line weights, and palette, and nothing the comp never shows. Where the comp does not cover a region, it inherits the recorded system.
- `IMP-0807` **motion.** The signature interaction, reveals, and motion, orchestrated once rather than scattered.
- `IMP-0808` **responsive.** The other viewports, and the first viewport at common desktop widths (1280 to 1600), not only at the comp's exact size: fluid columns, no fixed-pixel grid that wraps a hundred pixels narrower. Capture `desktop.png` (1440 wide, full page) and `mobile.png` (390 wide) into `.impeccable/review/`; the gate diffs the desktop capture against the comp and refuses a first viewport that only held at the comp's width. A comp'd surface that is mobile-first was comped portrait; the plates were produced for that frame.
- `IMP-0809` **The first viewport is a thesis, not a header.** Demonstrate the mechanism immediately, at the scale the form has in life; do not trap the concept inside a standard hero or card shell. The memory test: if someone left after one viewport, what would they describe an hour later? If the honest answer is a mood, the concept has not committed yet.
- `IMP-0810` **Prove, don't claim.** Show the subject doing its job: the interface at work, the mechanism dramatized, specifics a competitor could not copy-paste. Demonstration data is design material: author it at full fidelity and label it synthetic; claims stay uninventable.
- `IMP-0811` **Author the assets; never substitute chrome.** Great surfaces live on carefully made content: names, entries, copy, covers, thumbnails, textures. In greenfield work every blank the ask round left open is yours to author at production fidelity; content is authorable, claims are labelable, no section is omittable. Gradients, glass, generic icon tiles, and many-vertex `clip-path` polygons where an authored asset belongs are the gap wearing chrome; the detector flags the last two.
- `IMP-0812` **Build the form's web leverage.** When the chosen world names a technique (canvas, WebGL, view transitions, generative motion), build the technique itself, not a static imitation of it.
- `IMP-0813` **Pace the scroll like a studio.** Vary density, scale, image, motion, and quiet inside one grammar; a dense passage earns a quiet one, and the page ends anchored by a real close. One spacing rhythm throughout, with more space above a heading than below it.
- `IMP-0814` **Use real, verified imagery when the brief implies it.** Search for the subject's physical object rather than the category; one decisive photo beats five mediocre ones. Verify stock URLs resolve.
- `IMP-0815` **Author motion as material.** Give the page the form's native motion once, orchestrated, rather than scattered hover effects. Bound expensive effects and keep content visible by default.
- `IMP-0816` **Identify the challenge**:
- `IMP-0817` What are users trying to accomplish?
- `IMP-0818` What's confusing or unclear about current experience?
- `IMP-0819` Where do users get stuck or drop off?
- `IMP-0820` What's the "aha moment" we want users to reach?
- `IMP-0821` **Understand the users**:
- `IMP-0822` What's their experience level? (Beginners, power users, mixed?)
- `IMP-0823` What's their motivation? (Excited and exploring? Required by work?)
- `IMP-0824` What's their time commitment? (5 minutes? 30 minutes?)
- `IMP-0825` What alternatives do they know? (Coming from competitor? New to category?)
- `IMP-0826` **Define success**:
- `IMP-0827` What's the minimum users need to learn to be successful?
- `IMP-0828` What's the key action we want them to take? (First project? First invite?)
- `IMP-0829` How do we know onboarding worked? (Completion rate? Time to value?)
- `IMP-0830` Demonstrate with working examples, not just descriptions
- `IMP-0831` Provide real functionality in onboarding, not separate tutorial mode
- `IMP-0832` Use progressive disclosure, teach one thing at a time
- `IMP-0833` Let experienced users skip onboarding
- `IMP-0834` Don't block access to product
- `IMP-0835` Provide "Skip" or "I'll explore on my own" options
- `IMP-0836` Get users to their "aha moment" ASAP
- `IMP-0837` Front-load most important concepts
- `IMP-0838` Teach 20% that delivers 80% of value
- `IMP-0839` Save advanced features for contextual discovery
- `IMP-0840` Teach features when users need them, not upfront
- `IMP-0841` Empty states are onboarding opportunities
- `IMP-0842` Tooltips and hints at point of use
- `IMP-0843` Don't patronize or over-explain
- `IMP-0844` Be concise and clear
- `IMP-0845` Assume users can figure out standard patterns
- `IMP-0846` Clear value proposition (what is this product?)
- `IMP-0847` What users will learn/accomplish
- `IMP-0848` Time estimate (honest about commitment)
- `IMP-0849` Option to skip (for experienced users)
- `IMP-0850` Minimal required information (collect more later)
- `IMP-0851` Explain why you're asking for each piece of information
- `IMP-0852` Smart defaults where possible
- `IMP-0853` Social login when appropriate
- `IMP-0854` Introduce 1-3 core concepts (not everything)
- `IMP-0855` Use simple language and examples
- `IMP-0856` Interactive when possible (do, don't just read)
- `IMP-0857` Progress indication (step 1 of 3)
- `IMP-0858` Guide users to accomplish something real
- `IMP-0859` Pre-populated examples or templates
- `IMP-0860` Celebrate completion (but don't overdo it)
- `IMP-0861` Clear next steps
- `IMP-0862` What will appear here (description + screenshot/illustration)
- `IMP-0863` Why it's valuable
- `IMP-0864` Clear CTA to create first item
- `IMP-0865` Example or template option
- `IMP-0866` Appear at relevant moment (first time user sees feature)
- `IMP-0867` Point directly at relevant UI element
- `IMP-0868` Brief explanation + benefit
- `IMP-0869` Dismissable (with "Don't show again" option)
- `IMP-0870` Optional "Learn more" link
- `IMP-0871` Highlight new features when they're released
- `IMP-0872` Show what's new and why it matters
- `IMP-0873` Let users try immediately
- `IMP-0874` Dismissable
- `IMP-0875` Teach features when users encounter them
- `IMP-0876` Badges or indicators on new/unused features
- `IMP-0877` Unlock complexity gradually (don't show all options immediately)
- `IMP-0878` Complex interfaces with many features
- `IMP-0879` Significant changes to existing product
- `IMP-0880` Industry-specific tools needing domain knowledge
- `IMP-0881` Spotlight specific UI elements (dim rest of page)
- `IMP-0882` Keep steps short (3-7 steps max per tour)
- `IMP-0883` Allow users to click through tour freely
- `IMP-0884` Include "Skip tour" option
- `IMP-0885` Make replayable (help menu)
- `IMP-0886` Interactive over passive (let users click real buttons)
- `IMP-0887` Focus on workflow, not features ("Create a project" not "This is the project button")
- `IMP-0888` Provide sample data so actions work
- `IMP-0889` Users need hands-on practice
- `IMP-0890` Concepts are complex or unfamiliar
- `IMP-0891` High stakes (better to practice in safe environment)
- `IMP-0892` Sandbox environment with sample data
- `IMP-0893` Clear objectives ("Create a chart showing sales by region")
- `IMP-0894` Step-by-step guidance
- `IMP-0895` Validation (confirm they did it right)
- `IMP-0896` Graduation moment (you're ready!)
- `IMP-0897` Contextual help links throughout interface
- `IMP-0898` Keyboard shortcut reference
- `IMP-0899` Search-able help center
- `IMP-0900` Video tutorials for complex workflows
- `IMP-0901` `?` icon near complex features
- `IMP-0902` "Learn more" links in tooltips
- `IMP-0903` Keyboard shortcut hints (`⌘K` shown on search box)
- `IMP-0904` **First use**: Never used this feature (emphasize value, provide template)
- `IMP-0905` **User cleared**: Intentionally deleted everything (light touch, easy to recreate)
- `IMP-0906` **No results**: Search or filter returned nothing (suggest different query, clear filters)
- `IMP-0907` **No permissions**: Can't access (explain why, how to get access)
- `IMP-0908` **Error state**: Failed to load (explain what happened, retry option)
- `IMP-0909` Force users through long onboarding before they can use product
- `IMP-0910` Patronize users with obvious explanations
- `IMP-0911` Show same tooltip repeatedly (respect dismissals)
- `IMP-0912` Block all UI during tour (let users explore)
- `IMP-0913` Create separate tutorial mode disconnected from real product
- `IMP-0914` Overwhelm with information upfront (progressive disclosure!)
- `IMP-0915` Hide "Skip" or make it hard to find
- `IMP-0916` Forget about returning users (don't show initial onboarding again)
- `IMP-0917` **Time to completion**: Can users complete onboarding quickly?
- `IMP-0918` **Comprehension**: Do users understand after completing?
- `IMP-0919` **Action**: Do users take desired next step?
- `IMP-0920` **Skip rate**: Are too many users skipping? (Maybe it's too long or not valuable)
- `IMP-0921` **Completion rate**: Are users completing? (If low, simplify)
- `IMP-0922` **Time to value**: How long until users get first value?
- `IMP-0923` **One family is often right.** Product UIs don't need display/body pairing. A well-tuned sans carries headings, buttons, labels, body, data.
- `IMP-0924` **Fixed rem scale, not fluid.** Clamp-sized headings don't serve product UI. Users view at consistent DPI, and a fluid h1 that shrinks in a sidebar looks worse, not better.
- `IMP-0925` **Tighter scale ratio.** 1.125–1.2 between steps is typical. More type elements here than on brand surfaces; exaggerated contrast creates noise.
- `IMP-0926` **Line length still applies for prose** (65–75ch). Data and compact UI can run denser; tables at 120ch+ are fine.
- `IMP-0927` State-rich semantic vocabulary: hover, focus, active, disabled, selected, loading, error, warning, success, info. Standardize these.
- `IMP-0928` Accent color used for primary actions, current selection, and state indicators only, not decoration.
- `IMP-0929` A second neutral layer for sidebars, toolbars, and panels (slightly cooler or warmer than the content surface).
- `IMP-0930` Responsive behavior is structural (collapse sidebar, responsive table, breakpoint-driven columns), not fluid typography.
- `IMP-0931` Skeleton states for loading, not spinners in the middle of content.
- `IMP-0932` Empty states that teach the interface, not "nothing here."
- `IMP-0933` Consistent affordances across the surface. Same button shape. Same form-control vocabulary. Same icon style.
- `IMP-0934` Overlays escape their container. An absolutely positioned dropdown inside an `overflow: hidden` or `overflow: auto` ancestor gets clipped; reach for `<dialog>`, the popover API, `position: fixed`, or a portal.
- `IMP-0935` 150–250 ms on most transitions. Users are in flow; don't make them wait for choreography.
- `IMP-0936` Motion conveys state, not decoration. State change, feedback, loading, reveal: nothing else.
- `IMP-0937` No orchestrated page-load sequences. Product loads into a task; users don't want to watch it load.
- `IMP-0938` Decorative motion that doesn't convey state.
- `IMP-0939` Inconsistent component vocabulary across screens. If the "save" button looks different in two places, one is wrong.
- `IMP-0940` Display fonts in UI labels, buttons, data.
- `IMP-0941` Reinventing standard affordances for flavor (custom scrollbars, weird form controls, non-standard modals).
- `IMP-0942` Heavy color or full-saturation accents on inactive states.
- `IMP-0943` Modal as first thought. Modals are usually laziness. Exhaust inline / progressive alternatives first.
- `IMP-0944` System fonts and familiar sans defaults.
- `IMP-0945` Standard navigation patterns: top bar + side nav, breadcrumbs, tabs, command palettes.
- `IMP-0946` Density. Tables with many rows, panels with many labels, dense information when users need it.
- `IMP-0947` Consistency over surprise. The same visual vocabulary screen to screen is a virtue; delight is saved for moments, not pages.
- `IMP-0948` **Measure current state**:
- `IMP-0949` **Core Web Vitals**: LCP, INP, CLS scores
- `IMP-0950` **Load time**: Time to interactive, first contentful paint
- `IMP-0951` **Bundle size**: JavaScript, CSS, image sizes
- `IMP-0952` **Runtime performance**: Frame rate, memory usage, CPU usage
- `IMP-0953` **Network**: Request count, payload sizes, waterfall
- `IMP-0954` **Identify bottlenecks**:
- `IMP-0955` What's slow? (Initial load? Interactions? Animations?)
- `IMP-0956` What's causing it? (Large images? Expensive JavaScript? Layout thrashing?)
- `IMP-0957` How bad is it? (Perceivable? Annoying? Blocking?)
- `IMP-0958` Who's affected? (All users? Mobile only? Slow connections?)
- `IMP-0959` Use modern formats (WebP, AVIF)
- `IMP-0960` Proper sizing (don't load 3000px image for 300px display)
- `IMP-0961` Lazy loading for below-fold images
- `IMP-0962` Responsive images (`srcset`, `picture` element)
- `IMP-0963` Compress images (80-85% quality is usually imperceptible)
- `IMP-0964` Use CDN for faster delivery
- `IMP-0965` Code splitting (route-based, component-based)
- `IMP-0966` Tree shaking (remove unused code)
- `IMP-0967` Remove unused dependencies
- `IMP-0968` Lazy load non-critical code
- `IMP-0969` Use dynamic imports for large components
- `IMP-0970` Remove unused CSS
- `IMP-0971` Critical CSS inline, rest async
- `IMP-0972` Minimize CSS files
- `IMP-0973` Use CSS containment for independent regions
- `IMP-0974` Use `font-display: swap` or `optional`
- `IMP-0975` Subset fonts (only characters you need)
- `IMP-0976` Preload critical fonts
- `IMP-0977` Use system fonts when appropriate
- `IMP-0978` Limit font weights loaded
- `IMP-0979` Critical resources first (async/defer non-critical)
- `IMP-0980` Preload critical assets
- `IMP-0981` Prefetch likely next pages
- `IMP-0982` Service worker for offline/caching
- `IMP-0983` HTTP/2 or HTTP/3 for multiplexing
- `IMP-0984` Use CSS `contain` property for independent regions
- `IMP-0985` Minimize DOM depth (flatter is faster)
- `IMP-0986` Reduce DOM size (fewer elements)
- `IMP-0987` Use `content-visibility: auto` for long lists
- `IMP-0988` Virtual scrolling for very long lists (react-window, TanStack Virtual)
- `IMP-0989` Use `transform` and `opacity` for reliable movement, but allow blur, filters, masks, clip paths, shadows, and color shifts when they create meaningful polish
- `IMP-0990` Avoid casual animation of layout-driving properties (`width`, `height`, `top`, `left`, margins)
- `IMP-0991` Use `will-change` sparingly for known expensive operations
- `IMP-0992` Bound expensive paint areas for blur/filter/shadow effects (smaller and isolated is faster)
- `IMP-0993` Target 16ms per frame (60fps)
- `IMP-0994` Use `requestAnimationFrame` for JS animations
- `IMP-0995` Debounce/throttle scroll handlers
- `IMP-0996` Use CSS animations when possible
- `IMP-0997` Avoid long-running JavaScript during animations
- `IMP-0998` Use `memo()` for expensive components
- `IMP-0999` `useMemo()` and `useCallback()` for expensive computations
- `IMP-1000` Virtualize long lists
- `IMP-1001` Code split routes
- `IMP-1002` Avoid inline function creation in render
- `IMP-1003` Use React DevTools Profiler
- `IMP-1004` Minimize re-renders
- `IMP-1005` Debounce expensive operations
- `IMP-1006` Memoize computed values
- `IMP-1007` Lazy load routes and components
- `IMP-1008` Combine small files
- `IMP-1009` Use SVG sprites for icons
- `IMP-1010` Inline small critical assets
- `IMP-1011` Remove unused third-party scripts
- `IMP-1012` Use pagination (don't load everything)
- `IMP-1013` GraphQL to request only needed fields
- `IMP-1014` Response compression (gzip, brotli)
- `IMP-1015` HTTP caching headers
- `IMP-1016` CDN for static assets
- `IMP-1017` Adaptive loading based on connection (navigator.connection)
- `IMP-1018` Optimistic UI updates
- `IMP-1019` Request prioritization
- `IMP-1020` Progressive enhancement
- `IMP-1021` Optimize hero images
- `IMP-1022` Inline critical CSS
- `IMP-1023` Preload key resources
- `IMP-1024` Use CDN
- `IMP-1025` Server-side rendering
- `IMP-1026` Break up long tasks
- `IMP-1027` Defer non-critical JavaScript
- `IMP-1028` Use web workers for heavy computation
- `IMP-1029` Reduce JavaScript execution time
- `IMP-1030` Set dimensions on images and videos
- `IMP-1031` Don't inject content above existing content
- `IMP-1032` Use `aspect-ratio` CSS property
- `IMP-1033` Reserve space for ads/embeds
- `IMP-1034` Avoid animations that cause layout shifts
- `IMP-1035` Chrome DevTools (Lighthouse, Performance panel)
- `IMP-1036` WebPageTest
- `IMP-1037` Core Web Vitals (Chrome UX Report)
- `IMP-1038` Bundle analyzers (webpack-bundle-analyzer)
- `IMP-1039` Performance monitoring (Sentry, DataDog, New Relic)
- `IMP-1040` LCP, INP, CLS (Core Web Vitals; INP replaced FID in March 2024)
- `IMP-1041` Time to Interactive (TTI)
- `IMP-1042` First Contentful Paint (FCP)
- `IMP-1043` Total Blocking Time (TBT)
- `IMP-1044` Bundle size
- `IMP-1045` Request count
- `IMP-1046` Optimize without measuring (premature optimization)
- `IMP-1047` Sacrifice accessibility for performance
- `IMP-1048` Break functionality while optimizing
- `IMP-1049` Use `will-change` everywhere (creates new layers, uses memory)
- `IMP-1050` Lazy load above-fold content
- `IMP-1051` Optimize micro-optimizations while ignoring major issues (optimize the biggest bottleneck first)
- `IMP-1052` Forget about mobile performance (often slower devices, slower connections)
- `IMP-1053` **Before/after metrics**: Compare Lighthouse scores
- `IMP-1054` **Real user monitoring**: Track improvements for real users
- `IMP-1055` **Different devices**: Test on low-end Android, not just flagship iPhone
- `IMP-1056` **Slow connections**: Throttle to 3G, test experience
- `IMP-1057` **No regressions**: Ensure functionality still works
- `IMP-1058` **User perception**: Does it *feel* faster?
- `IMP-1059` **Think through 2-3 different directions**: consider different techniques, levels of ambition, and aesthetic approaches. For each direction, briefly describe what the result would look and feel like.
- `IMP-1060` **Get the user's pick before writing any code.** STOP and call the AskUserQuestion tool to clarify. Carry each direction's description and its trade-offs (browser support, performance cost, complexity) inside the option itself, so the user is choosing between things they can read. A structured question blocks the message it rides in until the user answers, so directions written alongside the question stay invisible while the user is being asked to choose between them.
- `IMP-1061` Only proceed with the direction the user confirms.
- `IMP-1062` **View Transitions API** (same-document: all browsers; cross-document: no Firefox): shared element morphing between states. A list item expanding into a detail page. A button morphing into a dialog. This is the closest thing to native FLIP animations.
- `IMP-1063` **`@starting-style`** (all browsers): animate elements from `display: none` to visible with CSS only, including entry keyframes
- `IMP-1064` **Spring physics**: natural motion with mass, tension, and damping instead of cubic-bezier. Libraries: motion (formerly Framer Motion), GSAP, or roll your own spring solver.
- `IMP-1065` **Scroll-driven animations** (`animation-timeline: scroll()`): CSS-only, no JS. Parallax, progress bars, reveal sequences all driven by scroll position. (Chrome/Edge/Safari; Firefox: flag only; always provide a static fallback)
- `IMP-1066` **WebGL** (all browsers): shader effects, post-processing, particle systems. Libraries: Three.js, OGL (lightweight), regl. Use for effects CSS can't express.
- `IMP-1067` **WebGPU** (Chrome/Edge; Safari 26+; Firefox on Windows/macOS; flag only on Firefox Linux/Android): next-gen GPU compute, more powerful than WebGL. Always fall back to WebGL2.
- `IMP-1068` **Canvas 2D / OffscreenCanvas**: custom rendering, pixel manipulation, or moving heavy rendering off the main thread entirely via Web Workers + OffscreenCanvas.
- `IMP-1069` **SVG filter chains**: displacement maps, turbulence, morphology for organic distortion effects. CSS-animatable.
- `IMP-1070` **Virtual scrolling**: render only visible rows for tables/lists with tens of thousands of items. No library required for simple cases; TanStack Virtual for complex ones.
- `IMP-1071` **GPU-accelerated charts**: Canvas or WebGL-rendered data visualization for datasets too large for SVG/DOM. Libraries: deck.gl, regl-based custom renderers.
- `IMP-1072` **Animated data transitions**: morph between chart states rather than replacing. D3's `transition()` or View Transitions for DOM-based charts.
- `IMP-1073` **`@property`** (all browsers): register custom CSS properties with types, enabling animation of gradients, colors, and complex values that CSS can't normally interpolate.
- `IMP-1074` **Web Animations API** (all browsers): JavaScript-driven animations with the performance of CSS. Composable, cancellable, reversible. The foundation for complex choreography.
- `IMP-1075` **Web Workers**: move computation off the main thread. Heavy data processing, image manipulation, search indexing: anything that would cause jank.
- `IMP-1076` **OffscreenCanvas**: render in a Worker thread. The main thread stays free while complex visuals render in the background.
- `IMP-1077` **WASM**: near-native performance for computation-heavy features. Image processing, physics simulations, codecs.
- `IMP-1078` **Web Audio API**: spatial audio, audio-reactive visualizations, sonic feedback. Requires user gesture to start.
- `IMP-1079` **Device APIs**: orientation, ambient light, geolocation. Use sparingly and always with user permission.
- `IMP-1080` Target 60fps. If dropping below 50, simplify.
- `IMP-1081` Lazy-initialize heavy resources (WebGL contexts, WASM modules) only when near viewport.
- `IMP-1082` Pause off-screen rendering. Kill what you can't see.
- `IMP-1083` Test on real mid-range devices, not just your development machine.
- `IMP-1084` Ship effects that cause jank on mid-range devices
- `IMP-1085` Use bleeding-edge APIs without a functional fallback
- `IMP-1086` Add sound without explicit user opt-in
- `IMP-1087` Use technical ambition to mask weak design fundamentals; fix those first with other commands
- `IMP-1088` Layer multiple competing extraordinary moments. Focus creates impact, excess creates noise
- `IMP-1089` **The wow test**: Show it to someone who hasn't seen it. Do they react?
- `IMP-1090` **The removal test**: Take it away. Does the experience feel diminished, or does nobody notice?
- `IMP-1091` **The device test**: Run it on a phone, a tablet, a Chromebook. Still smooth?
- `IMP-1092` **The context test**: Does this make sense for THIS brand and audience?
- `IMP-1093` **missing token:** the system needs a reusable value;
- `IMP-1094` **one-off implementation:** an existing shared component or pattern should replace it;
- `IMP-1095` **conceptual mismatch:** the flow, information architecture, or hierarchy differs from comparable product areas;
- `IMP-1096` **local defect:** the implementation is simply incomplete or inconsistent.
- `IMP-1097` whether the path is functionally complete;
- `IMP-1098` the intended quality bar and time available;
- `IMP-1099` known constraints or deliberately unfinished work;
- `IMP-1100` the states, content lengths, roles, and input methods users will actually encounter.
- `IMP-1101` broken or blocked tasks, data loss, misleading state, and inaccessible paths;
- `IMP-1102` missing loading, empty, error, success, disabled, and permission states;
- `IMP-1103` flow, hierarchy, responsive, and design-system drift;
- `IMP-1104` visual and motion inconsistencies;
- `IMP-1105` code and asset cleanup.
- `IMP-1106` Match neighboring mental models, terminology, disclosure, routing, save behavior, and optimistic or pessimistic patterns.
- `IMP-1107` Make the primary task and current state obvious without flattening every element to equal weight.
- `IMP-1108` Ensure arrival, transition, empty, and recovery paths connect instead of behaving as isolated screens.
- `IMP-1109` Align to the project's grid and spacing scale; fix optical as well as mathematical alignment.
- `IMP-1110` Group related content tightly and separate distinct groups generously.
- `IMP-1111` Keep same-role typography consistent; test measure, wrapping, localization expansion, zoom, and font loading.
- `IMP-1112` Verify every supported viewport rather than correcting only the current screenshot.
- `IMP-1113` Use semantic tokens and stable color meanings across themes.
- `IMP-1114` Verify text, control, and focus contrast in every state.
- `IMP-1115` Keep icon families, stroke/weight, sizing, and optical alignment coherent.
- `IMP-1116` Prevent image layout shift; use correct aspect ratios, responsive sources, and useful alt text. [H04]
- `IMP-1117` Every control needs appropriate default, hover, focus, active, disabled, loading, error, and success behavior.
- `IMP-1118` Preserve visible keyboard focus, logical tab order, labels, and platform-appropriate touch targets.
- `IMP-1119` Keep motion coherent, interruptible, and performant. Do not add animation merely to make polish visible.
- `IMP-1120` Validate long, missing, localized, offline, slow, and permission-limited content where the product can encounter it.
- `IMP-1121` Keep terminology, capitalization, punctuation, and factual copy consistent. Ask before changing claims.
- `IMP-1122` Remove debug output, dead code, unused imports, obsolete styles, and polish-created duplication.
- `IMP-1123` Replace custom implementations with shared components where the system owns the pattern.
- `IMP-1124` Promote genuinely reusable values to tokens; do not create a system abstraction for one local exception.
- `IMP-1125` mobile, intermediate, and wide layouts on the web; phone and tablet size classes in both supported orientations on native;
- `IMP-1126` loading, empty, error, success, disabled, long-content, and missing-content states;
- `IMP-1127` zoom, contrast, focus, semantics, and screen-reader names;
- `IMP-1128` console errors, layout shift, interaction latency, and image loading everywhere; supported browsers on the web; supported OS versions, runtime warnings, and dropped frames on native;
- `IMP-1129` agreement with DESIGN.md, neighboring features, and the user's scope.
- `IMP-1130` **Identify intensity sources**:
- `IMP-1131` **Color saturation**: Overly bright or saturated colors
- `IMP-1132` **Contrast extremes**: Too much high-contrast juxtaposition
- `IMP-1133` **Visual weight**: Too many bold, heavy elements competing
- `IMP-1134` **Animation excess**: Too much motion or overly dramatic effects
- `IMP-1135` **Complexity**: Too many visual elements, patterns, or decorations
- `IMP-1136` **Scale**: Everything is large and loud with no hierarchy
- `IMP-1137` **Understand the context**:
- `IMP-1138` What's the purpose? (Marketing vs tool vs reading experience)
- `IMP-1139` Who's the audience? (Some contexts need energy)
- `IMP-1140` What's working? (Don't throw away good ideas)
- `IMP-1141` What's the core message? (Preserve what matters)
- `IMP-1142` **Color approach**: Desaturate or shift to more restrained tones?
- `IMP-1143` **Hierarchy approach**: Which elements should stay bold (very few), which should recede?
- `IMP-1144` **Simplification approach**: What can be removed entirely?
- `IMP-1145` **Sophistication approach**: How can we signal quality through restraint?
- `IMP-1146` **Reduce saturation**: Shift from fully saturated to 70-85% saturation
- `IMP-1147` **Soften palette**: Replace bright colors with muted tones
- `IMP-1148` **Reduce color variety**: Use fewer colors more thoughtfully
- `IMP-1149` **Neutral dominance**: Let neutrals do more work, use color as accent (10% rule)
- `IMP-1150` **Gentler contrasts**: High contrast only where it matters most
- `IMP-1151` **Tinted grays**: Use warm or cool tinted grays instead of pure gray. Adds depth without loudness
- `IMP-1152` **Never gray on color**: If you have gray text on a colored background, use a darker shade of that color or transparency instead
- `IMP-1153` **Typography**: Reduce font weights (900 → 600, 700 → 500), decrease sizes where appropriate
- `IMP-1154` **Hierarchy through subtlety**: Use weight, size, and space instead of color and boldness
- `IMP-1155` **White space**: Increase breathing room, reduce density
- `IMP-1156` **Borders & lines**: Reduce thickness, decrease opacity, or remove entirely
- `IMP-1157` **Remove decorative elements**: Gradients, shadows, patterns, textures that don't serve purpose
- `IMP-1158` **Simplify shapes**: Reduce border radius extremes, simplify custom shapes
- `IMP-1159` **Reduce layering**: Flatten visual hierarchy where possible
- `IMP-1160` **Clean up effects**: Reduce or remove blur effects, glows, multiple shadows
- `IMP-1161` **Reduce animation intensity**: Shorter distances (10-20px instead of 40px), gentler easing
- `IMP-1162` **Remove decorative animations**: Keep functional motion, remove flourishes
- `IMP-1163` **Subtle micro-interactions**: Replace dramatic effects with gentle feedback
- `IMP-1164` **Refined easing**: Use ease-out-quart for smooth, understated motion. Never bounce or elastic
- `IMP-1165` **Remove animations entirely** if they're not serving a clear purpose
- `IMP-1166` **Reduce scale jumps**: Smaller contrast between sizes creates calmer feeling
- `IMP-1167` **Align to grid**: Bring rogue elements back into systematic alignment
- `IMP-1168` **Even out spacing**: Replace extreme spacing variations with consistent rhythm
- `IMP-1169` Make everything the same size/weight (hierarchy still matters)
- `IMP-1170` Remove all color (quiet ≠ grayscale)
- `IMP-1171` Eliminate all personality (maintain character through refinement)
- `IMP-1172` Sacrifice usability for aesthetics (functional elements still need clear affordances)
- `IMP-1173` Make everything small and light (some anchors needed)
- `IMP-1174` **Still functional**: Can users still accomplish tasks easily?
- `IMP-1175` **Still distinctive**: Does it have character, or is it generic now?
- `IMP-1176` **Better reading**: Is text easier to read for extended periods?
- `IMP-1177` **Restrained, not absent**: Does the POV survive the cuts?
- `IMP-1178` Use the structured question tool when available; otherwise ask and stop.
- `IMP-1179` Ask two or three related questions per round, then wait. One round is the default; add a second only when the answers expose a material gap.
- `IMP-1180` Do not dump a questionnaire, repeat settled facts, or turn obvious facts into menus. Assert the likely reading and invite correction.
- `IMP-1181` A sparse prompt requires at least one answer round. A precise prompt may need only a compact confirmation.
- `IMP-1182` What is this surface or feature for, and what problem must it solve?
- `IMP-1183` Who specifically reaches it, in what situation and state of mind?
- `IMP-1184` What is the primary thing they must understand or do? What would success look like?
- `IMP-1185` What is uniquely true here that a neighboring product or generic template could not claim?
- `IMP-1186` What real content, evidence, data, and assets must the experience carry? What are realistic minimum, typical, and maximum ranges?
- `IMP-1187` Which states and transitions matter: first-run, empty, loading, error, success, permissions, overflow, or expert use?
- `IMP-1188` What is the intended fidelity, breadth, and interactivity: exploration, production-ready screen, full flow, or broader surface?
- `IMP-1189` What must remain untouched? What would make the result feel wrong even if it looked polished?
- `IMP-1190` Which platform, framework, performance, accessibility, localization, or delivery constraints are binding?
- `IMP-1191` **Job and audience:** who arrives, their context, need, and visitor mode.
- `IMP-1192` **Outcome and proof:** primary task/action, success, real evidence, and product-specific truth.
- `IMP-1193` **Selected direction:** visual authority, structural/interaction thesis, sequence, focal moment, and implementation consequence.
- `IMP-1194` **Scope and boundaries:** fidelity, breadth, interactivity, named target, what remains untouched, and explicit anti-goals.
- `IMP-1195` **States and ranges:** realistic content/data ranges and material states.
- `IMP-1196` **Interaction and layout:** hierarchy, topology, responsiveness, affordances, feedback, and transitions; intent, not CSS.
- `IMP-1197` **Constraints and open decisions:** platform, delivery, accessibility, localization, reusable components, and choices a builder must not invent.
- `IMP-1198` **Persuade + Experience:** display type may carry the voice. Use decisive contrast and responsive scale when the composition benefits.
- `IMP-1199` **Operate + Read:** stability, scanability, and measure come first. A single well-tuned family and fixed role scale are often right.
- `IMP-1200` **Native:** follow [ios.md](ios.md) or [android.md](android.md), including platform scaling and accessibility behavior.
- `IMP-1201` **Typographic assessment:** inspect representative pages and styles. Answer every question below with a file, selector, or computed value:
- `IMP-1202` **Authority and fit:** Which faces, weights, and roles are established? Do they fit the product and selected world, or are they unexamined defaults? Is every family necessary?
- `IMP-1203` **Hierarchy:** Can heading, body, label, metadata, and data roles be distinguished at a glance? Are adjacent sizes or weights too close to carry different jobs?
- `IMP-1204` **Scale and consistency:** Is there a deliberate role scale, or a collection of arbitrary values? Do repeated roles stay identical across screens and states?
- `IMP-1205` **Reading:** Does body copy stay within a comfortable 45–75 character measure? Are line height, paragraph rhythm, contrast, and tracking tuned to the actual face, width, language, and surface?
- `IMP-1206` **Stress:** What happens with long headings, localization expansion, zoom, narrow containers, missing weights, and font fallback?
- `IMP-1207` **Delivery:** Are only used assets loaded? Do fallback metrics, loading strategy, and variable-font settings avoid invisible text and disruptive reflow?
- `IMP-1208` **Mechanical scan:** run:
- `IMP-1209` the roles the interface needs;
- `IMP-1210` the intended contrast between those roles;
- `IMP-1211` the reading measure and density;
- `IMP-1212` which existing faces and weights are authoritative;
- `IMP-1213` any performance, localization, or accessibility constraints.
- `IMP-1214` Keep body copy comfortably readable and zoomable. Use 1rem / 16px as the ordinary web body floor unless a dense role, platform convention, or user setting justifies otherwise.
- `IMP-1215` Keep prose in the 45–75ch range. Tune line height inversely with measure: wider lines generally need more leading.
- `IMP-1216` Compensate light text on dark surfaces on all three perceptual axes: slightly more line height, a touch more tracking, and one step more weight when the face needs it.
- `IMP-1217` Tune line height to the face, width, language, and contrast, not a universal ratio.
- `IMP-1218` Keep repeated roles consistent across screens and states.
- `IMP-1219` Use numeric, tabular, code, and label features when their content benefits.
- `IMP-1220` Load only used font assets and weights. Provide metric-compatible fallbacks and avoid blocking text.
- `IMP-1221` Let marketing display type respond to available space when useful; keep dense product and reading surfaces spatially predictable.
- `IMP-1222` Preserve browser zoom, user font settings, Dynamic Type, and platform text scaling.
- `IMP-1223` Use paragraph spacing or first-line indentation as the primary paragraph rhythm; combining both usually double-marks the boundary.
- `IMP-1224` Primary, secondary, body, and metadata roles are recognizable without reading the copy.
- `IMP-1225` Long text remains comfortable across relevant widths and languages.
- `IMP-1226` The typography belongs to the product and its established world.
- `IMP-1227` Loading does not create disruptive reflow or invisible text.
- `IMP-1228` Zoom, text scaling, focus, contrast, and reduced viewport paths remain usable.
- `IMP-1229` The final mechanical scan has no unexplained findings.
- `IMP-1230` Go all out. No hedging, no shortcuts. The deliverable must be complete (except assets the user must provide).
- `IMP-1231` Dream big and bold. Distinct, beautiful, outstanding and highly inspiring work.
- `IMP-1232` Verify in bounded passes, not a loop, and the ceiling covers the whole cycle: screenshots, defect scans, micro-edits, and rebuilds alike. Build fully, inspect once with a batched round (desktop and mobile together on the web; the shipped device classes on a native platform), fix everything it shows in one batch, confirm with at most one more round, and stop polishing. Open-ended self-QA burns the user's money doing worse what the finish handoffs do better.
- `IMP-1233` Run `<skill-base-dir>/scripts/impeccable context` once per session, where `<skill-base-dir>` is the directory that contains this SKILL.md (the skill folder, not a plugin root two levels above it); keep cwd at the user's project. That base directory resolves every `.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable <verb>` command in this skill and its references, and `.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts` is the fallback only when the runtime reports no base directory. On a Windows shell without `sh`, call `.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable.cmd` instead. The launcher runs a self-contained binary that ships next to it or is downloaded once on first run; no Node or other runtime is required. Pass a named source file or route as `--target <path>`. It loads PRODUCT.md, DESIGN.md, the matching surface brief, and native-platform guidance when applicable; follow its directives and do not rerun it.
- `IMP-1234` Load the request's playbook: its Commands-table reference for an explicit/implied sub-command, or [reference/new-work.md](reference/new-work.md) for a new surface or replacement visual world. Inspect target and incumbent visual truth before editing. When the app cannot run, start with committed visual-regression goldens or screenshot fixtures; verify target and freshness against current tokens, CSS, components, or assets, resolve conflicts, and compare theme/variant captures.
- `IMP-1235` After resolving analysis and direction, read [reference/craft-floor.md](reference/craft-floor.md) immediately before any UI edit, including small refinements. It carries the quality floor, the absolute bans, and the reflexes no detector catches. Do not load it for planning-only work.
- `IMP-1236` **The brief wins.** Honor pinned aesthetics, eras, materials, fonts, and palettes even when they conflict with a saturated-pattern warning. Redirecting a clear brief toward your taste is failure.
- `IMP-1237` **Refinement preserves; redesign replaces.** Refinement keeps the incumbent identity, behavior, copy, and everything outside scope. Ask before replacing factual copy or adding claims. Redesign keeps product truth, content, function, native affordances, and constraints, but treats the old look as evidence and anti-reference; choose a replacement world in new-work and replace DESIGN.md. Never split the difference into polish on the discarded look.
- `IMP-1238` **Visual authority is evidence, not a filename.** Missing DESIGN.md alone does not make a project greenfield; new-work decides whether to preserve, expand, or replace the incumbent world.
- `IMP-1239` **Persuade:** the visitor decides and acts; design is the product. Landing pages, marketing, campaigns, pricing. Earn attention and action. Ship real imagery when the brief needs it; follow the committed world, not category habit.
- `IMP-1240` **Operate:** the visitor completes a task. App UI, dashboards, editors, admin, settings, tools. Scanability, consistency, native expectations, and the real usage scene outrank expression. Brand lives in precise details.
- `IMP-1241` **Read:** the visitor understands something. Docs, articles, guides, help, changelogs. Structure for comprehension, then make the reading experience worth staying in.
- `IMP-1242` **Experience:** the visitor is inside the work itself. Portfolios, galleries, showcases. Let the artifact lead from the first viewport; the interface recedes.
- `IMP-1243` **No argument:** read [routing.md](reference/routing.md) and present its context-aware menu; never auto-run a command.
- `IMP-1244` **Explicit or clearly implied request to run a command:** load its reference (native variant on native platforms) and follow it. Ask once if two commands fit.
- `IMP-1245` **Workflow or command-selection question:** read [Workflow questions](reference/routing.md#workflow-questions).
- `IMP-1246` **Otherwise:** treat the request as general design work. Missing PRODUCT.md routes a new surface or replacement world through init, then new-work; a narrow refinement of existing code proceeds on the incumbent implementation as `impeccable context` directs, offering init afterward rather than blocking on it.
- `IMP-1247` `teach` aliases `init`. `craft` is a deprecated alias for ordinary new-work and adds nothing. `shape` owns task discovery, then enters new-work only for visual-world and surface-concept decisions.

### taste-skill

- `TASTE-001` **Page kind** - landing (SaaS / consumer / agency / event), portfolio (dev / designer / creative studio), redesign (preserve vs overhaul), editorial / blog.
- `TASTE-002` **Vibe words** the user used - "minimalist", "calm", "Linear-style", "Awwwards", "brutalist", "premium consumer", "Apple-y", "playful", "serious B2B", "editorial", "agency-y", "glassy", "dark tech".
- `TASTE-003` **Reference signals** - URLs they linked, screenshots they pasted, products they named, brands they're competing with.
- `TASTE-004` **Audience** - B2B procurement panel vs. design-conscious consumer vs. recruiter scanning a portfolio. The audience picks the aesthetic, not your taste.
- `TASTE-005` **Brand assets that already exist** - logo, color, type, photography. For redesigns, these are starting material, not optional input (see Section 11).
- `TASTE-006` **Quiet constraints** - accessibility-first audiences, public-sector, regulated industries, trust-first commerce, kids' products. These constraints OVERRIDE aesthetic preference.
- `TASTE-007` *"Reading this as: B2B SaaS landing for technical buyers, with a Linear-style minimalist language, leaning toward Tailwind utilities + Geist + restrained motion."*
- `TASTE-008` *"Reading this as: solo designer portfolio for hiring managers, with an editorial / kinetic-type language, leaning toward native CSS + scroll-driven animation + custom typography."*
- `TASTE-009` *"Reading this as: redesign of a public-sector service site, with a trust-first language, leaning toward GOV.UK Frontend or USWDS."*
- `TASTE-010` **`DESIGN_VARIANCE: 8`** - 1 = Perfect Symmetry, 10 = Artsy Chaos
- `TASTE-011` **`MOTION_INTENSITY: 6`** - 1 = Static, 10 = Cinematic / Physics
- `TASTE-012` **`VISUAL_DENSITY: 4`** - 1 = Art Gallery / Airy, 10 = Cockpit / Packed Data
- `TASTE-013` **Framework:** React or Next.js. Default to Server Components (RSC).
- `TASTE-014` **RSC SAFETY:** Global state works ONLY in Client Components. In Next.js, wrap providers in a `"use client"` component.
- `TASTE-015` **INTERACTIVITY ISOLATION:** Any component using Motion, scroll listeners, or pointer physics MUST be an isolated leaf with `'use client'` at the top. Server Components render static layouts only.
- `TASTE-016` **Styling:** **Tailwind v4** (default). Tailwind v3 only if the existing project demands it.
- `TASTE-017` For v4: do NOT use `tailwindcss` plugin in `postcss.config.js`. Use `@tailwindcss/postcss` or the Vite plugin.
- `TASTE-018` **Animation:** **Motion** (the library formerly known as Framer Motion). Import from `motion/react` (`import { motion } from "motion/react"`). The `framer-motion` package still works as a legacy alias - prefer `motion/react` in new code.
- `TASTE-019` **Fonts:** Always use `next/font` (Next.js) or self-host with `@font-face` + `font-display: swap`. Never link Google Fonts via `<link>` in production.
- `TASTE-020` Local `useState` / `useReducer` for isolated UI.
- `TASTE-021` Global state ONLY for deep prop-drilling avoidance - Zustand, Jotai, or React context.
- `TASTE-022` **NEVER** use `useState` to track continuous values driven by user input (mouse position, scroll progress, pointer physics, magnetic hover). Use Motion's `useMotionValue` / `useTransform` / `useScroll`. `useState` re-renders the React tree on every change and collapses on mobile.
- `TASTE-023` **Allowed libraries (priority order):** `@phosphor-icons/react`, `hugeicons-react`, `@radix-ui/react-icons`, `@tabler/icons-react`.
- `TASTE-024` **Discouraged:** `lucide-react`. Acceptable only when the user explicitly asks for it or the project already depends on it.
- `TASTE-025` **NEVER hand-roll SVG icons.** If a glyph is missing, install a second library or compose from primitives - do not draw icon paths from scratch.
- `TASTE-026` **One family per project.** Do not mix Phosphor with Lucide in the same component tree.
- `TASTE-027` **Standardize `strokeWidth` globally** (e.g. `1.5` or `2.0`).
- `TASTE-028` Standardize breakpoints (`sm 640`, `md 768`, `lg 1024`, `xl 1280`, `2xl 1536`).
- `TASTE-029` Contain page layouts using `max-w-[1400px] mx-auto` or `max-w-7xl`.
- `TASTE-030` **Viewport Stability:** NEVER use `h-screen` for full-height Hero sections. ALWAYS use `min-h-[100dvh]` to prevent layout jumping on mobile (iOS Safari address bar).
- `TASTE-031` **Grid over Flex-Math:** NEVER use complex flexbox percentage math (`w-[calc(33%-1rem)]`). ALWAYS use CSS Grid (`grid grid-cols-1 md:grid-cols-3 gap-6`).
- `TASTE-032` **Display / Headlines:** Default `text-4xl md:text-6xl tracking-tighter leading-none`.
- `TASTE-033` **Body / Paragraphs:** Default `text-base text-gray-600 leading-relaxed max-w-[65ch]`.
- `TASTE-034` **Sans font choice:**
- `TASTE-035` **Discouraged as default:** `Inter`. Pick `Geist`, `Outfit`, `Cabinet Grotesk`, `Satoshi`, or a brand-appropriate serif first.
- `TASTE-036` **Override:** Inter is acceptable when the user explicitly asks for a neutral / standard / Linear-style feel, or when the brief is a public-sector / accessibility-first site.
- `TASTE-037` **Pairings to know:** `Geist` + `Geist Mono`, `Satoshi` + `JetBrains Mono`, `Cabinet Grotesk` + `Inter Tight`, `GT America` + `IBM Plex Mono`.
- `TASTE-038` **SERIF DISCIPLINE (VERY DISCOURAGED AS DEFAULT):**
- `TASTE-039` Serif is **very discouraged as the default font for any project.** "It feels creative / premium / editorial" is NOT a reason to reach for serif. The agent's default mental model that "creative brief = serif" is the single most-tested AI tell in production rounds.
- `TASTE-040` **Serif is only acceptable when ONE of these is explicitly true:**
- `TASTE-041` The brand brief literally names a serif font, OR
- `TASTE-042` The aesthetic family is genuinely editorial / luxury / publication / manuscript / heritage / vintage AND you can articulate why this specific serif fits this specific brand
- `TASTE-043` For everything else (creative agency, design studio, modern brand, premium consumer, portfolio, lifestyle), **default sans-serif display** (Geist Display, ABC Diatype, Söhne Breit, Cabinet Grotesk Display, Migra Sans, GT Walsheim, Inter Display, PP Neue Montreal). Sans display fonts are not "boring" — they are the default for the same reason black is the default in fashion.
- `TASTE-044` **EMPHASIS RULE (related):** When you want to emphasize a word within a headline (the kinetic "and `spatial` design" type move), use **italic or bold of the SAME font**. Do NOT inject a random serif word into a sans headline (or vice versa) just to add visual interest. Mixed-family emphasis is amateur. Italic/bold emphasis in the same family is the right move.
- `TASTE-045` **Specifically BANNED as defaults:** `Fraunces` and `Instrument_Serif` (the two LLM-favorite display serifs).
- `TASTE-046` **If a serif is justified** (rare, per the above), rotate from this pool, do NOT reuse the same serif across consecutive projects: PP Editorial New, GT Sectra Display, Cardinal Grotesque, Reckless Neue, Tiempos Headline, Recoleta, Cormorant Garamond, Playfair Display, EB Garamond, IvyPresto, Migra, Editorial Old, Saol Display, Söhne Breit Kursiv, Domaine Display, Canela, Schnyder, Tobias, NB Architekt, ITC Galliard.
- `TASTE-047` **ITALIC DESCENDER CLEARANCE (mandatory):** When italic is used in display type and the word contains a descender letter (`y g j p q`), `leading-[1]` or `leading-none` will clip the descender. Use `leading-[1.1]` minimum and add `pb-1` or `mb-1` reserve on the wrapping element. Audit every italic word in display headlines before shipping.
- `TASTE-048` Max 1 accent color. Saturation < 80% by default. [D05]
- `TASTE-049` **THE LILA RULE:** The "AI Purple / Blue glow" aesthetic is discouraged as a default. No automatic purple button glows, no random neon gradients. Use neutral bases (Zinc / Slate / Stone) with high-contrast singular accents (Emerald, Electric Blue, Deep Rose, Burnt Orange, etc.).
- `TASTE-050` **Override:** if the brand or brief explicitly asks for purple / violet / lila, embrace it. But execute with intent: consistent palette, harmonised neutrals, restrained gradients. Not generic AI gradient slop.
- `TASTE-051` **One palette per project.** Do not fluctuate between warm and cool grays within the same project.
- `TASTE-052` **COLOR CONSISTENCY LOCK (mandatory):** Once an accent color is chosen for a page, it is used on the WHOLE page. A warm-grey site does not suddenly get a blue CTA in section 7. A rose-accented site does not get a teal status badge in the footer. Pick one accent, lock it, audit every component before shipping.
- `TASTE-053` **PREMIUM-CONSUMER PALETTE BAN (mandatory, second-most-recurring AI-tell):**
- `TASTE-054` For premium-consumer briefs (cookware, wellness, artisan, luxury, heritage craft, DTC home goods, etc.) the LLM default is **warm beige/cream + brass/clay/oxblood/ochre + espresso/ink dark text**. Concretely banned hex families as default backgrounds and accents:
- `TASTE-055` Backgrounds: `#f5f1ea`, `#f7f5f1`, `#fbf8f1`, `#efeae0`, `#ece6db`, `#faf7f1`, `#e8dfcb` (all "warm paper / cream / chalk / bone")
- `TASTE-056` Accents: `#b08947`, `#b6553a`, `#9a2436`, `#9c6e2a`, `#bc7c3a`, `#7d5621` (all "brass / clay / oxblood / ochre")
- `TASTE-057` Text: `#1a1714`, `#1a1814`, `#1b1814` (all "espresso / warm near-black")
- `TASTE-058` This palette is BANNED as the default reach for premium-consumer briefs. Every premium-consumer site you have ever shipped uses this exact palette. The brand becomes invisible.
- `TASTE-059` **Default alternatives (rotate, do not reuse):**
- `TASTE-060` **Cold Luxury:** silver-grey + chrome + smoke (think Tesla, Apple Watch Hermes-without-the-leather)
- `TASTE-061` **Forest:** deep green + bone + amber accent (think Filson, Patagonia premium)
- `TASTE-062` **Black and Tan:** true off-black + warm tan, sharp contrast, no beige
- `TASTE-063` **Cobalt + Cream:** saturated blue against a single neutral, no brass
- `TASTE-064` **Terracotta + Slate:** warm rust against cool grey, no brass
- `TASTE-065` **Olive + Brick + Paper:** muted olive plus brick-red accent
- `TASTE-066` **Pure monochrome + single saturated pop:** off-white + off-black + one bright accent (electric blue, emerald, hot pink, etc.)
- `TASTE-067` **Palette-rotation rule:** if the previous premium-consumer project you generated used the beige+brass family, this one MUST use a different family. Do not ship the same warm-craft palette twice in a row.
- `TASTE-068` **Override:** the beige+brass+espresso palette is acceptable ONLY when the brand brief explicitly names those colors, or when the brand identity is genuinely vintage / artisan / warm-craft AND you can articulate why this specific palette fits this specific brand. Default-reaching for it because "this is a cookware brief" is banned.
- `TASTE-069` **ANTI-CENTER BIAS:** Centered Hero / H1 sections are avoided when `DESIGN_VARIANCE > 4`. Force "Split Screen" (50/50), "Left-aligned content / right-aligned asset", "Asymmetric white-space", or scroll-pinned structures.
- `TASTE-070` **Override:** centered hero is OK for editorial / manifesto / launch-announcement briefs where the message itself is the design.
- `TASTE-071` Use cards ONLY when elevation communicates real hierarchy. Otherwise group with `border-t`, `divide-y`, or negative space.
- `TASTE-072` When a shadow is used, tint it to the background hue. No pure-black drop shadows on light backgrounds.
- `TASTE-073` For `VISUAL_DENSITY > 7`: generic card containers are banned. Data metrics breathe in plain layout.
- `TASTE-074` **SHAPE CONSISTENCY LOCK (mandatory):** Pick ONE corner-radius scale for the page and stick to it. Options: all-sharp (radius 0), all-soft (radius 12-16px), all-pill (full radius for interactive). Mixed systems are allowed only when there is a documented rule (e.g. "buttons are full-pill, cards are 16px, inputs are 8px") and that rule is followed everywhere. Round buttons in a square layout, or square cards on a pill-button page, is broken design.
- `TASTE-075` **Loading:** Skeletal loaders matching the final layout's shape. Avoid generic circular spinners.
- `TASTE-076` **Empty States:** Beautifully composed; indicate how to populate.
- `TASTE-077` **Error States:** Clear, inline (forms), or contextual (toasts only for transient).
- `TASTE-078` **Tactile Feedback:** On `:active`, use `-translate-y-[1px]` or `scale-[0.98]` to simulate a physical push.
- `TASTE-079` **BUTTON CONTRAST CHECK (mandatory, a11y):** Before shipping any button, verify the button text is readable against the button background. White button + white text, `bg-white` CTA with `text-white` label, transparent button against the page background with no border → all banned. Audit every CTA: contrast ratio WCAG AA min (4.5:1 for body, 3:1 for large text 18px+). Same rule applies to ghost buttons over photographic backgrounds (use a backdrop, scrim, or stroke). [D06]
- `TASTE-080` **CTA BUTTON WRAP BAN (mandatory):** Button text MUST fit on one line at desktop. If a label like "VIEW SELECTED WORK" wraps to 2 or 3 lines, the button is broken. Fix by EITHER shortening the label (3 words max for primary CTAs, ideally 1-2) OR widening the button (do not artificially constrain `max-width` on CTAs). Wrapped CTAs at desktop are a Pre-Flight Fail.
- `TASTE-081` **NO DUPLICATE CTA INTENT (mandatory):** Two CTAs with the same intent on one page is a Pre-Flight Fail. Examples of same intent: "Get in touch" + "Contact us" + "Let's talk" + "Start a project" + "Start something" + "Reach out" = all "contact" intent → pick ONE label and use it everywhere on the page (nav, hero, footer). Same for "Try free" + "Get started" + "Sign up free" (all "signup" intent) and "View work" + "See selected work" + "Browse projects" (all "portfolio" intent). One label per intent.
- `TASTE-082` **FORM CONTRAST CHECK (mandatory, a11y):** Form inputs, placeholder text, focus rings, helper text, and error text all pass WCAG AA contrast against the section background. Light placeholders on a near-white form, white form on white page section, form labels grayer than 4.5:1 contrast → all banned. Audit every form before shipping. [H02, D06]
- `TASTE-083` Label ABOVE input. Helper text optional but present in markup. Error text BELOW input. Standard `gap-2` for input blocks.
- `TASTE-084` No placeholder-as-label. Ever.
- `TASTE-085` **Hero MUST fit in the initial viewport.** Headline max 2 lines on desktop, subtext max **20 words** AND max 3-4 lines, CTAs visible without scroll. If the copy is too long: reduce font scale OR cut copy. If you cannot describe the value-prop in 20 words of subtext, the value-prop is unclear, not the rule too tight. Never let the hero overflow and force scroll to find the CTA.
- `TASTE-086` **Hero font-scale discipline.** Plan font size and image size *together*. If the hero asset is large and the headline is more than 6 words, do not start at `text-7xl/text-8xl`. Default sensible range: `text-4xl md:text-5xl lg:text-6xl` for most heroes; `text-6xl md:text-7xl` only when the headline is 3-5 words. A 4-line hero headline is always a font-size error, never a copy-length error.
- `TASTE-087` **HERO TOP PADDING CAP (mandatory):** Hero top padding max `pt-24` (≈6rem) at desktop. More than that means the hero content floats halfway down the viewport and reads as a layout bug, not as intentional space. If your hero needs more breathing room, increase font scale or asset size, not top padding.
- `TASTE-088` **HERO STACK DISCIPLINE (max 4 text elements).** The hero is a single moment, not a feature list. Allowed text elements, max 4 in total:
- `TASTE-089` Eyebrow (small uppercase label) OR brand strip OR neither - pick zero or one [D03]
- `TASTE-090` Headline (max 2 lines, see above)
- `TASTE-091` Subtext (max 20 words, max 4 lines)
- `TASTE-092` CTAs (1 primary + max 1 secondary)
- `TASTE-093` **BANNED in the hero:** tiny tagline below CTAs ("Works with GitHub, GitLab, and self-hosted Git"), trust micro-strip ("Used by engineering teams at..."), pricing teaser ("Free for solo, $10/user for teams"), feature bullet list, social-proof avatar row. All of those move to dedicated sections directly below the hero.
- `TASTE-094` If you have an eyebrow AND a tagline below CTAs in the same hero, drop the tagline. If you have a brand strip AND a tagline, drop the tagline. One small text element per hero, max. [D03]
- `TASTE-095` **"Used by" / "Trusted by" logo wall belongs UNDER the hero, never inside it.** The hero is for the value prop and primary CTA. The logo wall is a separate section directly below. Do not stuff trust logos into the same flex row as the hero copy.
- `TASTE-096` **Navigation MUST render on a single line on desktop.** If items don't fit at `lg` (1024px), condense labels, drop secondary items, or move to a hamburger. A two-line nav at desktop is broken design.
- `TASTE-097` **Navigation height cap: 80px max desktop, default 64-72px.** No huge "agency" nav bars that eat 15% of the viewport.
- `TASTE-098` **Bento grids MUST have rhythm, not one-sided repetition.** Do not stack 6 left-image / right-text rows. Vary the composition: alternate full-width feature rows, asymmetric tile sizes, vertical breaks.
- `TASTE-099` **BENTO CELL COUNT RULE (mandatory):** A bento grid has EXACTLY as many cells as you have content for. 3 items → 3 cells (1+2 split, or 2+1, or asymmetric trio). 5 items → 5 cells (2+3, 3+2, hero+4, etc.). If your grid has an empty cell in the middle or at the end, you planned wrong. Re-shape the grid; do not paste a blank tile.
- `TASTE-100` **Section-Layout-Repetition Ban.** Once you use a layout family for a section (e.g., 3-column-image-cards, full-width-quote, split-text-image), that family can appear at most ONCE on the page. "Selected commissions" must not look like "What we do." A landing page with 8 sections must use at least 4 different layout families.
- `TASTE-101` **ZIGZAG ALTERNATION CAP (mandatory).** Alternating "left-image + right-text" then "left-text + right-image" zigzag layout = banal. Max 2 sections in a row with this image+text-split pattern. The 3rd consecutive image+text split is a Pre-Flight Fail. Break the pattern with a full-width section, a vertical-stack section, a bento grid, a marquee, or a different layout family.
- `TASTE-102` **EYEBROW RESTRAINT (mandatory, the #1 violated rule in production tests).** An "eyebrow" is the small uppercase wide-tracking label sitting above a section headline (e.g. `FOUR COLORWAYS`, `SELECTED WORK`, `THE HARDWARE`, `Git-native task management`). Typical CSS signature: `text-[11px] uppercase tracking-[0.18em]`, `font-mono text-[10.5px] uppercase tracking-[0.22em]`. Every AI-built site puts an eyebrow above EVERY section header, producing the same templated rhythm. Hard rule: [D03]
- `TASTE-103` **Maximum 1 eyebrow per 3 sections.** Hero counts as 1. So a page with 9 sections may use at most 3 eyebrows total. [D03]
- `TASTE-104` If section A has an eyebrow, the next 2 sections cannot have one. [D03]
- `TASTE-105` **Pre-Flight Check is mechanical:** count instances of `uppercase tracking` (or similar small-caps mono labels above headlines) across all section components. If count > ceil(sectionCount / 3), the output fails. [D03]
- `TASTE-106` **What to do instead of an eyebrow:** drop it entirely. The headline alone is enough. If you need to categorize a section, the section's location on the page already categorizes it; no label needed. [D03]
- `TASTE-107` **SPLIT-HEADER BAN (mandatory).** The pattern "left big headline + right small explainer paragraph" as a section header (left col-span-7/8, right col-span-4/5 with a small body paragraph floating in the right column) is **banned as default**. Sections should have ONE focused message. If you genuinely need both a headline and an explainer paragraph, stack them vertically (headline on top, body below, max-width 65ch). Reach for the split-header pattern only when there is a real compositional reason (e.g., the right column carries a visual or interactive element, not just filler text).
- `TASTE-108` **Bento Background Diversity (mandatory).** Bento and feature-grid sections cannot be 6 white-on-white cards with text inside. At least 2-3 cells in any multi-cell grid need real visual variation: a real image, a brand-appropriate gradient (not AI-purple), a pattern, a tinted background. A cream-on-cream bento with only typography inside reads as boring AI default, even when the rest of the page is good.
- `TASTE-109` **Mobile collapse must be explicit per section.** For every multi-column layout, declare the `< 768px` fallback in the same component. No "it'll work, Tailwind handles it" assumptions.
- `TASTE-110` **Image-generation tool first.** If ANY image-gen tool is available in the environment (`generate_image`, MCP image tool, IDE-integrated gen, OpenAI image tools, etc.) you MUST use it to create section-specific assets: hero photography, product shots, texture backgrounds, mood images. Generate at the right aspect ratio for the section. Do not skip this step because hand-rolled CSS feels faster.
- `TASTE-111` **Real web images second.** When no gen tool is available, use real photography sources. Acceptable defaults:
- `TASTE-112` `https://picsum.photos/seed/{descriptive-seed}/{w}/{h}` for placeholder photography (seed should describe the section, e.g. `marrow-cookware-kitchen`)
- `TASTE-113` Actual stock or brand URLs when the brief provides them
