- `TASTE-114` Open-license sources (Unsplash via direct URL, Pexels) if explicitly allowed
- `TASTE-115` **Last resort: tell the user.** If neither is possible, do NOT fill the page with hand-rolled SVG illustrations or div-based "fake screenshots." Instead, leave clearly-labeled placeholder slots (`<!-- TODO: hero product photo, 1600x1200 -->`) and at the end of the response say: *"This page needs real images at: \[list of placements\]. Please generate or provide them."*
- `TASTE-116` **Source: Simple Icons** (`https://cdn.simpleicons.org/{slug}/ffffff` for any color, or `simple-icons` npm package). Covers most known brands.
- `TASTE-117` **Alternative: devicon** for tech-stack logos (`@svgr/cli` or CDN).
- `TASTE-118` **Make-up the brand name? Then make-up an SVG mark too.** Generate a simple monogram (one letter in a circle, two-letter ligature, abstract glyph) rendered as an inline `<svg>` matching the page style. Plain text wordmarks for invented brand names look generic.
- `TASTE-119` **Always** ensure logos render in both light and dark mode (white-on-dark, black-on-light, or single-color theme variable).
- `TASTE-120` **LOGO-ONLY rule (mandatory):** logo wall = logos and nothing else. Do NOT print industry / category labels below each logo (no `Vercel` + `hosting` underneath, no `Stripe` + `payments`, no `Cloudflare` + `infra`). The logo is the credibility, the label adds nothing the user does not already know. Optional: brand name as alt-text for screen readers, optional link to the brand's site. That is it.
- `TASTE-121` SVG icons from libraries: fine (see Section 3.C).
- `TASTE-122` Hand-rolled decorative SVGs (custom illustrations, logos, marks): **strongly discouraged**, never as default. Acceptable only when:
- `TASTE-123` The brief explicitly calls for it ("draw me an SVG logo")
- `TASTE-124` It's a single, simple geometric mark (a square, a circle, a wordmark in display type)
- `TASTE-125` You're confident in the output quality
- `TASTE-126` Use a real screenshot URL if one exists
- `TASTE-127` Generate one via image tool
- `TASTE-128` Use a real component preview (an actual mini-version of the UI inside the page)
- `TASTE-129` Or skip the preview entirely and use editorial photography
- `TASTE-130` **Default content shape per section:** short headline (≤ 8 words) + short sub-paragraph (≤ 25 words) + one visual asset OR one CTA. Anything more must be justified by the section's job.
- `TASTE-131` **No data-dump sections.** A 20-row publication table, a 30-row award list, a giant pricing matrix on a marketing page = wrong layout. Use:
- `TASTE-132` Top 3-5 highlights + "View full list" link
- `TASTE-133` Marquee / carousel for breadth
- `TASTE-134` Different page entirely if the data is the product
- `TASTE-135` **Long lists need a different UI component, not a longer list.** Default `<ul>` with bullets / `divide-y` rows is the lazy choice. If you have > 5 items, reach for one of these instead:
- `TASTE-136` 2-column split with grouped items
- `TASTE-137` Card grid with image + label per item
- `TASTE-138` Tabs / accordion if items are categorisable
- `TASTE-139` Horizontal scroll-snap pills
- `TASTE-140` Carousel for breadth-heavy lists (testimonials, logos, capabilities)
- `TASTE-141` Marquee for "lots-of-things-that-don't-need-individual-attention"
- `TASTE-142` **Spec sheets specifically (the Marrow-cookware pattern).** A long product specification table with `border-b` on every row is the AI default for cookware / hardware / apparel / artisan-goods briefs. Banned. Concrete alternatives:
- `TASTE-143` **2-col card grid:** each spec gets its own card with the spec name, the value (large display number), and a one-line "why it matters" body. Cards arranged 2-col on desktop, 1-col mobile.
- `TASTE-144` **Scroll-snap horizontal pills:** each spec is a pill, user can flick through.
- `TASTE-145` **Grouped chunks:** group 10 specs into 3 logical clusters (e.g. "Materials", "Cooking", "Warranty"), each cluster gets ONE soft divider and a cluster heading.
- `TASTE-146` **Featured-vs-rest:** 3-4 hero specs visualised as large display tiles, the rest collapsed under a "View full specifications" disclosure.
- `TASTE-147` **COPY SELF-AUDIT (mandatory before ship):** Before declaring any task done, re-read every visible string on the page (headlines, subheads, eyebrows, button labels, body copy, captions, alt text, footer text, error messages). Flag any string that is:
- `TASTE-148` **Grammatically broken** ("free on its past", "two plans but one is honest", "to put it on the table" out of context)
- `TASTE-149` **Has unclear referents** ("we plan to stay that way" without prior context)
- `TASTE-150` **Sounds like AI hallucination** (cute-but-wrong wordplay, forced metaphors that don't track, "elegant nothing" phrases)
- `TASTE-151` **Reads like an LLM trying to sound thoughtful** (passive-aggressive humility, fake-craftsman labels, mock-poetic micro-meta)
- `TASTE-152` **Fake-precise numbers are flagged.** Numbers like `92%`, `4.1×`, `48k`, `5.8 mm`, `13.4 lb` either:
- `TASTE-153` Come from real data (brief, brand guidelines, public metrics) - fine
- `TASTE-154` Are explicitly labeled as mock (`<!-- mock -->`, "example", "sample data") - fine
- `TASTE-155` Are AI-invented spec aesthetics - banned. Don't fake engineering precision the brand doesn't claim.
- `TASTE-156` **One copy register per page.** Don't mix technical mono ("47 tasks · 0.6 ctx-switches/day"), editorial prose, and marketing punch in the same composition unless the brand voice explicitly calls for it.
- `TASTE-157` **Max 3 lines** of quote body. Never 6. If the original quote is longer → cut it. A landing-page quote is a snippet, not the full review.
- `TASTE-158` For very small font sizes (e.g. footer-style testimonials), the line cap can stretch slightly. Spirit: "fits in a glance."
- `TASTE-159` **No em-dashes inside the quote text** as design flourish (long pauses, kinetic em-dashes, em-dash-bullets). See Section 9.G - em-dash is completely banned.
- `TASTE-160` Attribution: name + role + (optionally) company. Never name only ("- Sarah").
- `TASTE-161` Quote marks: use real typographic quotes ( " " ) or none at all. Not straight ASCII ( " ).
- `TASTE-162` If the page is dark mode, ALL sections are dark mode. No light-mode-warm-paper section sandwiched between dark sections (or vice versa). The user must not feel they walked into a different website mid-scroll.
- `TASTE-163` The exception: if the brief explicitly calls for a "Color Block Story" or "Theme Switch on Scroll" device AND that is a deliberate composition (one full theme switch with a strong transition, not random alternation), it is allowed once per page.
- `TASTE-164` Default behaviour: pick light, dark, or auto (`prefers-color-scheme`) at the page level and lock it. Section-level background tints within the same theme family are fine (`bg-zinc-950` next to `bg-zinc-900`); flipping to `bg-amber-50` in the middle of a `bg-zinc-950` page is broken.
- `TASTE-165` When using a design system with built-in theming (Radix Themes, shadcn/ui with `<Theme>`), set the theme ONCE in `layout.tsx` or the page root. Do not let individual sections override.
- `TASTE-166` **Liquid Glass / Glassmorphism:** Appropriate for premium consumer, Apple-adjacent, luxury brand, or media-overlay vibes. Inappropriate for dashboards, public-sector, or "boring B2B." When used, go beyond `backdrop-blur`: add a 1px inner border (`border-white/10`) and a subtle inner shadow (`shadow-[inset_0_1px_0_rgba(255,255,255,0.1)]`) for physical edge refraction. Provide a solid-fill fallback under `prefers-reduced-transparency`.
- `TASTE-167` **Magnetic Micro-physics:** Use when `MOTION_INTENSITY > 5` AND the brief reads premium / playful / agency. Implement EXCLUSIVELY with Motion's `useMotionValue` / `useTransform` outside the React render cycle. Never `useState`. See Section 3.B.
- `TASTE-168` **Perpetual Micro-Interactions** (Pulse, Typewriter, Float, Shimmer, Carousel): Use when `MOTION_INTENSITY > 5` AND the section actively benefits from motion (status indicators, live feeds, AI-feel). **Not every card needs an infinite loop.** If a section is informational, leave it still. Apply Spring Physics (`type: "spring", stiffness: 100, damping: 20`) - no linear easing.
- `TASTE-169` **"Motion claimed, motion shown."** If `MOTION_INTENSITY > 4`, the page must actually move: entry transitions on hero, scroll-reveal on key sections, hover physics on CTAs, at minimum. A static page that claims `MOTION_INTENSITY: 7` is broken. Conversely, if you cannot ship working motion in the available scope, drop the dial to 3 and ship a clean static page. Never half-build motion that breaks (cut-off ScrollTriggers, jumpy enters, missing cleanups).
- `TASTE-170` **MOTION MUST BE MOTIVATED (mandatory).** Before adding any animation, ask: "what does this animation communicate?" Valid answers: hierarchy (drawing attention to the right thing), storytelling (revealing content in sequence that matches a narrative), feedback (acknowledging a user action), state transition (showing something changed). Invalid answer: "it looked cool". GSAP everywhere because GSAP is available is amateur. Each ScrollTrigger, each marquee, each pinned section needs a reason. If you cannot articulate the reason in one sentence, drop the animation.
- `TASTE-171` **MARQUEE MAX-ONE-PER-PAGE (mandatory).** Horizontal scrolling text marquees ("logos endlessly scrolling", "manifesto scrolling sideways", "kinetic word strip") are appropriate at most ONCE per page. Two or more marquees on the same page reads as lazy filler. Pick the one section where the marquee actually serves the content; the others get a different layout.
- `TASTE-172` **GSAP Sticky-Stack Pattern (when scroll-stack is used).** A "card stack on scroll" must be a REAL sticky-stack, not a sequential reveal list. See Section 5.A below for the canonical code skeleton. Common failure: trigger fires halfway through scroll instead of pinning at viewport top. Fix: `start: "top top"` not `start: "top center"` or `"top 80%"`.
- `TASTE-173` **GSAP Horizontal-Pan Pattern (when horizontal scroll-hijack is used).** See Section 5.B below for the canonical skeleton. Common failure: animation starts before the section is pinned, so the user sees half a slide. Same fix: `start: "top top"`, pin the wrapper, scrub the inner track.
- `TASTE-174` **`window.addEventListener("scroll", ...)`** is banned. It runs on every scroll frame, jank-prone, no batching. Use Motion's `useScroll()`, GSAP's `ScrollTrigger`, IntersectionObserver, or CSS `scroll-driven animations` (`animation-timeline: view()`).
- `TASTE-175` **Custom scroll progress calculations using `window.scrollY`** in React state. Same reason. Re-renders on every frame.
- `TASTE-176` **`requestAnimationFrame` loops that touch React state.** Use motion values (`useMotionValue` + `useTransform`) instead.
- `TASTE-177` **Layout Transitions:** Use Motion's `layout` and `layoutId` props for visible state changes (re-ordering lists, expanding modals, shared elements between routes). Do not wrap static content in `layout` props "for safety" - it costs measurement work.
- `TASTE-178` **Staggered Orchestration:** Use `staggerChildren` (Motion) or CSS cascade (`animation-delay: calc(var(--index) * 100ms)`) for reveal moments where sequence matters. For `staggerChildren`, parent (`variants`) and children MUST share the same Client Component tree.
- `TASTE-179` Animate ONLY `transform` and `opacity`. Never animate `top`, `left`, `width`, `height`.
- `TASTE-180` Use `will-change: transform` sparingly - only on elements that will actually animate.
- `TASTE-181` **Any motion above `MOTION_INTENSITY > 3` MUST honor `prefers-reduced-motion`.** This is non-negotiable.
- `TASTE-182` In Motion: wrap with `useReducedMotion()` and degrade to static.
- `TASTE-183` In CSS: gate animations behind `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: no-preference)` or provide an override block under `@media (prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)` that disables.
- `TASTE-184` Infinite loops, parallax, scroll-hijack, and magnetic physics MUST collapse to static / instant under reduced motion.
- `TASTE-185` Design for **both modes from the start**. Never ship light-only or dark-only without explicit user instruction.
- `TASTE-186` Use Tailwind `dark:` variant OR CSS variables for tokens. Pick one strategy per project.
- `TASTE-187` **Do not prescribe specific dark-mode colors here.** The brief decides. Maintain visual hierarchy, brand identity, and WCAG AA contrast (AAA for body) across both modes.
- `TASTE-188` Respect `prefers-color-scheme: dark`. Default to system preference unless the brand insists on one mode.
- `TASTE-189` **LCP** < 2.5s. Hero image must be `next/image priority` or preloaded.
- `TASTE-190` **INP** < 200ms. Heavy work off main thread.
- `TASTE-191` **CLS** < 0.1. Reserve space for images, fonts, embeds.
- `TASTE-192` Run Lighthouse before declaring a page done.
- `TASTE-193` Apply grain / noise filters EXCLUSIVELY to fixed, `pointer-events-none` pseudo-elements (e.g., `fixed inset-0 z-[60] pointer-events-none`). NEVER on scrolling containers - continuous GPU repaints destroy mobile FPS.
- `TASTE-194` Be aware of bundle size. Motion is not tiny. Three.js is large. Lazy-load anything that's not above-the-fold.
- `TASTE-195` **1-3 (Predictable):** Symmetrical CSS Grid (12-col, equal fr-units), equal paddings, centered alignment.
- `TASTE-196` **4-7 (Offset):** `margin-top: -2rem` overlaps, varied image aspect ratios (4:3 next to 16:9), left-aligned headers over center-aligned data.
- `TASTE-197` **8-10 (Asymmetric):** Masonry layouts, CSS Grid with fractional units (`grid-template-columns: 2fr 1fr 1fr`), massive empty zones (`padding-left: 20vw`).
- `TASTE-198` **MOBILE OVERRIDE:** For levels 4-10, asymmetric layouts above `md:` MUST collapse to strict single-column (`w-full`, `px-4`, `py-8`) on viewports `< 768px`.
- `TASTE-199` **1-3 (Static):** No automatic animations. CSS `:hover` and `:active` states only. `prefers-reduced-motion` is the default mode anyway.
- `TASTE-200` **4-7 (Fluid CSS):** `transition: all 0.3s cubic-bezier(0.16, 1, 0.3, 1)`. `animation-delay` cascades for load-ins. Focus on `transform` and `opacity`.
- `TASTE-201` **8-10 (Advanced Choreography):** Complex scroll-triggered reveals, parallax, scroll-driven animation (CSS `animation-timeline` or GSAP ScrollTrigger). Use Motion hooks. **NEVER use `window.addEventListener('scroll')`** - it is a hard ban, not a "prefer-not." See Section 5.D for the allowed alternatives.
- `TASTE-202` **1-3 (Art Gallery):** Lots of white space. Huge section gaps (`py-32` to `py-48`). Expensive, clean.
- `TASTE-203` **4-7 (Daily App):** Standard web app spacing (`py-16` to `py-24`).
- `TASTE-204` **8-10 (Cockpit):** Tight paddings. No card boxes; 1px lines separate data. Mandatory: `font-mono` for all numbers.
- `TASTE-205` **Tailwind `dark:` variant** (default for utility-first projects): every color utility paired with its dark variant (`bg-white dark:bg-zinc-950`, `text-gray-900 dark:text-gray-100`).
- `TASTE-206` **CSS variables** (for shadcn/ui, Radix Themes, or component libraries with theming): define semantic tokens (`--surface`, `--surface-elevated`, `--text-primary`, `--accent`) and swap values under `[data-theme="dark"]` or `@media (prefers-color-scheme: dark)`.
- `TASTE-207` **Contrast** - WCAG AA minimum for body text, AAA target for hero copy.
- `TASTE-208` **Hierarchy parity** - visual hierarchy that works in light must work in dark. If a CTA pops in light, it pops in dark.
- `TASTE-209` **Brand fidelity** - primary brand color stays recognisable. Don't desaturate the brand into a dark mode.
- `TASTE-210` **No pure `#000000` and no pure `#ffffff`** - use off-black (zinc-950, near-black warm gray) and off-white. Pure values kill depth.
- `TASTE-211` **NO neon / outer glows** by default. Use inner borders or subtle tinted shadows.
- `TASTE-212` **NO pure black (`#000000`).** Off-black, zinc-950, or charcoal.
- `TASTE-213` **NO oversaturated accents.** Desaturate to blend with neutrals.
- `TASTE-214` **NO excessive gradient text** for large headers. [H06]
- `TASTE-215` **NO custom mouse cursors.** Outdated, accessibility-hostile, perf-hostile.
- `TASTE-216` **AVOID Inter as default.** See Section 4.1. Override path exists.
- `TASTE-217` **NO oversized H1s** that just scream. Control hierarchy with weight + color, not raw scale.
- `TASTE-218` **Serif constraints:** Serif for editorial / luxury / publication. Not for dashboards.
- `TASTE-219` **Mathematically perfect** padding and margins. No floating elements with awkward gaps.
- `TASTE-220` **NO 3-column equal feature cards.** The generic "three identical cards horizontally" feature row is banned. Use 2-column zig-zag, asymmetric grid, scroll-pinned, or horizontal-scroll alternative.
- `TASTE-221` **NO generic names.** "John Doe", "Sarah Chan", "Jack Su" → use creative, realistic, locale-appropriate names.
- `TASTE-222` **NO generic avatars.** No SVG "egg" or Lucide user icons → use believable photo placeholders or specific styling.
- `TASTE-223` **NO fake-perfect numbers.** Avoid `99.99%`, `50%`, `1234567`. Use organic, messy data (`47.2%`, `+1 (312) 847-1928`).
- `TASTE-224` **NO startup-slop brand names.** "Acme", "Nexus", "SmartFlow", "Cloudly" → invent contextual, premium names that sound real.
- `TASTE-225` **NO filler verbs.** "Elevate", "Seamless", "Unleash", "Next-Gen", "Revolutionize" → concrete verbs only.
- `TASTE-226` **NO hand-rolled SVG icons.** Use Phosphor / HugeIcons / Radix / Tabler. Lucide on explicit request only.
- `TASTE-227` **Hand-rolled decorative SVGs strongly discouraged** as default (see Section 4.8).
- `TASTE-228` **NO div-based fake screenshots.** Never build a fake product UI out of `<div>` rectangles to simulate a screenshot. Use real images, generated images, or skip the preview.
- `TASTE-229` **NO broken Unsplash links.** Use `https://picsum.photos/seed/{descriptive-string}/{w}/{h}`, or generated photo placeholders, or actual assets.
- `TASTE-230` **shadcn/ui customization:** Allowed, but NEVER in default state. Customize radii, colors, shadows, typography to the project aesthetic.
- `TASTE-231` **Production-Ready Cleanliness:** Code visually clean, memorable, meticulously refined.
- `TASTE-232` **NO version labels in the hero.** `V0.6`, `v2.0`, `BETA`, `INVITE-ONLY PREVIEW`, `EARLY ACCESS`, `ALPHA` - banned as default eyebrows. Only acceptable when the brief is explicitly about a product launch / preview status. [D03]
- `TASTE-233` **NO "Brand · No. 01"-style sub-eyebrows.** "Marrow · No. 01 · The 6-quart" type micro-meta lines. Skip them. [D03]
- `TASTE-234` **NO section-number eyebrows.** `00 / INDEX`, `001 · Capabilities`, `002 · Featured commission`, `06 · how it works`, `05 · The honest table` - banned. Eyebrows should name the topic in plain language, not enumerate. [D03]
- `TASTE-235` **NO `01 / 4`-style pagination on images or bento tiles.** If the user can count, they don't need the label.
- `TASTE-236` **NO `Scroll · 001 Capabilities`-style scroll cues.** A simple arrow or "Scroll" is enough; no section-number prefix.
- `TASTE-237` **NO "Index of Work, 2018 - 2026"-style range labels** as eyebrows. Just say what the section is. [D03]
- `TASTE-238` **The middle-dot (`·`) is rationed.** Maximum 1 per line in metadata strips. Do NOT use it as the default separator for everything ("foo · bar · baz · qux · quux"). If you need a separator family, prefer line breaks, hairlines, or columns.
- `TASTE-239` **NO decorative colored status dots on every list/nav/badge.** A colored dot before "ONE Q4 SLOT OPEN" or before every nav link, or every task row - banned by default. Acceptable only when the dot conveys actual semantic state (a server status, an availability flag) and is used sparingly.
- `TASTE-240` **NO em-dash (`—`) as a design element OR anywhere else.** See Section 9.G below for the complete, non-negotiable ban. The em-dash character is forbidden in headlines, eyebrows, pills, body copy, quotes, attribution, captions, button text, and alt text. Use the regular hyphen (`-`).
- `TASTE-241` **NO `<br>`-broken-and-italicized headlines** as a default "design move." "for thirty\<br\>*years.*" type splits. Headlines should read naturally first, get clever only when the brief demands it.
- `TASTE-242` **NO vertical rotated text** ("INDEX OF WORK, 2018 - 2026" rotated 90°). Agency-portfolio cliché. Use it only when the brief is explicitly agency / Awwwards / experimental AND it serves a real composition purpose.
- `TASTE-243` **NO crosshair / hairline grid lines as decoration.** Vertical and horizontal lines drawn just to make the page "feel designed" - banned. Use them only when they organize real content.
- `TASTE-244` **NO div-based fake product UI in the hero** (fake task list, fake terminal, fake dashboard built from styled divs). It is the #1 LLM-design Tell. Use a real screenshot, a generated image, a real component preview, or none at all.
- `TASTE-245` **NO fake version footers** ("v0.6.2-rc.1", "last sync 4s ago · main") inside fake screenshots. Adds nothing, screams AI.
- `TASTE-246` **NO "Quietly in use at" / "Quietly trusted by"** social-proof headers. Use natural language: "Trusted by", "Used at", "Customers include", or skip the heading entirely if the logos speak.
- `TASTE-247` **NO "From the field" / "Field notes" / "Currently on the bench" / "On our desks" / "Loose plates" style poetic labels** on quote, blog, or sidebar sections. Reads as performative-craftsman. Use plain functional labels ("Testimonials", "Latest writing", "Now working on") or skip the label.
- `TASTE-248` **NO "We respect the French ones"-style** mock-humble industry-references in body copy. Cute and AI-y.
- `TASTE-249` **NO weather / locale strips** ("LIS 14:23 · 18°C") in headers/footers unless the brief is explicitly about a place / time-zone-distributed studio.
- `TASTE-250` **NO micro-meta-sentences under eyebrows.** Sentences like *"Each of these is a feature we ship today, not a roadmap promise. The list will stay short on purpose."* sitting under a section heading are clutter. Eyebrow + Headline + Body is enough. [D03]
- `TASTE-251` **NO generic step labels.** "Stage 1 / Stage 2 / Stage 3", "Step 1 / Step 2 / Step 3", "Phase 01 / Phase 02 / Phase 03", "Pass One / Pass Two / Pass Three". Banned. The actual step content is the label. If you must show progression, use the verb-noun directly ("Install", "Configure", "Ship") not "Stage 1: Install".
- `TASTE-252` **NO pills/labels/tags overlaid on images.** No `<span>` overlays on photos with tags like `Brand · 02`, `PLATE · BRAND`, `Field notes - journal`. Either let the image speak alone, or add a caption directly below (outside the image).
- `TASTE-253` **NO photo-credit captions as decoration.** Strings like `Field study no. 12 · Ines Caetano`, `Plate 03 · House archive`, `Frame XII · 35mm` under stock/picsum images are pretentious. Photo credit is allowed ONLY when there is a real photographer being credited for a real photo (with permission). Otherwise: skip the caption or use a one-line functional caption ("The 6-quart, in Sage.").
- `TASTE-254` **NO version footers on marketing pages.** Footer strings like `v1.4.2`, `Build 0048`, `last sync 4s ago · main` are CLI / devtool fixtures, not landing-page content. Banned on marketing/landing/portfolio pages.
- `TASTE-255` **NO "Reservation 412 of 800"-style live-stock counters** as decoration. Only if the brief is explicitly a limited-run waitlist with real data.
- `TASTE-256` **NO decoration text strip at hero bottom.** Patterns like `BRAND. MOTION. SPATIAL.`, `TYPE / FORM / MOTION`, `DESIGN · BUILD · SHIP`, `ESTD. 2018 · LISBON · BRAND. MOTION. SPATIAL.` as a small mono-caps strip across the bottom of the hero are an agency-portfolio cliché. Banned by default. Only acceptable when the strip carries real, navigable links (sticky bottom nav) or real status info (cookie banner, build info on a docs site).
- `TASTE-257` **NO floating top-right sub-text in section headings.** Pattern: section has a giant left-aligned headline; in the top-right corner of the same section header there is a small explainer paragraph floating with no clear alignment to anything else. That floater is the Tell. Either put the sub-text directly under the headline, or build a clean 2-column header (left: headline, right: aligned body), but not a tiny corner paragraph.
- `TASTE-258` **NO `border-t` + `border-b` on every row of a long list / spec table.** Pick one (bottom-border between rows OR top-border above the group) and use it sparsely. A 10-row spec table with hairlines under each row is the laziest layout - see Section 4.9 for alternative UI components.
- `TASTE-259` **NO scoring/progress bars with filled background tracks** as comparison visuals. If you need to show "X out of Y" comparisons, prefer a number + small icon, or a tiny inline bar WITHOUT a background track. Big filled `bg-zinc-200` tracks with a partial fill on top are dashboard-UI clutter on a landing page.
- `TASTE-260` **Locale / city-name / time / weather strips are banned for 99% of briefs.** "Lisbon, working with founders" in the hero, "1200-690 Lisbon, Portugal" in the footer, "Lisbon 14:23 · 18°C" in the nav. These are agency-portfolio decoration tells. Allowed ONLY when: the brief explicitly describes a globally-distributed studio with timezone-relevant work, OR a travel-focused brand, OR a real-world physical venue. A single contact-address mention in the footer is fine; an atmospheric locale strip is not.
- `TASTE-261` **Scroll cues are banned.** `Scroll`, `↓ scroll`, `Scroll to explore`, `Scroll to walk through it`, animated mouse-wheel icons. If the user has not scrolled yet, they are looking at the hero. They know what scroll is. The bottom of the viewport does not need a label.
- `TASTE-262` **ZERO decorative status dots by default.** A coloured dot before nav items, before list rows, before badges, before status labels is a Tell. Only acceptable when conveying real semantic state (a live indicator on actual server status, a live availability flag) and limited to one per page section.
- `TASTE-263` **Banned in headlines.** Use a period or a comma.
- `TASTE-264` **Banned in eyebrows / labels / pills / button text / image captions / nav items.** Replace with line breaks, columns, or hairlines. [D03]
- `TASTE-265` **Banned in body copy.** Restructure the sentence: two sentences with a period, OR a comma, OR parentheses, OR a colon.
- `TASTE-266` **Banned in quote attribution.** Use a normal hyphen with spaces (` - `) or a line break + smaller-weight name.
- `TASTE-267` **Banned in en-dash form too (`–`) when used as a separator.** Date ranges (`2018-2026`) use a hyphen. Number ranges (`€40-80k`) use a hyphen.
- `TASTE-268` Regular hyphen `-` (for compound words, ranges, line dividers in markup)
- `TASTE-269` Minus sign in math (`-5°C`)
- `TASTE-270` **Asymmetric Split Hero** - Text on one side, asset on the other, generous white space.
- `TASTE-271` **Editorial Manifesto Hero** - Large type, no asset, almost-poster.
- `TASTE-272` **Video / Media Mask Hero** - Type cut out as mask over video background.
- `TASTE-273` **Kinetic-Type Hero** - Animated typography as the primary visual.
- `TASTE-274` **Curtain-Reveal Hero** - Hero parts on scroll like a curtain.
- `TASTE-275` **Scroll-Pinned Hero** - Hero stays pinned while content scrolls behind.
- `TASTE-276` **Mac OS Dock Magnification** - Edge nav, icons scale fluidly on hover.
- `TASTE-277` **Magnetic Button** - Pulls toward cursor.
- `TASTE-278` **Gooey Menu** - Sub-items detach like viscous liquid.
- `TASTE-279` **Dynamic Island** - Morphing pill for status / alerts.
- `TASTE-280` **Contextual Radial Menu** - Circular menu expanding at click point.
- `TASTE-281` **Floating Speed Dial** - FAB springing into curved secondary actions.
- `TASTE-282` **Mega Menu Reveal** - Full-screen dropdown, stagger-fade content.
- `TASTE-283` **Bento Grid** - Asymmetric tile grouping (Apple Control Center).
- `TASTE-284` **Masonry Layout** - Staggered grid, no fixed row height.
- `TASTE-285` **Chroma Grid** - Borders / tiles with subtle animating gradients.
- `TASTE-286` **Split-Screen Scroll** - Two halves sliding in opposite directions.
- `TASTE-287` **Sticky-Stack Sections** - Sections that pin and stack on scroll.
- `TASTE-288` **Parallax Tilt Card** - 3D tilt tracking mouse coordinates.
- `TASTE-289` **Spotlight Border Card** - Borders illuminate under cursor.
- `TASTE-290` **Glassmorphism Panel** - Frosted glass with inner refraction.
- `TASTE-291` **Holographic Foil Card** - Iridescent rainbow shift on hover.
- `TASTE-292` **Tinder Swipe Stack** - Physical card stack, swipe-away.
- `TASTE-293` **Morphing Modal** - Button expands into its own dialog.
- `TASTE-294` **Sticky Scroll Stack** - Cards stick and physically stack.
- `TASTE-295` **Horizontal Scroll Hijack** - Vertical scroll → horizontal pan.
- `TASTE-296` **Locomotive / Sequence Scroll** - Video / 3D sequence tied to scrollbar.
- `TASTE-297` **Zoom Parallax** - Central background image zooming on scroll.
- `TASTE-298` **Scroll Progress Path** - SVG line drawing along scroll.
- `TASTE-299` **Liquid Swipe Transition** - Page transition like viscous liquid.
- `TASTE-300` **Dome Gallery** - 3D panoramic gallery.
- `TASTE-301` **Coverflow Carousel** - 3D carousel with angled edges.
- `TASTE-302` **Drag-to-Pan Grid** - Boundless draggable canvas.
- `TASTE-303` **Accordion Image Slider** - Narrow strips expanding on hover.
- `TASTE-304` **Hover Image Trail** - Mouse leaves popping image trail.
- `TASTE-305` **Glitch Effect Image** - RGB-channel shift on hover.
- `TASTE-306` **Kinetic Marquee** - Endless text bands reversing on scroll.
- `TASTE-307` **Text Mask Reveal** - Massive type as transparent window to video.
- `TASTE-308` **Text Scramble Effect** - Matrix-style decoding on load / hover.
- `TASTE-309` **Circular Text Path** - Text curving along spinning circle.
- `TASTE-310` **Gradient Stroke Animation** - Outlined text with running gradient.
- `TASTE-311` **Kinetic Typography Grid** - Letters dodging the cursor.
- `TASTE-312` **Particle Explosion Button** - CTA shatters into particles on success.
- `TASTE-313` **Liquid Pull-to-Refresh** - Reload indicator like detaching droplets.
- `TASTE-314` **Skeleton Shimmer** - Shifting light reflection across placeholders.
- `TASTE-315` **Directional Hover-Aware Button** - Fill enters from cursor's exact side.
- `TASTE-316` **Ripple Click Effect** - Wave from click coordinates.
- `TASTE-317` **Animated SVG Line Drawing** - Vectors drawing themselves in real time.
- `TASTE-318` **Mesh Gradient Background** - Organic lava-lamp blobs.
- `TASTE-319` **Lens Blur Depth** - Background UI blurred to focus foreground action.
- `TASTE-320` **Motion (`motion/react`)** - default for UI / Bento / state-change motion.
- `TASTE-321` **GSAP + ScrollTrigger** - for full-page scrolltelling and scroll hijacks. Isolate in dedicated leaf components with `useEffect` cleanup.
- `TASTE-322` **Three.js / WebGL** - for canvas backgrounds and 3D scenes. Same isolation rule.
- `TASTE-323` **NEVER mix GSAP / Three.js with Motion in the same component tree.** They fight over the same frames.
- `TASTE-324` **Greenfield** - no existing site, or full overhaul approved. Dial baseline from Section 1.
- `TASTE-325` **Redesign - Preserve** - modernise without breaking the brand. Audit first, extract brand tokens, evolve gradually.
- `TASTE-326` **Redesign - Overhaul** - new visual language on top of existing content. Treat as greenfield for visuals; preserve content and IA.
- `TASTE-327` **Brand tokens** - primary / accent colors, type stack, logo treatment, radii.
- `TASTE-328` **Information architecture** - page tree, primary nav, key conversion paths.
- `TASTE-329` **Content blocks** - what exists, what's doing work, what's filler.
- `TASTE-330` **Patterns to preserve** - signature interactions, recognisable hero, copy voice.
- `TASTE-331` **Patterns to retire** - AI-slop tells, broken layouts, dead links, generic stock imagery, perf traps.
- `TASTE-332` **Dial reading of the existing site** - infer current `DESIGN_VARIANCE` / `MOTION_INTENSITY` / `VISUAL_DENSITY`. That's your starting point, not the baseline.
- `TASTE-333` **SEO baseline** - current ranking pages, meta titles, structured data, OG cards. **SEO migration is the #1 redesign risk.**
- `TASTE-334` **Do not change information architecture** unless asked. Keep page slugs, anchor IDs, primary nav labels stable for SEO and muscle memory.
- `TASTE-335` **Extract brand colors before applying Section 4.2.** A brand that is already purple stays purple - apply the LILA RULE's override.
- `TASTE-336` **Preserve copy voice** unless asked for a rewrite. Visual modernisation ≠ content rewrite.
- `TASTE-337` **Honor existing accessibility wins.** Do not regress focus states, alt text, keyboard nav, contrast. [H02, H04]
- `TASTE-338` **Respect existing analytics events.** Do not rename buttons, form fields, section IDs that downstream tracking depends on.
- `TASTE-339` **Typography refresh** - biggest visual lift per unit of risk.
- `TASTE-340` **Spacing & rhythm** - increase section padding, fix vertical rhythm.
- `TASTE-341` **Color recalibration** - desaturate, unify neutrals, keep brand accent.
- `TASTE-342` **Motion layer** - add `MOTION_INTENSITY`-appropriate micro-interactions to existing components.
- `TASTE-343` **Hero & key-section recomposition** - restructure top-of-funnel using Section 10 vocabulary.
- `TASTE-344` **Full block replacement** - only when the existing block is unsalvageable.
- `TASTE-345` IA, content, and SEO sound → **targeted evolution** (Levers 1-4). ~70% of value at ~40% of risk.
- `TASTE-346` Visual debt is structural (broken IA, no design system, broken mobile) → **full redesign** with strict content preservation.
- `TASTE-347` Brand itself is changing → **greenfield**.
- `TASTE-348` URL structure / route slugs.
- `TASTE-349` Primary nav labels.
- `TASTE-350` Form field names or order (breaks analytics + autofill).
- `TASTE-351` Brand logo or wordmark.
- `TASTE-352` Existing legal / consent / cookie copy.
- `TASTE-353` **Visual sketch** - short ASCII or description of the layout.
- `TASTE-354` **Props API** - the component's interface.
- `TASTE-355` **Code sketch** - minimal working implementation (Server Component default, Client island for motion).
- `TASTE-356` **Mobile fallback** - explicit collapse rules for `< 768px`.
- `TASTE-357` **Motion variants** - one variant per `MOTION_INTENSITY` band (1-3, 4-7, 8-10). Reduced-motion fallback explicit.
- `TASTE-358` **Dark-mode notes** - token strategy specific to this block.
- `TASTE-359` **Anti-patterns** - common ways this block goes wrong.
- `TASTE-360` **References** - links to real examples in production.
- `TASTE-361` One block per file. No multi-block files.
- `TASTE-362` Every block must work standalone (drop it into a page, it renders).
- `TASTE-363` Every block must pass the Pre-Flight Check (Section 14).
- `TASTE-364` Blocks that depend on a design system from Section 2.A live under `blocks/<category>/<name>--<system>.md` (e.g. `feature/bento-grid--material.md`).
- `TASTE-365` Dashboards / dense product UI / admin panels (use Fluent, Carbon, Atlassian, or Polaris from Section 2.A).
- `TASTE-366` Data tables (use TanStack Table or AG Grid).
- `TASTE-367` Multi-step forms / wizards (use Form-specific patterns; this skill won't make them better).
- `TASTE-368` Code editors (use Monaco / CodeMirror with their official skinning).
- `TASTE-369` Native mobile (use Apple HIG / Material directly).
- `TASTE-370` Realtime collab UIs (presence, cursors, OT-aware - different problem class).
- `TASTE-371` [ ] **Brief inference** declared (Section 0.B one-liner)?
- `TASTE-372` [ ] **Dial values** explicit and reasoned from the brief, not silently using baseline?
- `TASTE-373` [ ] **Design system** chosen from Section 2 if applicable, or aesthetic labeled honestly?
- `TASTE-374` [ ] **Redesign mode** detected and audit performed (if applicable, Section 11)?
- `TASTE-375` [ ] **ZERO em-dashes (`—`) anywhere on the page.** Headlines, eyebrows, pills, body, quotes, attribution, captions, buttons, alt text. Zero. (Section 9.G - non-negotiable.)
- `TASTE-376` [ ] **Page Theme Lock**: ONE theme (light, dark, or auto) for the whole page. No section flips to inverted mode mid-page (Section 4.11)?
- `TASTE-377` [ ] **Color Consistency Lock**: one accent color used identically across all sections (Section 4.2)? [D05]
- `TASTE-378` [ ] **Shape Consistency Lock**: one corner-radius system applied consistently (Section 4.4)?
- `TASTE-379` [ ] **Button Contrast Check**: every CTA text is readable against its background (no white-on-white, WCAG AA 4.5:1)? [D06]
- `TASTE-380` [ ] **CTA Button Wrap**: no CTA label wraps to 2+ lines at desktop?
- `TASTE-381` [ ] **Form Contrast Check**: form inputs, placeholders, focus rings, labels all pass WCAG AA against the section background? [H02]
- `TASTE-382` [ ] **Serif discipline**: if a serif is used, it is NOT Fraunces or Instrument_Serif (or it is, with explicit brand justification)? Different serif from your previous project?
- `TASTE-383` [ ] **Premium-consumer palette check**: if the brief is premium-consumer (cookware / wellness / artisan / luxury), the palette is NOT the AI-default beige+brass+oxblood+espresso family? Different family from your previous premium-consumer project?
- `TASTE-384` [ ] **Italic descender clearance**: every italic word with `y g j p q` has `leading-[1.1]` min + `pb-1` reserve?
- `TASTE-385` [ ] **Hero fits the viewport**: headline ≤ 2 lines, subtext ≤ 20 words AND ≤ 4 lines, CTA visible without scroll, font scale planned around image?
- `TASTE-386` [ ] **Hero top padding**: max `pt-24` at desktop, hero content does not float halfway down the viewport?
- `TASTE-387` [ ] **Hero stack discipline**: max 4 text elements in hero (eyebrow OR brand strip, headline, subtext, CTAs)? No tiny tagline below CTAs, no trust micro-strip in hero? [D03]
- `TASTE-388` [ ] **EYEBROW COUNT (mechanical)**: count instances of `uppercase tracking` micro-labels above section headlines across all components. Count ≤ ceil(sectionCount / 3)? Hero counts as 1.
- `TASTE-389` [ ] **Split-Header Ban**: no "left big headline + right small explainer paragraph" pattern as a section header (vertical stack instead)?
- `TASTE-390` [ ] **Zigzag Alternation Cap**: no 3+ consecutive sections with the same image+text-split layout?
- `TASTE-391` [ ] **No Duplicate CTA Intent**: no two CTAs with the same intent ("Get in touch" + "Let's talk" both on page = Fail)?
- `TASTE-392` [ ] **Logo wall = logo only**: no industry / category labels printed below logos?
- `TASTE-393` [ ] **Bento Background Diversity**: at least 2-3 bento cells have real visual variation (image, gradient, pattern), not all white-on-white text cards?
- `TASTE-394` [ ] **"Used by / Trusted by" logo wall** lives UNDER the hero, not inside it, uses REAL SVG logos (Simple Icons / devicon) or generated SVG marks, NOT plain text wordmarks?
- `TASTE-395` [ ] **Copy Self-Audit**: every visible string re-read, no grammatically-broken or AI-hallucinated phrases ("free on its past" type) shipped?
- `TASTE-396` [ ] **Motion motivated**: every animation can be justified in one sentence (hierarchy / storytelling / feedback / state transition), no GSAP-for-show?
- `TASTE-397` [ ] **Marquee max-one-per-page**: no two horizontal marquees on the same page?
- `TASTE-398` [ ] **Navigation on ONE line** at desktop, height ≤ 80px?
- `TASTE-399` [ ] **Section-Layout-Repetition** check: no two sections share the same layout family (at least 4 different families across 8 sections)?
- `TASTE-400` [ ] **Bento has rhythm AND exact cell count** (N items → N cells, no empty cells in middle or at end)?
- `TASTE-401` [ ] **Long lists use the right UI component** (not default `<ul>` with `divide-y` for > 5 items - see Section 4.9 alternatives)?
- `TASTE-402` [ ] **Real images used** (gen-tool first, then Picsum-seed, then explicit placeholder slots) - NO div-based fake screenshots, NO hand-rolled decorative SVGs, NO pure-text minimalism?
- `TASTE-403` [ ] **No pills/labels overlaid on images** (no `Plate · Brand`, no `Field notes - journal`)?
- `TASTE-404` [ ] **No photo-credit captions as decoration** (`Field study no. 12 · Ines Caetano`)?
- `TASTE-405` [ ] **No version footers** (`v1.4.2`, `Build 0048`) on marketing pages?
- `TASTE-406` [ ] **No micro-meta-sentences** under eyebrows ("Each of these is a feature we ship today...")? [D03]
- `TASTE-407` [ ] **No decoration text strip at hero bottom** (`BRAND. MOTION. SPATIAL.`)?
- `TASTE-408` [ ] **No floating top-right sub-text** in section headings?
- `TASTE-409` [ ] **No scoring/progress bars with filled background tracks** as comparison visuals?
- `TASTE-410` [ ] **No locale / city-name / time / weather strips** unless brief is genuinely globally-distributed or place-focused?
- `TASTE-411` [ ] **No scroll cues** (`Scroll`, `↓ scroll`, `Scroll to explore`)?
- `TASTE-412` [ ] **No version labels in hero** (V0.6, BETA, INVITE-ONLY) unless the brief is a launch?
- `TASTE-413` [ ] **No section-numbering eyebrows** (`00 / INDEX`, `001 · Capabilities`, `06 · how it works`)? [D03]
- `TASTE-414` [ ] **No decorative dots** (zero by default, only for real semantic state)?
- `TASTE-415` [ ] **No `border-t` + `border-b` on every row** of long lists / spec tables?
- `TASTE-416` [ ] **Content density** sane: no 20-row data tables, no fake-precise specs without justification, ≤ 25-word sub-paragraphs by default?
- `TASTE-417` [ ] **Quotes ≤ 3 lines** of body, attribution clean (no em-dash)?
- `TASTE-418` [ ] **Motion claimed = motion shown**: if `MOTION_INTENSITY > 4`, page actually animates, not just claimed?
- `TASTE-419` [ ] **GSAP sticky-stack / horizontal-pan** implemented per Section 5.A / 5.B canonical skeleton (`start: "top top"`, `pin: true`, correct scrub)?
- `TASTE-420` [ ] **No `window.addEventListener('scroll')`** - using Motion `useScroll()` / ScrollTrigger / IntersectionObserver / CSS scroll-driven animations only?
- `TASTE-421` [ ] **Reduced motion** wrapped for everything `MOTION_INTENSITY > 3`?
- `TASTE-422` [ ] **Dark mode** tokens defined and tested in both modes?
- `TASTE-423` [ ] **Mobile collapse** explicit (`w-full`, `px-4`, `max-w-7xl mx-auto`) for high-variance layouts?
- `TASTE-424` [ ] **Viewport stability**: `min-h-[100dvh]`, never `h-screen`?
- `TASTE-425` [ ] **`useEffect` animations** have strict cleanup functions?
- `TASTE-426` [ ] **Empty / loading / error** states provided?
- `TASTE-427` [ ] **Cards omitted** in favor of spacing where possible?
- `TASTE-428` [ ] **Icons** from an allowed library only (Phosphor / HugeIcons / Radix / Tabler), no hand-rolled SVG paths?
- `TASTE-429` [ ] **Motion** isolated in client-leaf components with `'use client'` at the top, memoized?
- `TASTE-430` [ ] **No AI Tells** from Section 9 (Inter as default, AI-purple, three-equal cards, Jane Doe, Acme, "Quietly in use at")?
- `TASTE-431` [ ] **Core Web Vitals** plausibly hit (LCP < 2.5s, INP < 200ms, CLS < 0.1)?
- `TASTE-432` [ ] **One design system** per project (no Material + shadcn mixed)?

### ui-ux-pro-max

- `UUX-001` [ ] Ran only searches relevant to the interface, such as `"keyboard focus modal" --domain ux` for modal keyboard behavior
- `UUX-002` [ ] Reviewed `quick-reference.md` §1–§3 (CRITICAL + HIGH) as a final pass
- `UUX-003` [ ] Tested on 375px (small phone) and in landscape orientation
- `UUX-004` [ ] Verified behavior with **reduced-motion** enabled and **Dynamic Type**/largest system text size
- `UUX-005` [ ] Checked dark mode contrast independently (never assume light-mode values carry over)
- `UUX-006` [ ] Confirmed all touch targets ≥44pt and no content hidden behind safe areas
- `UUX-007` [ ] No emojis used as icons (use SVG instead) [D04, H08]
- `UUX-008` [ ] All icons come from a consistent icon family and style
- `UUX-009` [ ] Official brand assets are used with correct proportions and clear space
- `UUX-010` [ ] Pressed-state visuals do not shift layout bounds or cause jitter
- `UUX-011` [ ] Semantic theme tokens are used consistently (no ad-hoc per-screen hardcoded colors)
- `UUX-012` [ ] All tappable elements provide clear pressed feedback (ripple/opacity/elevation)
- `UUX-013` [ ] Touch targets meet minimum size (>=44x44pt iOS, >=48x48dp Android)
- `UUX-014` [ ] Micro-interaction timing uses shared, platform-appropriate tokens and remains responsive in context
- `UUX-015` [ ] Disabled states are visually clear and non-interactive
- `UUX-016` [ ] Screen reader focus order matches visual order, and interactive labels are descriptive
- `UUX-017` [ ] Gesture regions avoid nested/conflicting interactions (tap/drag/back-swipe conflicts)
- `UUX-018` [ ] Primary text contrast >=4.5:1 in both light and dark mode [D06]
- `UUX-019` [ ] Normal primary and secondary text contrast >=4.5:1 in both light and dark mode [D06]
- `UUX-020` [ ] Dividers/borders and interaction states are distinguishable in both modes
- `UUX-021` [ ] Modal/drawer scrim is measured against the real background and preserves foreground legibility
- `UUX-022` [ ] Both themes are tested before delivery (not inferred from a single theme)
- `UUX-023` [ ] Safe areas are respected for headers, tab bars, and bottom CTA bars
- `UUX-024` [ ] Scroll content is not hidden behind fixed/sticky bars
- `UUX-025` [ ] Verified on small phone, large phone, and tablet (portrait + landscape)
- `UUX-026` [ ] Horizontal insets/gutters adapt correctly by device size and orientation
- `UUX-027` [ ] 4/8dp spacing rhythm is maintained across component, section, and page levels
- `UUX-028` [ ] Long-form text measure remains readable on larger devices (no edge-to-edge paragraphs)
- `UUX-029` [ ] Decorative icons beside visible text are hidden from the accessibility tree (`aria-hidden="true"` on web or the native equivalent)
- `UUX-030` [ ] Meaningful images/icons without equivalent visible text have a text alternative
- `UUX-031` [ ] Icon controls have an accessible name and announce applicable selected/pressed/expanded state
- `UUX-032` [ ] Form fields have labels, hints, and clear error messages
- `UUX-033` [ ] Color is not the only indicator
- `UUX-034` [ ] Reduced motion and dynamic text size are supported without layout breakage
- `UUX-035` [ ] Sticky UI and overlays do not obscure keyboard focus
- `UUX-036` [ ] Dragging and swipe-only interactions have button/keyboard alternatives
- `UUX-037` [ ] Authentication allows password managers and paste, with a non-cognitive alternative
- `UUX-038` [ ] Auto-rotating content has pause/stop controls and stops on focus or reduced motion
- `UUX-039` [ ] Failed forms retain inline field errors; multi-error forms also focus a linked error summary after submit
- `UUX-040` `color-contrast` - Minimum 4.5:1 ratio for normal text (large text 3:1); Material Design [D06]
- `UUX-041` `focus-states` - Visible focus rings on interactive elements (2–4px; Apple HIG, MD) [H02]
- `UUX-042` `alt-text` - Descriptive alt text for meaningful images [H04]
- `UUX-043` `aria-labels` - aria-label for icon-only buttons; accessibilityLabel in native (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-044` `icon-context` - Semantics depend on use: decorative icons beside visible text are hidden from the accessibility tree; meaningful icons need a text alternative; icon controls need an accessible name and applicable state
- `UUX-045` `keyboard-nav` - Tab order matches visual order; full keyboard support (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-046` `form-labels` - Use label with for attribute
- `UUX-047` `skip-links` - Skip to main content for keyboard users
- `UUX-048` `heading-hierarchy` - Sequential h1→h6, no level skip
- `UUX-049` `color-not-only` - Don't convey info by color alone (add icon/text)
- `UUX-050` `dynamic-type` - Support system text scaling; avoid truncation as text grows (Apple Dynamic Type, MD)
- `UUX-051` `reduced-motion` - Respect prefers-reduced-motion; reduce/disable animations when requested (Apple Reduced Motion API, MD)
- `UUX-052` `voiceover-sr` - Meaningful accessibilityLabel/accessibilityHint; logical reading order for VoiceOver/screen readers (Apple HIG, MD)
- `UUX-053` `escape-routes` - Provide cancel/back in modals and multi-step flows (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-054` `keyboard-shortcuts` - Preserve system and a11y shortcuts; offer keyboard alternatives for drag-and-drop (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-055` `focus-not-obscured` - Sticky UI, overlays, and banners must not hide the keyboard-focused control (WCAG 2.2 AA)
- `UUX-056` `focus-not-obscured-enhanced` - Keep the entire focused component visible (WCAG 2.2 AAA)
- `UUX-057` `focus-appearance` - Verify focus indicator area and 3:1 state contrast; visible focus alone is not enough (WCAG 2.2 AAA) [H02]
- `UUX-058` `dragging-alternative` - Every author-controlled drag action needs a single-pointer and keyboard alternative (WCAG 2.2 AA)
- `UUX-059` `web-target-size` - Web pointer targets need 24×24 CSS px or a documented exception; do not substitute native units (WCAG 2.2 AA)
- `UUX-060` `consistent-help` - Repeated help mechanisms stay in the same relative order across a page set (WCAG 2.2 A)
- `UUX-061` `redundant-entry` - Reuse information already supplied in the same process unless re-entry is essential (WCAG 2.2 A)
- `UUX-062` `accessible-authentication` - Allow password managers and paste; provide a non-cognitive authentication path (WCAG 2.2 Minimum, AA). The Enhanced AAA criterion is not represented in the dataset
- `UUX-063` `auto-rotation-controls` - Carousels and moving content need pause/stop controls and must stop on focus or reduced motion (WAI)
- `UUX-064` `contextual-live-badge-updates` - Announce a changed count/status as a complete contextual phrase without moving focus; use one appropriate live/status region and atomic updates only when needed
- `UUX-065` `touch-target-size` - Min 44×44pt (Apple) / 48×48dp (Material); extend hit area beyond visual bounds if needed
- `UUX-066` `touch-spacing` - Minimum 8px/8dp gap between touch targets (Apple HIG, MD)
- `UUX-067` `hover-vs-tap` - Use click/tap for primary interactions; don't rely on hover alone
- `UUX-068` `loading-buttons` - Disable button during async operations; show spinner or progress
- `UUX-069` `error-feedback` - Clear error messages near problem
- `UUX-070` `cursor-pointer` - Add cursor-pointer to clickable elements (Web)
- `UUX-071` `gesture-conflicts` - Avoid horizontal swipe on main content; prefer vertical scroll
- `UUX-072` `tap-delay` - Use touch-action: manipulation to reduce 300ms delay (Web)
- `UUX-073` `standard-gestures` - Use platform standard gestures consistently; don't redefine (e.g. swipe-back, pinch-zoom) (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-074` `system-gestures` - Don't block system gestures (Control Center, back swipe, etc.) (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-075` `press-feedback` - Visual feedback on press (ripple/highlight; MD state layers)
- `UUX-076` `haptic-feedback` - Use haptic for confirmations and important actions; avoid overuse (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-077` `gesture-alternative` - Don't rely on gesture-only interactions; always provide visible controls for critical actions
- `UUX-078` `safe-area-awareness` - Keep primary touch targets away from notch, Dynamic Island, gesture bar and screen edges
- `UUX-079` `no-precision-required` - Avoid requiring pixel-perfect taps on small icons or thin edges
- `UUX-080` `swipe-clarity` - Swipe actions must show clear affordance or hint (chevron, label, tutorial)
- `UUX-081` `drag-threshold` - Use a movement threshold before starting drag to avoid accidental drags
- `UUX-082` `image-optimization` - Use WebP/AVIF, responsive images (srcset/sizes), lazy load non-critical assets
- `UUX-083` `image-dimension` - Declare width/height or use aspect-ratio to prevent layout shift (Core Web Vitals: CLS)
- `UUX-084` `font-loading` - Use font-display: swap/optional to avoid invisible text (FOIT); reserve space to reduce layout shift (MD)
- `UUX-085` `font-preload` - Preload only critical fonts; avoid overusing preload on every variant
- `UUX-086` `critical-css` - Prioritize above-the-fold CSS (inline critical CSS or early-loaded stylesheet)
- `UUX-087` `lazy-loading` - Lazy load non-hero components via dynamic import / route-level splitting
- `UUX-088` `bundle-splitting` - Split code by route/feature (React Suspense / Next.js dynamic) to reduce initial load and TTI
- `UUX-089` `third-party-scripts` - Load third-party scripts async/defer; audit and remove unnecessary ones (MD)
- `UUX-090` `reduce-reflows` - Avoid frequent layout reads/writes; batch DOM reads then writes
- `UUX-091` `content-jumping` - Reserve space for async content to avoid layout jumps (Core Web Vitals: CLS)
- `UUX-092` `lazy-load-below-fold` - Use loading="lazy" for below-the-fold images and heavy media
- `UUX-093` `virtualize-lists` - Virtualize lists with 50+ items to improve memory efficiency and scroll performance
- `UUX-094` `main-thread-budget` - Keep per-frame work under ~16ms for 60fps; move heavy tasks off main thread (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-095` `progressive-loading` - Use skeleton screens / shimmer instead of long blocking spinners for >1s operations (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-096` `input-latency` - Keep input latency under ~100ms for taps/scrolls (Material responsiveness standard)
- `UUX-097` `tap-feedback-speed` - Provide visual feedback within 100ms of tap (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-098` `debounce-throttle` - Use debounce/throttle for high-frequency events (scroll, resize, input)
- `UUX-099` `offline-support` - Provide offline state messaging and basic fallback (PWA / mobile)
- `UUX-100` `network-fallback` - Offer degraded modes for slow networks (lower-res images, fewer animations)
- `UUX-101` `style-match` - Match style to product type (use `--design-system` for recommendations)
- `UUX-102` `consistency` - Use same style across all pages
- `UUX-103` `no-emoji-icons` - Use SVG icons (Heroicons, Lucide), not emojis [D04, H08]
- `UUX-104` `color-palette-from-product` - Choose palette from product/industry (search `--domain color`)
- `UUX-105` `effects-match-style` - Shadows, blur, radius aligned with chosen style (glass / flat / clay etc.)
- `UUX-106` `platform-adaptive` - Respect platform idioms (iOS HIG vs Material): navigation, controls, typography, motion
- `UUX-107` `state-clarity` - Make hover/pressed/disabled states visually distinct while staying on-style (Material state layers)
- `UUX-108` `elevation-consistent` - Use a consistent elevation/shadow scale for cards, sheets, modals; avoid random shadow values
- `UUX-109` `dark-mode-pairing` - Design light/dark variants together to keep brand, contrast, and style consistent
- `UUX-110` `icon-style-consistent` - Use one icon set/visual language (stroke width, corner radius) across the product
- `UUX-111` `system-controls` - Prefer native/system controls over fully custom ones; only customize when branding requires it (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-112` `blur-purpose` - Use blur to indicate background dismissal (modals, sheets), not as decoration (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-113` `primary-action` - Each screen should have only one primary CTA; secondary actions visually subordinate (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-114` `viewport-meta` - width=device-width initial-scale=1 (never disable zoom) [H03]
- `UUX-115` `mobile-first` - Design mobile-first, then scale up to tablet and desktop
- `UUX-116` `breakpoint-consistency` - Use systematic breakpoints (e.g. 375 / 768 / 1024 / 1440)
- `UUX-117` `readable-font-size` - Minimum 16px body text on mobile (avoids iOS auto-zoom)
- `UUX-118` `line-length-control` - Mobile 35–60 chars per line; desktop 60–75 chars
- `UUX-119` `horizontal-scroll` - No horizontal scroll on mobile; ensure content fits viewport width
- `UUX-120` `spacing-scale` - Use 4pt/8dp incremental spacing system (Material Design)
- `UUX-121` `touch-density` - Keep component spacing comfortable for touch: not cramped, not causing mis-taps
- `UUX-122` `container-width` - Consistent max-width on desktop (max-w-6xl / 7xl)
- `UUX-123` `z-index-management` - Define layered z-index scale (e.g. 0 / 10 / 20 / 40 / 100 / 1000)
- `UUX-124` `fixed-element-offset` - Fixed navbar/bottom bar must reserve safe padding for underlying content
- `UUX-125` `scroll-behavior` - Avoid nested scroll regions that interfere with the main scroll experience
- `UUX-126` `viewport-units` - Prefer min-h-dvh over 100vh on mobile
- `UUX-127` `orientation-support` - Keep layout readable and operable in landscape mode
- `UUX-128` `content-priority` - Show core content first on mobile; fold or hide secondary content
- `UUX-129` `visual-hierarchy` - Establish hierarchy via size, spacing, contrast — not color alone
- `UUX-130` `compact-label-overflow` - Choose badge, status tag, filter chip, or removable value from its semantics; keep essential labels available and disclose unavoidable truncation to pointer and keyboard users
- `UUX-131` `chip-collection-reflow` - Wrap the collection before shrinking labels; make a `+n` overflow summary an operable disclosure instead of hiding values
- `UUX-132` `line-height` - Use 1.5-1.75 for body text
- `UUX-133` `line-length` - Limit to 65-75 characters per line
- `UUX-134` `font-pairing` - Match heading/body font personalities
- `UUX-135` `font-scale` - Consistent type scale (e.g. 12 14 16 18 24 32)
- `UUX-136` `contrast-readability` - Darker text on light backgrounds (e.g. slate-900 on white)
- `UUX-137` `text-styles-system` - Use platform type system: iOS 11 Dynamic Type styles / Material 5 type roles (display, headline, title, body, label) (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-138` `weight-hierarchy` - Use font-weight to reinforce hierarchy: Bold headings (600–700), Regular body (400), Medium labels (500) (MD)
- `UUX-139` `color-semantic` - Define semantic color tokens (primary, secondary, error, surface, on-surface) not raw hex in components (Material color system)
- `UUX-140` `color-dark-mode` - Dark mode uses desaturated / lighter tonal variants, not inverted colors; test contrast separately (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-141` `color-accessible-pairs` - Foreground/background pairs must meet 4.5:1 (AA) or 7:1 (AAA); use tools to verify (WCAG, MD) [D06]
- `UUX-142` `color-not-decorative-only` - Functional color (error red, success green) must include icon/text; avoid color-only meaning (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-143` `truncation-strategy` - Prefer wrapping over truncation; when truncating use ellipsis and provide full text via tooltip/expand (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-144` `letter-spacing` - Respect default letter-spacing per platform; avoid tight tracking on body text (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-145` `number-tabular` - Use tabular/monospaced figures for data columns, prices, and timers to prevent layout shift
- `UUX-146` `whitespace-balance` - Use whitespace intentionally to group related items and separate sections; avoid visual clutter (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-147` `heading-line-balance` - Use balanced wrapping on short headings as a progressive, user-agent-controlled heuristic; keep natural wrapping readable and never force final words together with blanket nonbreaking spaces
- `UUX-148` `long-token-wrapping` - Let URLs, IDs, and user content reflow with `overflow-wrap: anywhere` and a shrinkable flex/grid text child; do not apply `word-break: break-all` to normal prose
- `UUX-149` `duration-timing` - Choose shared motion tokens by distance, complexity, platform, and user context; test that feedback remains responsive instead of treating one duration range as universal
- `UUX-150` `transform-performance` - Use transform/opacity only; avoid animating width/height/top/left
- `UUX-151` `loading-states` - Match feedback to the expected wait and platform/component guidance; avoid both flashing indicators for near-instant work and unexplained long waits
- `UUX-152` `excessive-motion` - Animate 1-2 key elements per view max
- `UUX-153` `easing` - Use deceleration when arriving, acceleration when leaving, and linear motion for genuinely constant-rate progress or rotation
- `UUX-154` `motion-meaning` - Every animation must express a cause-effect relationship, not just be decorative (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-155` `state-transition` - State changes (hover / active / expanded / collapsed / modal) should animate smoothly, not snap
- `UUX-156` `continuity` - Page/screen transitions should maintain spatial continuity (shared element, directional slide) (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-157` `parallax-subtle` - Use parallax sparingly; must respect reduced-motion and not cause disorientation (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-158` `spring-physics` - Prefer spring/physics-based curves over linear or cubic-bezier for natural feel (Apple HIG fluid animations)
- `UUX-159` `exit-faster-than-enter` - Exit animations shorter than enter (~60–70% of enter duration) to feel responsive (MD motion)
- `UUX-160` `stagger-sequence` - Stagger list/grid item entrance by 30–50ms per item; avoid all-at-once or too-slow reveals (MD)
- `UUX-161` `shared-element-transition` - Use shared element / hero transitions for visual continuity between screens (MD, HIG)
- `UUX-162` `interruptible` - Animations must be interruptible; user tap/gesture cancels in-progress animation immediately (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-163` `no-blocking-animation` - Never block user input during an animation; UI must stay interactive (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-164` `fade-crossfade` - Use crossfade for content replacement within the same container (MD)
- `UUX-165` `scale-feedback` - Subtle scale (0.95–1.05) on press for tappable cards/buttons; restore on release (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-166` `gesture-feedback` - Drag, swipe, and pinch must provide real-time visual response tracking the finger (MD Motion)
- `UUX-167` `hierarchy-motion` - Use translate/scale direction to express hierarchy: enter from below = deeper, exit upward = back (MD)
- `UUX-168` `motion-consistency` - Unify duration/easing tokens globally; all animations share the same rhythm and feel
- `UUX-169` `opacity-threshold` - Fading elements should not linger below opacity 0.2; either fade fully or remain visible
- `UUX-170` `modal-motion` - Modals/sheets should animate from their trigger source (scale+fade or slide-in) for spatial context (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-171` `navigation-direction` - Forward navigation animates left/up; backward animates right/down — keep direction logically consistent (HIG)
- `UUX-172` `layout-shift-avoid` - Animations must not cause layout reflow or CLS; use transform for position changes
- `UUX-173` `cancellable-state-transitions` - Rapid state changes must cancel/replace prior micro-interactions safely, set the new final state explicitly, and never depend on an animation-end event for correctness
- `UUX-174` `input-labels` - Visible label per input (not placeholder-only) [H09]
- `UUX-175` `error-placement` - Show a specific error below the related field and connect it with aria-describedby
- `UUX-176` `submit-feedback` - Loading then success/error state on submit
- `UUX-177` `required-indicators` - Mark required fields (e.g. asterisk)
- `UUX-178` `empty-states` - Helpful message and action when no content
- `UUX-179` `toast-dismiss` - Auto-dismiss toasts in 3-5s
- `UUX-180` `confirmation-dialogs` - Confirm before destructive actions
- `UUX-181` `input-helper-text` - Provide persistent helper text below complex inputs, not just placeholder (Material Design)
- `UUX-182` `disabled-states` - Disabled elements use reduced opacity (0.38–0.5) + cursor change + semantic attribute (MD)
- `UUX-183` `progressive-disclosure` - Reveal complex options progressively; don't overwhelm users upfront (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-184` `inline-validation` - Validate on blur (not keystroke); show error only after user finishes input (MD)
- `UUX-185` `input-type-keyboard` - Use semantic input types (email, tel, number) to trigger the correct mobile keyboard (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-186` `password-toggle` - Provide show/hide toggle for password fields (MD)
- `UUX-187` `autofill-support` - Use autocomplete / textContentType attributes so the system can autofill (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-188` `undo-support` - Allow undo for destructive or bulk actions (e.g. "Undo delete" toast) (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-189` `success-feedback` - Confirm completed actions with brief visual feedback (checkmark, toast, color flash) (MD)
- `UUX-190` `error-recovery` - Error messages must include a clear recovery path (retry, edit, help link) (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-191` `multi-step-progress` - Multi-step flows show step indicator or progress bar; allow back navigation (MD)
- `UUX-192` `form-autosave` - Long forms should auto-save drafts to prevent data loss on accidental dismissal (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-193` `sheet-dismiss-confirm` - Confirm before dismissing a sheet/modal with unsaved changes (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-194` `error-clarity` - Error messages must state cause + how to fix (not just "Invalid input") (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-195` `field-grouping` - Group related fields logically (fieldset/legend or visual grouping) (MD)
- `UUX-196` `read-only-distinction` - Read-only state should be visually and semantically different from disabled (MD)
- `UUX-197` `focus-management` - After failed submission with multiple errors, focus the error summary; without a summary, focus the first invalid field
- `UUX-198` `error-summary` - Put a focusable summary at the top after failed submit, link each item to its invalid field, and retain inline field errors
- `UUX-199` `touch-friendly-input` - Mobile input height ≥44px to meet touch target requirements (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-200` `destructive-emphasis` - Destructive actions use semantic danger color (red) and are visually separated from primary actions (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-201` `toast-accessibility` - Toasts must not steal focus; use aria-live="polite" for screen reader announcement (WCAG)
- `UUX-202` `aria-live-errors` - Form errors use aria-live region or role="alert" to notify screen readers (WCAG)
- `UUX-203` `contrast-feedback` - Error and success state colors must meet 4.5:1 contrast ratio (WCAG, MD) [D06]
- `UUX-204` `timeout-feedback` - Request timeout must show clear feedback with retry option (MD)
- `UUX-205` `bottom-nav-limit` - Bottom navigation max 5 items; use labels with icons (Material Design)
- `UUX-206` `drawer-usage` - Use drawer/sidebar for secondary navigation, not primary actions (Material Design)
- `UUX-207` `back-behavior` - Back navigation must be predictable and consistent; preserve scroll/state (Apple HIG, MD)
- `UUX-208` `deep-linking` - All key screens must be reachable via deep link / URL for sharing and notifications (Apple HIG, MD)
- `UUX-209` `tab-bar-ios` - iOS: use bottom Tab Bar for top-level navigation (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-210` `top-app-bar-android` - Android: use Top App Bar with navigation icon for primary structure (Material Design)
- `UUX-211` `nav-label-icon` - Navigation items must have both icon and text label; icon-only nav harms discoverability (MD)
- `UUX-212` `nav-state-active` - Current location must be visually highlighted (color, weight, indicator) in navigation (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-213` `nav-hierarchy` - Primary nav (tabs/bottom bar) vs secondary nav (drawer/settings) must be clearly separated (MD)
- `UUX-214` `modal-escape` - Modals and sheets must offer a clear close/dismiss affordance; swipe-down to dismiss on mobile (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-215` `search-accessible` - Search must be easily reachable (top bar or tab); provide recent/suggested queries (MD)
- `UUX-216` `breadcrumb-web` - Web: use breadcrumbs for 3+ level deep hierarchies to aid orientation (MD)
- `UUX-217` `state-preservation` - Navigating back must restore previous scroll position, filter state, and input (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-218` `gesture-nav-support` - Support system gesture navigation (iOS swipe-back, Android predictive back) without conflict (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-219` `tab-badge` - Use badges on nav items sparingly to indicate unread/pending; clear after user visits (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-220` `overflow-menu` - When actions exceed available space, use overflow/more menu instead of cramming (MD)
- `UUX-221` `bottom-nav-top-level` - Bottom nav is for top-level screens only; never nest sub-navigation inside it (MD)
- `UUX-222` `adaptive-navigation` - Large screens (≥1024px) prefer sidebar; small screens use bottom/top nav (Material Adaptive)
- `UUX-223` `back-stack-integrity` - Never silently reset the navigation stack or unexpectedly jump to home (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-224` `navigation-consistency` - Navigation placement must stay the same across all pages; don't change by page type
- `UUX-225` `avoid-mixed-patterns` - Don't mix Tab + Sidebar + Bottom Nav at the same hierarchy level
- `UUX-226` `modal-vs-navigation` - Modals must not be used for primary navigation flows; they break the user's path (HIG)
- `UUX-227` `focus-on-route-change` - After page transition, move focus to main content region for screen reader users (WCAG)
- `UUX-228` `persistent-nav` - Core navigation must remain reachable from deep pages; don't hide it entirely in sub-flows (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-229` `destructive-nav-separation` - Dangerous actions (delete account, logout) must be visually and spatially separated from normal nav items (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-230` `empty-nav-state` - When a nav destination is unavailable, explain why instead of silently hiding it (MD)
- `UUX-231` `chart-type` - Match chart type to data type (trend → line, comparison → bar, proportion → pie/donut)
- `UUX-232` `color-guidance` - Use accessible color palettes; avoid red/green only pairs for colorblind users (WCAG, MD)
- `UUX-233` `data-table` - Provide table alternative for accessibility; charts alone are not screen-reader friendly (WCAG)
- `UUX-234` `pattern-texture` - Supplement color with patterns, textures, or shapes so data is distinguishable without color (WCAG, MD)
- `UUX-235` `legend-visible` - Always show legend; position near the chart, not detached below a scroll fold (MD)
- `UUX-236` `tooltip-on-interact` - Provide tooltips/data labels on hover (Web) or tap (mobile) showing exact values (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-237` `axis-labels` - Label axes with units and readable scale; avoid truncated or rotated labels on mobile
- `UUX-238` `responsive-chart` - Charts must reflow or simplify on small screens (e.g. horizontal bar instead of vertical, fewer ticks)
- `UUX-239` `empty-data-state` - Show meaningful empty state when no data exists ("No data yet" + guidance), not a blank chart (MD)
- `UUX-240` `loading-chart` - Use skeleton or shimmer placeholder while chart data loads; don't show an empty axis frame
- `UUX-241` `animation-optional` - Chart entrance animations must respect prefers-reduced-motion; data should be readable immediately (HIG)
- `UUX-242` `large-dataset` - For 1000+ data points, aggregate or sample; provide drill-down for detail instead of rendering all (MD)
- `UUX-243` `number-formatting` - Use locale-aware formatting for numbers, dates, currencies on axes and labels (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-244` `touch-target-chart` - Interactive chart elements (points, segments) must have ≥44pt tap area or expand on touch (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-245` `no-pie-overuse` - Avoid pie/donut for >5 categories; switch to bar chart for clarity
- `UUX-246` `contrast-data` - Data lines/bars vs background ≥3:1; data text labels ≥4.5:1 (WCAG) [D06]
- `UUX-247` `legend-interactive` - Legends should be clickable to toggle series visibility (MD)
- `UUX-248` `direct-labeling` - For small datasets, label values directly on the chart to reduce eye travel
- `UUX-249` `tooltip-keyboard` - Tooltip content must be keyboard-reachable and not rely on hover alone (WCAG)
- `UUX-250` `sortable-table` - Data tables must support sorting with aria-sort indicating current sort state (WCAG)
- `UUX-251` `axis-readability` - Axis ticks must not be cramped; maintain readable spacing, auto-skip on small screens
- `UUX-252` `data-density` - Limit information density per chart to avoid cognitive overload; split into multiple charts if needed
- `UUX-253` `trend-emphasis` - Emphasize data trends over decoration; avoid heavy gradients/shadows that obscure the data
- `UUX-254` `gridline-subtle` - Grid lines should be low-contrast (e.g. gray-200) so they don't compete with data
- `UUX-255` `focusable-elements` - Interactive chart elements (points, bars, slices) must be keyboard-navigable (WCAG)
- `UUX-256` `screen-reader-summary` - Provide a text summary or aria-label describing the chart's key insight for screen readers (WCAG)
- `UUX-257` `error-state-chart` - Data load failure must show error message with retry action, not a broken/empty chart
- `UUX-258` `export-option` - For data-heavy products, offer CSV/image export of chart data
- `UUX-259` `drill-down-consistency` - Drill-down interactions must maintain a clear back-path and hierarchy breadcrumb
- `UUX-260` `time-scale-clarity` - Time series charts must clearly label time granularity (day/week/month) and allow switching
- `UUX-261` **New project/page or system-wide visual direction** → use `--design-system`.
- `UUX-262` **Targeted concern or component bug** → use one explicit `--domain`.
- `UUX-263` **Known implementation stack** → use `--stack`; add a separate domain search only for a distinct design concern.
- `UUX-264` **Product type**: SaaS, e-commerce, portfolio, dashboard, entertainment, tool, productivity, or hybrid
- `UUX-265` **Target audience & context**: age group, usage context (commute, leisure, work)
- `UUX-266` **Style keywords**: playful, vibrant, minimal, dark mode, content-first, immersive, etc.
- `UUX-267` **Stack**: detect from the project — check `package.json` deps (react/next/vue/svelte/nuxt/@angular), `pubspec.yaml` (Flutter), `*.xcodeproj`/`Package.swift` (SwiftUI), `composer.json` (Laravel), or React Native markers (`app.json` + `react-native` dep). If nothing is detectable and stack guidance matters, ask the user. **Never assume a stack** — a hardcoded default silently misroutes every recommendation.
- `UUX-268` `design-system/<project-slug>/MASTER.md` — Global Source of Truth
- `UUX-269` `design-system/<project-slug>/pages/` — Folder for page-specific overrides
- `UUX-270` Read `design-system/<project-slug>/MASTER.md`
- `UUX-271` Check if `design-system/<project-slug>/pages/<page-name>.md` exists — if so, its rules override Master
- `UUX-272` Otherwise use Master rules exclusively
- `UUX-273` `--motion` attaches a ready-to-use GSAP snippet (with framework notes, Do/Don't, and performance notes) pulled from `--domain gsap`, matched to the resolved tier (Subtle/Standard/Complex).
- `UUX-274` `--density` overrides the `--space-*` CSS variable table in the ASCII/markdown/MASTER.md output — use it for dashboards (high) vs. marketing pages (low) without hand-editing tokens.
- `UUX-275` Leaving a dial unset keeps that part of the output exactly as it was before (no behavior change).
- `UUX-276` Retry once with a narrower query or an explicit domain/stack.
- `UUX-277` If still empty, fall back to the priority table above and say explicitly to the user that this recommendation came from the built-in defaults, not a database match (e.g. "no palette match for X, using general SaaS defaults").
- `UUX-278` Never present a 0-result search as if it returned data.
- `UUX-279` Keep one dominant intent and 2–5 meaningful terms per query: `"keyboard focus modal"`, not a full audit checklist
- `UUX-280` Retry once with a narrower phrase or explicit domain/stack; do not cycle through unrelated keywords
- `UUX-281` Use `--design-system` for a new project/page and `--domain` for a focused concern
- `UUX-282` Pass the detected stack explicitly for implementation-specific guidance

### emil-design-eng

- `EMIL-001` **Spatial consistency**: toast enters and exits from the same direction, making swipe-to-dismiss feel intuitive
- `EMIL-002` **State indication**: a morphing feedback button shows the state change
- `EMIL-003` **Explanation**: a marketing animation that shows how a feature works
- `EMIL-004` **Feedback**: a button scales down on press, confirming the interface heard the user
- `EMIL-005` **Preventing jarring changes**: elements appearing or disappearing without transition feel broken
- `EMIL-006` A **fast-spinning spinner** makes loading feel faster (same load time, different perception)
- `EMIL-007` A **180ms select** animation feels more responsive than a **400ms** one
- `EMIL-008` **Instant tooltips** after the first one is open (skip delay + skip animation) make the whole toolbar feel faster
- `EMIL-009` Drag interactions with momentum
- `EMIL-010` Elements that should feel "alive" (like Apple's Dynamic Island)
- `EMIL-011` Gestures that can be interrupted mid-animation
- `EMIL-012` Decorative mouse-tracking interactions
- `EMIL-013` **Developer experience is key.** No hooks, no context, no complex setup. Insert `<Toaster />` once, call `toast()` from anywhere. The less friction to adopt, the more people will use it.
- `EMIL-014` **Good defaults matter more than options.** Ship beautiful out of the box. Most users never customize. The default easing, timing, and visual design should be excellent.
- `EMIL-015` **Naming creates identity.** "Sonner" (French for "to ring") feels more elegant than "react-toast". Sacrifice discoverability for memorability when appropriate.
- `EMIL-016` **Handle edge cases invisibly.** Pause toast timers when the tab is hidden. Fill gaps between stacked toasts with pseudo-elements to maintain hover state. Capture pointer events during drag. Users never notice these, and that is exactly right.
- `EMIL-017` **Use transitions, not keyframes, for dynamic UI.** Toasts are added rapidly. Keyframes restart from zero on interruption. Transitions retarget smoothly.
- `EMIL-018` **Build a great documentation site.** Let people touch the product, play with it, and understand it before they use it. Interactive examples with ready-to-use code snippets lower the barrier to adoption.
- `EMIL-019` Do colors transition smoothly, or do you see two distinct states overlapping?
- `EMIL-020` Does the easing feel right, or does it start/stop abruptly?
- `EMIL-021` Is the transform-origin correct, or does the element scale from the wrong point?
- `EMIL-022` Are multiple animated properties (opacity, transform, color) in sync?
