### accessibility-review

- `A11Y-001` **1.1.1** Non-text content has alt text [H04]
- `A11Y-002` **1.3.1** Info and structure conveyed semantically
- `A11Y-003` **1.4.3** Contrast ratio >= 4.5:1 (normal text), >= 3:1 (large text) [D06]
- `A11Y-004` **1.4.11** Non-text contrast >= 3:1 (UI components, graphics)
- `A11Y-005` **2.1.1** All functionality available via keyboard [H05]
- `A11Y-006` **2.4.3** Logical focus order
- `A11Y-007` **2.4.7** Visible focus indicator [H02]
- `A11Y-008` **2.5.5** Touch target >= 44x44 CSS pixels
- `A11Y-009` **3.2.1** Predictable on focus (no unexpected changes)
- `A11Y-010` **3.3.1** Error identification (describe the error)
- `A11Y-011` **3.3.2** Labels or instructions for inputs [H09]
- `A11Y-012` **4.1.2** Name, role, value for all UI components [H05]
- `A11Y-013` Insufficient color contrast [D06]
- `A11Y-014` Missing form labels [H09]
- `A11Y-015` No keyboard access to interactive elements [H05]
- `A11Y-016` Missing alt text on meaningful images [H04]
- `A11Y-020` Time limits without extension options
- `A11Y-021` Automated scan (catches ~30% of issues)
- `A11Y-022` Keyboard-only navigation
- `A11Y-023` Screen reader testing (VoiceOver, NVDA)
- `A11Y-024` Color contrast verification [D06]
- `A11Y-025` Zoom to 200% — does layout break?
- `A11Y-027` Check component ARIA roles and keyboard behavior in the design spec
- `A11Y-028` Create tickets for each accessibility finding with severity and WCAG criterion
- `A11Y-029` Link findings to existing accessibility remediation epics
- `A11Y-030` **Start with contrast and keyboard** — These catch the most common and impactful issues.
- `A11Y-031` **Test with real assistive technology** — My audit is a great start, but manual testing with VoiceOver/NVDA catches things I can't.
- `A11Y-032` **Prioritize by impact** — Fix issues that block users first, polish later.

### impeccable

- `IMP-0003` What assumptions were made? (Large screen? Mouse input? Fast connection?)
- `IMP-0007` **Input method**: Touch, mouse, keyboard, voice, gamepad?
- `IMP-0040` Multi-column forms where appropriate
- `IMP-0049` Multi-select with Shift/Cmd
- `IMP-0102` **Different input methods**: Touch, mouse, keyboard
- `IMP-0105` **Primary gesture**: Tap it and confirm it responds as designed, then drag it with the target input method; the drag must complete, not just start
- `IMP-0107` **Evidence**: Say what produced the evidence: an emulated viewport, synthesized touch input through a browser tool, which engine ran it (Chromium is not Safari), or a physical device. Screenshots and resized viewports verify layout, never a gesture. Name what stayed untested and move on; unreachable hardware is a reported gap, not a blocker
- `IMP-0151` **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators [H09]
- `IMP-0256` **Contrast:** body and placeholder text ≥4.5:1, large text ≥3:1. On colored surfaces tint secondary text from that hue or the foreground; never gray. [D06]
- `IMP-0295` **Read `.impeccable/critique/ignore.md`** if it exists. Drop matching findings silently; it is the only prior-run input critique consumes.
- `IMP-0325` Be specific. "The submit button," not "some elements."
- `IMP-0380` Long-form articles: one reading path; gather related links into a single block at the end instead of scattering them mid-flow
- `IMP-0384` Confirmation of user actions (save, submit, delete)
- `IMP-0387` Form validation feedback (inline, not just on submit)
- `IMP-0394` Cancel buttons on forms and modals
- `IMP-0404` Constraints preventing invalid input (date pickers, dropdowns)
- `IMP-0424` Specific problem identification ("Email is missing @" not "Invalid input")
- `IMP-0427` Non-blocking error handling (don't wipe the form)
- `IMP-0435` Tries to bulk-select, batch-edit, and automate
- `IMP-0476` Unlabeled form fields or buttons
- `IMP-0480` Submits forms with unexpected data (emoji, RTL text, very long values)
- `IMP-0488` How does the UI handle unexpected input (emoji, special chars, paste from Excel)?
- `IMP-0502` Can forms use autocomplete and smart defaults?
- `IMP-0506` Large text inputs required where selection would work
- `IMP-0514` **Fidelity.** Start from the measurement, then judge what it cannot: read `.impeccable/review/diff/final/report.json` (and hero) first; every region scored `missing` or `contradicted` is a matrix row in that state unless the paired crop under `regions/` shows the score is wrong, and you say why; a region scored `match` still gets your eye for lettering character and material, which the numbers do not measure. Then, against your own element inventory of the approved comp, never against the contract's summary of it: topology, reading order, focal scale, overlaps and z-order, density, signature geometry, the primary action's treatment (a CTA the comp physically works, dissolves, or stamps is a signature element; its plain-rectangle rendition is contradicted), navigation items and icons, headline levels and scale relationships. Classify every salient element: match, acceptable adaptation, missing, contradicted, or added without approval. Three rows are mandatory in every matrix. TYPE: the display lettering's character, compression, width, weight, contrast, terminals, against the comp's; a face of a different character is contradicted however the layout matches. MATERIAL: an element rendered as flat CSS or clean vector where the comp shows painted, textured, dimensional, or photographic material is contradicted regardless of placement; medium is part of the promise. GROUND: the page field's value and temperature against the comp's, sampled from pixels on both sides when tooling allows rather than judged from memory, and read as the net on-screen result where a texture or tile paints over the base color; a ground warmer or cooler than the comp's is contradicted however faithfully the layout matches, and drift toward the rendition prior (warm cream on light grounds, blue-black slate on dark) is the direction to hunt. With no approved comp, TYPE and MATERIAL do not lapse: judge them against the contract's OWN-WORLD and the world's real materials, and treat faked physicality (CSS bevels, embossing, stamped-metal or chalk effects imitating a material the page never renders) as contradicted on its face; imitation material is the single most reliable mark of machine-made design. GROUND narrows rather than lapses: with no comp to sample, a color OWN-WORLD names is the target and the same warmer-or-cooler judgment applies; when OWN-WORLD names none, there is no GROUND authority, and the review says so in place of a verdict, because a target the reviewer invents turns the check into taste. A critique-reference comp on such a build is provocation, not spec: no element matrix, no adaptation citations, no asset obligations; its one contribution is what the image dared that the build did not, and dares worth adopting enter material_fixes as ordinary ordered fixes. An adaptation counts as intentional only when it cites the user answer, surface brief, accessibility need, or product truth that forced it; an uncited deviation is a defect. A missing signature element, a changed topology, or content added without approval fails fidelity and outranks every craft point in material_fixes. When MATERIAL is contradicted on the focal element, or contradiction is the page rather than the exception, stop ordering repairs: make the first material fix a rebuild directive naming the comp regions to re-derive and the assets to produce; a list of patches against a rejected page launders the rejection into an approval. A fix that requires producing an asset says so explicitly ("produce: <region> as a raster asset"), never phrased as a style adjustment the parent will answer with CSS. The comp is the spec for composition, topology, element inventory, density, lettering character, and material; it is not a pixel spec for semantics, accessibility, or responsive reflow, and that allowance covers translation, never replacement.
- `IMP-0516` **Contract, promise by promise.** First verify FORM carries the seed key the concept roll printed; a contract with no seed key, or one the parent cannot corroborate, means the roll was skipped, a material fix ahead of any craft point. Then, for each of the five blocks: does the render keep the promise? Apply the memory test to the first viewport.
- `IMP-0517` **Truth.** Demonstration data authored and labeled synthetic; no invented commercial claims; unanswered claims present as marked placeholders, not omissions. Every raster region of the spec shipped as its plate (the spec names the file; the page references it; the region's diff row is not `missing`), not a gradient, an inline SVG, or a many-vertex `clip-path` standing in for it, and every produced asset visibly present in the screenshots; an asset applied at near-zero opacity or buried behind a wash is a compliance token, not a shipped material, and the detector's `buried-raster` and `organic-clip-path` findings in the packet are material fixes.
- `IMP-0568` **Combine related actions**: Merge similar buttons, consolidate forms, group related content
- `IMP-0611` **Test with extreme inputs**:
- `IMP-0621` Validation errors
- `IMP-0633` Set minimum readable sizes (16px body on mobile, the same floor the typography guidance sets; 14px only for genuinely secondary text. iOS Safari force-zooms focused inputs under 16px, which breaks form layouts)
- `IMP-0649` Inline errors near fields
- `IMP-0653` Preserve user input on error
- `IMP-0655` 400: Show validation errors
- `IMP-0694` Required fields
- `IMP-0695` Format validation (email, phone, URL)
- `IMP-0696` Length limits
- `IMP-0697` Pattern matching
- `IMP-0698` Custom validation rules
- `IMP-0699` Never trust client-side only
- `IMP-0700` Validate and sanitize all inputs
- `IMP-0701` Protect against injection attacks
- `IMP-0702` Rate limiting
- `IMP-0732` A behavioral regression for each confirmed gesture fix, when the project's test runner can drive input
- `IMP-0735` Assume perfect input (validate everything)
- `IMP-0739` Trust client-side validation alone
- `IMP-0744` **Emoji**: Use emoji in all text fields
- `IMP-0749` **Concurrent actions**: Click submit 10 times rapidly
- `IMP-0769` how the structure changes across containers, viewports, input modes, and content extremes.
- `IMP-0800` Run `.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable concept-seed --scope direction --mode <mode>` and follow what it prints. No substitute, no skip: on a new or replacement world, writing artifact code before this script has run and its assignment is acknowledged is a contract violation, whatever the harness, the model, or the time pressure; the roll is what keeps every run from converging on the category default. The script assigns the direction to build and deals catalog challengers. Fuse each challenger before judging it: the challenger supplies the form and its system grammar, the product supplies every fact, clarity wins conflicts. Weigh fused challengers against the assigned direction on exactly two axes, audience identification and product clarity. Losing to strong grounded material is a valid outcome; beating a thin or tool-monoculture list is the point. Close with a verdict per challenger, decided before any borrowing: wins (beats the assigned direction on both axes; becomes the build candidate), competitive (holds one axis; stays a full alternate), or declined (loses both). A declined challenger is not spent: name the one discipline of its system the assigned direction lacks, and raise the assigned direction to match before presenting it. A donation transfers ambition and system discipline (a palette's total commitment, a grid's density courage, a form's structural honesty), never the challenger's clothes; a lifted motif is a costume note, not a raise, and one world owns the page. Write each raise into the presented direction as its own line, named for its donor; a raise nobody can read did not happen.
- `IMP-0801` Present one direction, fully committed and already raised by the hand it beat, raises visible as named lines: world, first viewport, visitor path, signature interaction, cross-surface reach, honest risk. Route each challenger by verdict: winning and competitive challengers are full alternates with their QUALITY BAR cards and one-line case; declined challengers render demoted, compact and quiet, each carrying its verdict and what the direction kept from it, never full-size, never silently dropped, still adoptable on request. The verdict informs the user's choice, never pre-empts it; the demoted row is the hand's proof of judgment. A hand holds at most three full-card challengers: when the roll deals more, the three strongest join and the rest wait in the re-roll pool, noted in one line; dropping a challenger from the hand itself takes a named product-truth failure, disclosed. Add one card for your own top-ranked grounded candidate when it is not the assigned direction, kicker IMPECCABLE’S PICK, same anatomy as every card, with an honest risk line naming its familiarity when true: the strongest grounded direction is often where most runs in this category land, and the user deciding that trade is the point of showing it. Familiar and effective is a legitimate destination, not a failure of nerve; the pick card and the standing exit serve it at two depths. One pick card, never two, never a ranked list: a lineup of your candidates hands selection back to a taste function and invites the safest card. The pick never takes the lead position; when the dice assign your top candidate there is no pick card, and the assigned card notes it topped your list. Add re-roll with an optional one-line steer, in three registers: plain (a fresh hand, same spread), safer (your remaining conventional grounded candidates plus the canon against named competitors), bolder (foreign forms only, at full commitment). The register is the user's steering on the familiar-to-bold axis, never yours to pre-select; when the answer carries one, re-run the seed with `--register <value>` and the next `--reroll` round, and follow what it prints. A user saying "bolder" or "safer" while a direction round is open means these registers, never the bolder or harden commands. The two channels share this structure and differ only in richness: cards and boards on the decision page, names and one-liners through the structured tool, whose option list carries the assigned direction, the pick, the winning and competitive challengers, and the standing exit last; declined challengers fold into the assigned option's description as their kept lines, so the raise survives the text channel.
- `IMP-0809` **The first viewport is a thesis, not a header.** Demonstrate the mechanism immediately, at the scale the form has in life; do not trap the concept inside a standard hero or card shell. The memory test: if someone left after one viewport, what would they describe an hour later? If the honest answer is a mood, the concept has not committed yet.
- `IMP-0812` **Build the form's web leverage.** When the chosen world names a technique (canvas, WebGL, view transitions, generative motion), build the technique itself, not a static imitation of it.
- `IMP-0815` **Author motion as material.** Give the page the form's native motion once, orchestrated, rather than scattered hover effects. Bound expensive effects and keep content visible by default.
- `IMP-0895` Validation (confirm they did it right)
- `IMP-0933` Consistent affordances across the surface. Same button shape. Same form-control vocabulary. Same icon style.
- `IMP-0941` Reinventing standard affordances for flavor (custom scrollbars, weird form controls, non-standard modals).
- `IMP-1013` GraphQL to request only needed fields
- `IMP-1100` the states, content lengths, roles, and input methods users will actually encounter.

### taste-skill

- `TASTE-005` **Brand assets that already exist** - logo, color, type, photography. For redesigns, these are starting material, not optional input (see Section 11).
- `TASTE-022` **NEVER** use `useState` to track continuous values driven by user input (mouse position, scroll progress, pointer physics, magnetic hover). Use Motion's `useMotionValue` / `useTransform` / `useScroll`. `useState` re-renders the React tree on every change and collapses on mobile.
- `TASTE-074` **SHAPE CONSISTENCY LOCK (mandatory):** Pick ONE corner-radius scale for the page and stick to it. Options: all-sharp (radius 0), all-soft (radius 12-16px), all-pill (full radius for interactive). Mixed systems are allowed only when there is a documented rule (e.g. "buttons are full-pill, cards are 16px, inputs are 8px") and that rule is followed everywhere. Round buttons in a square layout, or square cards on a pill-button page, is broken design.
- `TASTE-077` **Error States:** Clear, inline (forms), or contextual (toasts only for transient).
- `TASTE-082` **FORM CONTRAST CHECK (mandatory, a11y):** Form inputs, placeholder text, focus rings, helper text, and error text all pass WCAG AA contrast against the section background. Light placeholders on a near-white form, white form on white page section, form labels grayer than 4.5:1 contrast → all banned. Audit every form before shipping. [H02, D06]
- `TASTE-083` Label ABOVE input. Helper text optional but present in markup. Error text BELOW input. Standard `gap-2` for input blocks.
- `TASTE-084` No placeholder-as-label. Ever.
- `TASTE-112` `https://picsum.photos/seed/{descriptive-seed}/{w}/{h}` for placeholder photography (seed should describe the section, e.g. `marrow-cookware-kitchen`)
- `TASTE-115` **Last resort: tell the user.** If neither is possible, do NOT fill the page with hand-rolled SVG illustrations or div-based "fake screenshots." Instead, leave clearly-labeled placeholder slots (`<!-- TODO: hero product photo, 1600x1200 -->`) and at the end of the response say: *"This page needs real images at: \[list of placements\]. Please generate or provide them."*
- `TASTE-222` **NO generic avatars.** No SVG "egg" or Lucide user icons → use believable photo placeholders or specific styling.
- `TASTE-229` **NO broken Unsplash links.** Use `https://picsum.photos/seed/{descriptive-string}/{w}/{h}`, or generated photo placeholders, or actual assets.
- `TASTE-247` **NO "From the field" / "Field notes" / "Currently on the bench" / "On our desks" / "Loose plates" style poetic labels** on quote, blog, or sidebar sections. Reads as performative-craftsman. Use plain functional labels ("Testimonials", "Latest writing", "Now working on") or skip the label.
- `TASTE-252` **NO pills/labels/tags overlaid on images.** No `<span>` overlays on photos with tags like `Brand · 02`, `PLATE · BRAND`, `Field notes - journal`. Either let the image speak alone, or add a caption directly below (outside the image).
- `TASTE-253` **NO photo-credit captions as decoration.** Strings like `Field study no. 12 · Ines Caetano`, `Plate 03 · House archive`, `Frame XII · 35mm` under stock/picsum images are pretentious. Photo credit is allowed ONLY when there is a real photographer being credited for a real photo (with permission). Otherwise: skip the caption or use a one-line functional caption ("The 6-quart, in Sage.").
- `TASTE-256` **NO decoration text strip at hero bottom.** Patterns like `BRAND. MOTION. SPATIAL.`, `TYPE / FORM / MOTION`, `DESIGN · BUILD · SHIP`, `ESTD. 2018 · LISBON · BRAND. MOTION. SPATIAL.` as a small mono-caps strip across the bottom of the hero are an agency-portfolio cliché. Banned by default. Only acceptable when the strip carries real, navigable links (sticky bottom nav) or real status info (cookie banner, build info on a docs site).
- `TASTE-267` **Banned in en-dash form too (`–`) when used as a separator.** Date ranges (`2018-2026`) use a hyphen. Number ranges (`€40-80k`) use a hyphen.
- `TASTE-314` **Skeleton Shimmer** - Shifting light reflection across placeholders.
- `TASTE-338` **Respect existing analytics events.** Do not rename buttons, form fields, section IDs that downstream tracking depends on.
- `TASTE-350` Form field names or order (breaks analytics + autofill).
- `TASTE-367` Multi-step forms / wizards (use Form-specific patterns; this skill won't make them better).
- `TASTE-381` [ ] **Form Contrast Check**: form inputs, placeholders, focus rings, labels all pass WCAG AA against the section background? [H02]
- `TASTE-402` [ ] **Real images used** (gen-tool first, then Picsum-seed, then explicit placeholder slots) - NO div-based fake screenshots, NO hand-rolled decorative SVGs, NO pure-text minimalism?
- `TASTE-403` [ ] **No pills/labels overlaid on images** (no `Plate · Brand`, no `Field notes - journal`)?
- `TASTE-404` [ ] **No photo-credit captions as decoration** (`Field study no. 12 · Ines Caetano`)?

### ui-ux-pro-max

- `UUX-028` [ ] Long-form text measure remains readable on larger devices (no edge-to-edge paragraphs)
- `UUX-032` [ ] Form fields have labels, hints, and clear error messages
- `UUX-039` [ ] Failed forms retain inline field errors; multi-error forms also focus a linked error summary after submit
- `UUX-046` `form-labels` - Use label with for attribute
- `UUX-096` `input-latency` - Keep input latency under ~100ms for taps/scrolls (Material responsiveness standard)
- `UUX-098` `debounce-throttle` - Use debounce/throttle for high-frequency events (scroll, resize, input)
- `UUX-163` `no-blocking-animation` - Never block user input during an animation; UI must stay interactive (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-174` `input-labels` - Visible label per input (not placeholder-only) [H09]
- `UUX-175` `error-placement` - Show a specific error below the related field and connect it with aria-describedby
- `UUX-176` `submit-feedback` - Loading then success/error state on submit
- `UUX-177` `required-indicators` - Mark required fields (e.g. asterisk)
- `UUX-178` `empty-states` - Helpful message and action when no content
- `UUX-179` `toast-dismiss` - Auto-dismiss toasts in 3-5s
- `UUX-180` `confirmation-dialogs` - Confirm before destructive actions
- `UUX-181` `input-helper-text` - Provide persistent helper text below complex inputs, not just placeholder (Material Design)
- `UUX-182` `disabled-states` - Disabled elements use reduced opacity (0.38–0.5) + cursor change + semantic attribute (MD)
- `UUX-183` `progressive-disclosure` - Reveal complex options progressively; don't overwhelm users upfront (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-184` `inline-validation` - Validate on blur (not keystroke); show error only after user finishes input (MD)
- `UUX-185` `input-type-keyboard` - Use semantic input types (email, tel, number) to trigger the correct mobile keyboard (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-186` `password-toggle` - Provide show/hide toggle for password fields (MD)
- `UUX-187` `autofill-support` - Use autocomplete / textContentType attributes so the system can autofill (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-188` `undo-support` - Allow undo for destructive or bulk actions (e.g. "Undo delete" toast) (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-189` `success-feedback` - Confirm completed actions with brief visual feedback (checkmark, toast, color flash) (MD)
- `UUX-190` `error-recovery` - Error messages must include a clear recovery path (retry, edit, help link) (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-191` `multi-step-progress` - Multi-step flows show step indicator or progress bar; allow back navigation (MD)
- `UUX-192` `form-autosave` - Long forms should auto-save drafts to prevent data loss on accidental dismissal (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-193` `sheet-dismiss-confirm` - Confirm before dismissing a sheet/modal with unsaved changes (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-194` `error-clarity` - Error messages must state cause + how to fix (not just "Invalid input") (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-195` `field-grouping` - Group related fields logically (fieldset/legend or visual grouping) (MD)
- `UUX-196` `read-only-distinction` - Read-only state should be visually and semantically different from disabled (MD)
- `UUX-197` `focus-management` - After failed submission with multiple errors, focus the error summary; without a summary, focus the first invalid field
- `UUX-198` `error-summary` - Put a focusable summary at the top after failed submit, link each item to its invalid field, and retain inline field errors
- `UUX-199` `touch-friendly-input` - Mobile input height ≥44px to meet touch target requirements (Apple HIG)
- `UUX-200` `destructive-emphasis` - Destructive actions use semantic danger color (red) and are visually separated from primary actions (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-201` `toast-accessibility` - Toasts must not steal focus; use aria-live="polite" for screen reader announcement (WCAG)
- `UUX-202` `aria-live-errors` - Form errors use aria-live region or role="alert" to notify screen readers (WCAG)
- `UUX-203` `contrast-feedback` - Error and success state colors must meet 4.5:1 contrast ratio (WCAG, MD) [D06]
- `UUX-204` `timeout-feedback` - Request timeout must show clear feedback with retry option (MD)
- `UUX-217` `state-preservation` - Navigating back must restore previous scroll position, filter state, and input (HIG, MD)
- `UUX-240` `loading-chart` - Use skeleton or shimmer placeholder while chart data loads; don't show an empty axis frame

### emil-design-eng

- `EMIL-007` A **180ms select** animation feels more responsive than a **400ms** one

## Dashboards (dashboard, 2096 rules)

- `AAC-WR-078` AAC-WR-001 Rule 78, Text color [D19, D15]
- `AAC-WR-094` AAC-WR-001 Rule 94, Use of tables
- `AAC-WR-095` AAC-WR-001 Rule 95, Table titles
- `AAC-WR-096` AAC-WR-001 Rule 96, Column headings
- `AAC-WR-097` AAC-WR-001 Rule 97, Numeric alignment
- `AAC-WR-098` AAC-WR-001 Rule 98, Table formatting [D16]
- `AAC-WR-099` AAC-WR-001 Rule 99, Empty and zero values

### AAC tokens

- `AAC-TOK-COLOR-ACCENT` DESIGN-SYSTEM.md token `accent` in the Color table [D05]
- `AAC-TOK-COLOR-TINT` DESIGN-SYSTEM.md token `tint` in the Color table
- `AAC-TOK-COLOR-CAPTION` DESIGN-SYSTEM.md token `caption` in the Color table [D19, D06]

- `AAC-TOK-COLOR-TEXT` DESIGN-SYSTEM.md token `text` in the Color table [D15, D19]

### accessibility-review

- `A11Y-001` **1.1.1** Non-text content has alt text [H04]
- `A11Y-002` **1.3.1** Info and structure conveyed semantically
- `A11Y-003` **1.4.3** Contrast ratio >= 4.5:1 (normal text), >= 3:1 (large text) [D06]
- `A11Y-004` **1.4.11** Non-text contrast >= 3:1 (UI components, graphics)
- `A11Y-005` **2.1.1** All functionality available via keyboard [H05]
- `A11Y-006` **2.4.3** Logical focus order
- `A11Y-007` **2.4.7** Visible focus indicator [H02]
- `A11Y-008` **2.5.5** Touch target >= 44x44 CSS pixels
- `A11Y-009` **3.2.1** Predictable on focus (no unexpected changes)
- `A11Y-011` **3.3.2** Labels or instructions for inputs [H09]
- `A11Y-012` **4.1.2** Name, role, value for all UI components [H05]
- `A11Y-013` Insufficient color contrast [D06]
- `A11Y-015` No keyboard access to interactive elements [H05]
- `A11Y-016` Missing alt text on meaningful images [H04]
- `A11Y-017` Focus traps in modals
- `A11Y-018` Missing ARIA landmarks
- `A11Y-019` Auto-playing media without controls
- `A11Y-020` Time limits without extension options
- `A11Y-021` Automated scan (catches ~30% of issues)
- `A11Y-022` Keyboard-only navigation
- `A11Y-023` Screen reader testing (VoiceOver, NVDA)
- `A11Y-024` Color contrast verification [D06]
- `A11Y-025` Zoom to 200% — does layout break?
- `A11Y-026` Inspect color values, font sizes, and touch targets directly from Figma
- `A11Y-027` Check component ARIA roles and keyboard behavior in the design spec
- `A11Y-028` Create tickets for each accessibility finding with severity and WCAG criterion
- `A11Y-029` Link findings to existing accessibility remediation epics
- `A11Y-030` **Start with contrast and keyboard** — These catch the most common and impactful issues.
- `A11Y-031` **Test with real assistive technology** — My audit is a great start, but manual testing with VoiceOver/NVDA catches things I can't.
- `A11Y-032` **Prioritize by impact** — Fix issues that block users first, polish later.

### frontend-design

- `FD-001` Accenting just a single word or phrase in a headline, like putting one word in italic/bold or a different color.
- `FD-002` Using all caps for labels. [D03]
- `FD-003` Adding unnecessary typographic labels above content.
- `FD-004` a warm cream background (near #F4F1EA) with a high-contrast serif display and a terracotta or warm-clay accent (often near #D97757 — Anthropic's own Claude-interaction accent, so on a user's brief it reads as a tell);
- `FD-005` a near-black background with a single bright acid-green or vermilion accent;
- `FD-006` a broadsheet-style layout with hairline rules, zero border-radius, and dense newspaper-like columns;
- `FD-007` the SaaS-card kit: content chopped into identical rounded cards, one border-radius on everything regardless of hierarchy, the same soft grey shadow (rgba(0,0,0,.1)) under each, and gradient washes as decoration;
- `FD-008` template chrome that appears whatever the subject: a tracked-out ALL-CAPS eyebrow label above every heading; meta strings joined with middle dots ('A · B · C'); labels built as 'WORD — fragment' with a spaced em dash; tinted near-black (#0B0B0B, #111) standing in for black; a monospace face for small data labels; a '→' appended to link and button text. [D03]
- `FD-009` Color: describe the core base palette as 4–6 named hex values.
- `FD-010` Type: the typefaces and their roles.
- `FD-011` Layout: a layout concept, using one-sentence prose descriptions and ASCII wireframes to ideate and compare. Include alignment guidance; should the content be left aligned, center aligned, justified?
- `FD-012` Principles: the high-level guidance for what makes this page unique.

### impeccable

- `IMP-0001` **Identify the source context**:
- `IMP-0002` What was it designed for originally? (Desktop web? Mobile app?)
- `IMP-0003` What assumptions were made? (Large screen? Mouse input? Fast connection?)
- `IMP-0004` What works well in current context?
- `IMP-0005` **Understand target context**:
- `IMP-0006` **Device**: Mobile, tablet, desktop, TV, watch, print?
- `IMP-0007` **Input method**: Touch, mouse, keyboard, voice, gamepad?
- `IMP-0008` **Screen constraints**: Size, resolution, orientation?
- `IMP-0009` **Connection**: Fast wifi, slow 3G, offline?
- `IMP-0010` **Usage context**: On-the-go vs desk, quick glance vs focused reading?
- `IMP-0011` **User expectations**: What do users expect on this platform?
- `IMP-0012` **Identify adaptation challenges**:
- `IMP-0013` What won't fit? (Content, navigation, features)
- `IMP-0014` What won't work? (Hover states on touch, tiny touch targets)
- `IMP-0015` What's inappropriate? (Desktop patterns on mobile, mobile patterns on desktop)
- `IMP-0016` Single column instead of multi-column
- `IMP-0017` Vertical stacking instead of side-by-side
- `IMP-0018` Full-width components instead of fixed widths
- `IMP-0019` Bottom navigation instead of top/side navigation
- `IMP-0020` Touch targets 44x44px minimum (not hover-dependent)
- `IMP-0021` Swipe gestures where appropriate (lists, carousels)
- `IMP-0022` Bottom sheets instead of dropdowns
- `IMP-0023` Thumbs-first design (controls within thumb reach)
- `IMP-0024` Larger tap areas with more spacing
- `IMP-0025` Progressive disclosure (don't show everything at once)
- `IMP-0026` Prioritize primary content (secondary content in tabs/accordions)
- `IMP-0027` Shorter text (more concise)
- `IMP-0028` Larger text (16px minimum)
- `IMP-0029` Hamburger menu or bottom navigation
- `IMP-0030` Reduce navigation complexity
- `IMP-0031` Sticky headers for context
- `IMP-0032` Back button in navigation flow
- `IMP-0033` Two-column layouts (not single or three-column)
- `IMP-0034` Side panels for secondary content
- `IMP-0035` Master-detail views (list + detail)
- `IMP-0036` Adaptive based on orientation (portrait vs landscape)
- `IMP-0037` Support both touch and pointer
- `IMP-0038` Touch targets 44x44px but allow denser layouts than phone
- `IMP-0039` Side navigation drawers
- `IMP-0040` Multi-column forms where appropriate
- `IMP-0041` Multi-column layouts (use horizontal space)
- `IMP-0042` Side navigation always visible
- `IMP-0043` Multiple information panels simultaneously
- `IMP-0044` Fixed widths with max-width constraints (don't stretch to 4K)
- `IMP-0045` Hover states for additional information
- `IMP-0046` Keyboard shortcuts
- `IMP-0047` Right-click context menus
- `IMP-0048` Drag and drop where helpful
- `IMP-0049` Multi-select with Shift/Cmd
- `IMP-0050` Show more information upfront (less progressive disclosure)
- `IMP-0051` Data tables with many columns
- `IMP-0052` Richer visualizations
- `IMP-0053` More detailed descriptions
- `IMP-0054` Page breaks at logical points
- `IMP-0055` Remove navigation, footer, interactive elements
- `IMP-0056` Black and white (or limited color)
- `IMP-0057` Proper margins for binding
- `IMP-0058` Expand shortened content (show full URLs, hidden sections)
- `IMP-0059` Add page numbers, headers, footers
- `IMP-0060` Include metadata (print date, page title)
- `IMP-0061` Convert charts to print-friendly versions
- `IMP-0062` Narrow width (600px max)
- `IMP-0063` Single column only
- `IMP-0064` Inline CSS (no external stylesheets)
- `IMP-0065` Table-based layouts (for email client compatibility)
- `IMP-0066` Large, obvious CTAs (buttons not text links)
- `IMP-0067` No hover states (not reliable)
- `IMP-0068` Deep links to web app for complex interactions
- `IMP-0069` Mobile: 320px-767px
- `IMP-0070` Tablet: 768px-1023px
- `IMP-0071` Desktop: 1024px+
- `IMP-0072` Or content-driven breakpoints (where design breaks)
- `IMP-0073` **CSS Grid/Flexbox**: Reflow layouts automatically
- `IMP-0074` **Container Queries**: Adapt based on container, not viewport
- `IMP-0075` **`clamp()`**: Fluid sizing between min and max
- `IMP-0076` **Media queries**: Different styles for different contexts
- `IMP-0077` **Display properties**: Show/hide elements per context
- `IMP-0078` Increase touch target sizes (44x44px minimum)
- `IMP-0079` Add more spacing between interactive elements
- `IMP-0080` Remove hover-dependent interactions
- `IMP-0081` Add touch feedback (ripples, highlights)
- `IMP-0082` Consider thumb zones (easier to reach bottom than top)
- `IMP-0083` Use `display: none` sparingly (still downloads)
- `IMP-0084` Progressive enhancement (core content first, enhancements on larger screens)
- `IMP-0085` Lazy loading for off-screen content
- `IMP-0086` Responsive images (`srcset`, `picture` element)
- `IMP-0087` Transform complex nav to hamburger/drawer on mobile
- `IMP-0088` Bottom nav bar for mobile apps
- `IMP-0089` Persistent side navigation on desktop
- `IMP-0090` Breadcrumbs on smaller screens for context
- `IMP-0091` Hide core functionality on mobile (if it matters, make it work)
- `IMP-0092` Assume desktop = powerful device (consider accessibility, older machines)
- `IMP-0093` Use different information architecture across contexts (confusing)
- `IMP-0094` Break user expectations for platform (mobile users expect mobile patterns)
- `IMP-0095` Forget landscape orientation on mobile/tablet
- `IMP-0096` Use generic breakpoints blindly (use content-driven breakpoints)
- `IMP-0097` Ignore touch on desktop (many desktop devices have touch)
- `IMP-0098` **Real devices**: Test on actual phones, tablets, desktops
- `IMP-0099` **Different orientations**: Portrait and landscape
- `IMP-0100` **Different browsers**: Safari, Chrome, Firefox, Edge
- `IMP-0101` **Different OS**: iOS, Android, Windows, macOS
- `IMP-0102` **Different input methods**: Touch, mouse, keyboard
- `IMP-0103` **Edge cases**: Very small screens (320px), very large screens (4K)
- `IMP-0104` **Slow connections**: Test on throttled network
- `IMP-0105` **Primary gesture**: Tap it and confirm it responds as designed, then drag it with the target input method; the drag must complete, not just start
- `IMP-0106` **Scroll across it**: A swipe along the page's scroll axis across the control scrolls the page or container without activating it; a drag that starts on the control along its axis moves the control, not the page. Neither failure throws an error, so try both
- `IMP-0107` **Evidence**: Say what produced the evidence: an emulated viewport, synthesized touch input through a browser tool, which engine ran it (Chromium is not Safari), or a physical device. Screenshots and resized viewports verify layout, never a gesture. Name what stayed untested and move on; unreachable hardware is a reported gap, not a blocker
- `IMP-0108` `srcset` lists available images with their actual widths (`w` descriptors)
- `IMP-0109` `sizes` tells the browser how wide the image will display
- `IMP-0110` Browser picks the best file based on viewport width AND device pixel ratio
- `IMP-0111` Actual touch interactions
- `IMP-0112` Real CPU/memory constraints
- `IMP-0113` Network latency patterns
- `IMP-0114` Font rendering differences
- `IMP-0115` Browser chrome/keyboard appearances
- `IMP-0116` **Persuade + Experience:** motion may carry the voice. Prefer one rehearsed focal sequence to repeated section reveals.
- `IMP-0117` **Operate + Read:** motion serves feedback, state, and continuity. Keep routine transitions fast and do not make users wait through page-load choreography.
- `IMP-0118` **Native (`ios` / `android` / `adaptive`):** follow the Motion section of [ios.md](ios.md) or [android.md](android.md), including the platform's Reduce Motion behavior. Do not apply the web tooling below.
- `IMP-0119` acknowledge an action;
- `IMP-0120` make a state change or spatial relationship legible;
- `IMP-0121` preserve continuity through navigation or layout change;
- `IMP-0122` direct attention at a meaningful moment;
- `IMP-0123` embody the selected visual world.
- `IMP-0124` **Focal moment:** the one sequence or interaction that deserves authorship, if any.
- `IMP-0125` **Continuity:** the state, layout, or navigation changes that need explanation.
- `IMP-0126` **Feedback:** the controls and outcomes that need acknowledgment.
- `IMP-0127` **Budget:** which effects may be expensive and how often they run.
- `IMP-0128` **Continuity and relationship:** shared-element motion, FLIP-style transforms, view transitions, or deliberate spatial movement.
- `IMP-0129` **Focus and depth:** bounded blur, filter, backdrop, light, or shadow changes.
- `IMP-0130` **Reveal and composition:** masks, clip paths, cropping, or controlled occlusion.
- `IMP-0131` **Material and energy:** color, gradient position, texture, distortion, or shader effects when the world and runtime support them.
- `IMP-0132` **State and feedback:** the smallest change that makes cause and result unmistakable.
- `IMP-0133` Use CSS transitions and keyframes for declarative state and bounded sequences.
- `IMP-0134` Use Web Animations API or the project's existing motion library for interruption, sequencing, and dynamic values.
- `IMP-0135` Use View Transitions or shared-element techniques when continuity across states is the point.
- `IMP-0136` Use scroll-driven motion only when the scroll relationship itself carries meaning, with a robust fallback.
- `IMP-0137` Do not add a dependency for an effect the existing stack can express cleanly.
- `IMP-0138` The focal motion is specific to the selected world and surface.
- `IMP-0139` Every supporting animation explains feedback, state, or relationship.
- `IMP-0140` Interruption and repeated use behave correctly.
- `IMP-0141` Desktop, mobile, and keyboard paths remain usable.
- `IMP-0142` The `prefers-reduced-motion` path reduces movement without erasing meaningful feedback or state changes.
- `IMP-0143` Expensive effects stay smooth on the target device.
- `IMP-0144` Removing an animation would lose meaning or authored character, not merely decoration.
- `IMP-0145` **Contrast issues**: Text contrast ratios < 4.5:1 (or 7:1 for AAA) [D06]
- `IMP-0146` **Motion sensitivity**: `prefers-reduced-motion` needs an intentional alternative that preserves state change and hierarchy; flag a global `0.01ms` kill that destroys useful feedback, flashing above threshold, and motion that blocks focus, reading, or task completion
- `IMP-0147` **Missing ARIA**: Interactive elements without proper roles, labels, or states
- `IMP-0148` **Keyboard navigation**: Missing focus indicators, illogical tab order, keyboard traps [H02]
- `IMP-0149` **Semantic HTML**: Improper heading hierarchy, missing landmarks, divs instead of buttons
- `IMP-0150` **Alt text**: Missing or poor image descriptions [H04]
- `IMP-0151` **Form issues**: Inputs without labels, poor error messaging, missing required indicators [H09]
- `IMP-0152` **Layout thrashing**: Reading/writing layout properties in loops
- `IMP-0153` **Expensive animations**: Casual layout-property animation, unbounded blur/filter/shadow effects, or effects that visibly drop frames
- `IMP-0154` **Missing optimization**: Images without lazy loading, unoptimized assets
- `IMP-0155` **will-change overuse**: `will-change` applied broadly or left on at rest (it is a targeted hint for known expensive animations, not a baseline requirement)
- `IMP-0156` **Bundle size**: Unnecessary imports, unused dependencies
- `IMP-0157` **Render performance**: Unnecessary re-renders, missing memoization
- `IMP-0158` **Hard-coded colors**: Colors not using design tokens
- `IMP-0159` **Broken dark mode**: Missing dark mode variants, poor contrast in dark theme
- `IMP-0160` **Inconsistent tokens**: Using wrong tokens, mixing token types
- `IMP-0161` **Theme switching issues**: Values that don't update on theme change
- `IMP-0162` **Fixed widths**: Hard-coded widths that break on mobile
- `IMP-0163` **Touch targets**: Interactive elements < 44x44px
- `IMP-0164` **Broken touch interaction**: Custom sliders, drag surfaces, and scrollable control strips whose primary gesture fails under touch, that swallow page scroll or lose the drag to it, or that stay stuck after an interrupted gesture. Code tells: mouse-only handlers, no `touch-action` on a pointer-event drag surface, drag state that nothing clears on cancel, lost capture, or blur. Exercise the gesture when a browser tool can synthesize touch (a rendered viewport proves layout, not the gesture), then say what produced the evidence (emulated viewport, synthesized touch, which engine, physical device) and what stayed untested
- `IMP-0165` **Horizontal scroll**: Content overflow on narrow viewports
- `IMP-0166` **Text scaling**: Layouts that break when text size increases
- `IMP-0167` **Missing breakpoints**: No mobile/tablet variants
- `IMP-0168` Audit Health Score: **??/20** ([rating band])
- `IMP-0169` Total issues found (count by severity: P0/P1/P2/P3)
- `IMP-0170` Top 3-5 critical issues
- `IMP-0171` Recommended next steps
- `IMP-0172` **P0 Blocking**: Prevents task completion. Fix immediately
- `IMP-0173` **P1 Major**: Significant difficulty or WCAG AA violation. Fix before release
- `IMP-0174` **P2 Minor**: Annoyance, workaround exists. Fix in next pass
- `IMP-0175` **P3 Polish**: Nice-to-fix, no real user impact. Fix if time permits
- `IMP-0176` **[P?] Issue name**
- `IMP-0177` **Location**: Component, file, line
- `IMP-0178` **Category**: Accessibility / Performance / Theming / Responsive / Implementation Integrity
- `IMP-0179` **Impact**: How it affects users
- `IMP-0180` **WCAG/Standard**: Which standard it violates (if applicable)
- `IMP-0181` **Recommendation**: How to fix it
- `IMP-0182` **Suggested command**: Which command to use (prefer: /impeccable adapt, /impeccable animate, /impeccable audit, /impeccable bolder, /impeccable clarify, /impeccable colorize, /impeccable critique, /impeccable delight, /impeccable distill, /impeccable document, /impeccable harden, /impeccable layout, /impeccable onboard, /impeccable optimize, /impeccable overdrive, /impeccable polish, /impeccable quieter, /impeccable shape, /impeccable typeset)
- `IMP-0183` "Hard-coded colors appear in 15+ components, should use design tokens"
- `IMP-0184` "Touch targets consistently too small (<44px) throughout mobile experience"
- `IMP-0185` **[P?] `/command-name`**: Brief description (specific context from audit findings)
- `IMP-0186` **[P?] `/command-name`**: Brief description (specific context)
- `IMP-0187` Report issues without explaining impact (why does this matter?)
- `IMP-0188` Provide generic recommendations (be specific and actionable)
- `IMP-0189` Skip positive findings (celebrate what works)
- `IMP-0190` Forget to prioritize (everything can't be P0)
- `IMP-0191` Report false positives without verification
- `IMP-0192` **Amplify what the system already owns.** Reuse its motif and its type scale at full strength, turned up for this section rather than invented for it. The bolder version should look more like the same brand, not less.
- `IMP-0193` **Keep content true.** Existing claims are part of the scope: preserve them unless the user supplies replacements. If real evidence is essential to the direction but absent, ask for it.
- `IMP-0194` **Commit, then clarify.** Half-measures read as noise. Make the one decisive move completely, then quiet everything around it so the move is legible. If every element got louder, the section got flatter.
- `IMP-0195` **Give it its own rhythm.** The target should read as a peak in the scroll, a shift in density or pace from what surrounds it, not simply more of the same.
- `IMP-0196` Everything outside the named target is unchanged.
- `IMP-0197` No new color, font, or system primitive appeared without being asked for.
- `IMP-0198` The conventions the section carried, including anything that drives an action, still work the same way.
- `IMP-0199` The section is unmistakably the same brand, only more sure of itself.
- `IMP-0200` ambiguous nouns, verbs, and actions;
- `IMP-0201` internal jargon or assumed knowledge;
- `IMP-0202` vague labels, outcomes, and system states;
- `IMP-0203` missing consequences, recovery, or timing;
- `IMP-0204` inconsistent terminology and capitalization;
- `IMP-0205` redundant headings, intros, helper text, and confirmations;
- `IMP-0206` text that breaks at realistic widths or in translation;
- `IMP-0207` tone that ignores stress, risk, success, or urgency.
- `IMP-0208` the one fact the user needs now;
- `IMP-0209` the action available next;
- `IMP-0210` supporting context that changes the decision;
- `IMP-0211` the appropriate tone for this moment.
- `IMP-0212` what failed;
- `IMP-0213` why, when known and useful;
- `IMP-0214` how to recover or what alternative remains.
- `IMP-0215` Write complete translatable messages rather than concatenated fragments.
- `IMP-0216` Keep variables and numbers structured so translators can reorder them.
- `IMP-0217` Allow expansion instead of abbreviating prematurely.
- `IMP-0218` Make alt text convey the image's information; use empty alt for decoration. [H04]
- `IMP-0219` Keep screen-reader names aligned with visible labels and outcomes.
- `IMP-0220` Do not rely on punctuation, color, or iconography to carry the message alone.
- `IMP-0221` comprehension without hidden product knowledge;
- `IMP-0222` actionability at errors, empty states, and decision points;
- `IMP-0223` factual accuracy and consistent terminology;
- `IMP-0224` scanability at target widths and 200% zoom;
- `IMP-0225` long names, localization expansion, pluralization, and dynamic values;
- `IMP-0226` accessible names and announced state changes;
- `IMP-0227` tone appropriate to consequence and emotional context.
- `IMP-0228` **Persuade + Experience:** color may carry the voice and own large regions when the selected world calls for it.
- `IMP-0229` **Operate + Read:** color primarily encodes action, selection, status, wayfinding, and reading hierarchy. Rarity gives an accent force.
- `IMP-0230` which colors are confirmed brand commitments;
- `IMP-0231` current surface, text, action, and semantic roles;
- `IMP-0232` places where grayscale obscures hierarchy or state;
- `IMP-0233` contrast failures and color-only communication;
- `IMP-0234` light/dark or data-visualization requirements;
- `IMP-0235` whether the task asks for more color or a new identity.
- `IMP-0236` canvas and elevated surfaces;
- `IMP-0237` primary and secondary text;
- `IMP-0238` action, focus, and selection;
- `IMP-0239` borders and separators;
- `IMP-0240` success, warning, error, and information;
- `IMP-0241` data categories or scales when needed.
- `IMP-0242` Let the strongest color own a deliberate region or role instead of scattering tiny accents.
- `IMP-0243` Keep the primary action easy to find; do not spend its color on decoration.
- `IMP-0244` Tint neutrals only when the brand hue genuinely creates cohesion. Neutral gray is valid when it serves the world.
- `IMP-0245` On colored surfaces, derive secondary text from the foreground or surface hue rather than using washed-out generic gray.
- `IMP-0246` Keep semantic meanings consistent, but respect platform and domain conventions instead of assuming fixed hues.
- `IMP-0247` For data, use distinct lightness, chroma, shape, label, or pattern so color is not the only code.
- `IMP-0248` In dark mode, design surface elevation and contrast explicitly; do not invert the light theme mechanically.
- `IMP-0249` Define primitive values and semantic tokens when the project has a token system. Theme changes should normally remap semantic roles.
- `IMP-0250` Every color has a stable role or a world-specific atmospheric purpose.
- `IMP-0251` Attention lands on the intended action, content, or state.
- `IMP-0252` The palette works across quiet, dense, interactive, error, and empty states.
- `IMP-0253` Light and dark themes are each composed, not mechanically inverted.
- `IMP-0254` Contrast and non-color cues pass in all relevant states.
- `IMP-0255` The result is recognizably this product, not a generic “colorful” treatment.
- `IMP-0256` **Contrast:** body and placeholder text ≥4.5:1, large text ≥3:1. On colored surfaces tint secondary text from that hue or the foreground; never gray. [D06]
- `IMP-0257` **Depth:** shadows carry an offset and a soft blur. A zero-offset colored halo is decoration.
- `IMP-0258` **Spacing:** tight groups, generous separation, more space above a heading than below it. Read the computed values.
- `IMP-0259` **Type:** body measure 65–75ch, display max 6rem, tracking floor -0.04em, balanced headings, obvious scale and weight steps. Run the real copy at every breakpoint and fix what overflows.
- `IMP-0260` **Motion:** one authored moment, not scattered effects and not one identical entrance on every section. Exponential ease-out from an already-visible default. Reach past transform and opacity: blur, backdrop-filter, clip-path, mask, and shadow belong to the palette when they stay smooth.
- `IMP-0261` **States:** hover, disabled, loading, error, empty. Plus real content, working controls, responsive composition, keyboard focus.
- `IMP-0262` **Browser surfaces:** the parts you did not draw still carry the design. Text selection, the caret, custom scrollbars, focus rings, underline offset, and the numerals in tabular data all ship with browser defaults that belong to no design system. Theme them from the palette. This is the cheapest signal that a page was built rather than assembled, and the one models skip most reliably. [H02]
- `IMP-0263` **Copy:** the product's own language. Controls name their action; errors name the problem and the recovery.
- `IMP-0264` **Coverage:** every brief requirement present and findable within seconds.
- `IMP-0265` Same-size cards of icon plus heading plus text as the page structure. Cards are the lazy container; nested cards are always wrong.
- `IMP-0266` The hero-metric template: big number, small label, supporting stats, accent.
- `IMP-0267` A kicker or eyebrow above a heading. This one is a ban, not a default: no brief earns it back. The heading carries its own weight; delete the label and let the heading speak. [D03]
- `IMP-0268` Section numbers (01 / 02 / 03) unless the sequence itself carries information the reader needs.
- `IMP-0269` A modal for a task that needs neither interruption nor protected focus.
- `IMP-0270` Gradient text. Emphasis comes from weight or size. [H06]
- `IMP-0271` Glass and blur as decoration rather than as a specific effect.
- `IMP-0272` A colored `border-left` or `border-right` above 1px on cards, list items, callouts, or alerts. [D08]
- `IMP-0273` Hard offset shadows (`box-shadow: 4px 4px 0`) outside a world that is actually neobrutalist. The zero-blur block shadow is a costume, not a depth system; a world that did not choose it never earns it as a default.
- `IMP-0274` Sparklines, progress rings, and soft-shadowed rounded rectangles standing in for content.
- `IMP-0275` Monospace as a costume for "technical" rather than for code, data, or measurement.
- `IMP-0276` A system display face (Impact, Arial Black, the platform sans) as the display voice of an own-world page. Source and self-host a face whose character matches the approved lettering; the closest installed font is a failure, not a fallback.
- `IMP-0277` Unicode glyphs or emoji standing in for an icon system. Icons are drawn, from a real library or authored SVG, in one consistent stroke and weight. [D04, H08]
- `IMP-0278` Geometric masks standing in for organic contours. A circle, polygon, or radial-gradient cutout approximating a photographic subject's edge is the cheap version of the effect and reads worse than omitting it. Derive an alpha matte from the actual image, or produce a cut-out asset.
- `IMP-0279` Light or dark picked by category. Pick it from the use scene: who, where, under what ambient light.
- `IMP-0280` Assessment A (design review) and Assessment B (detector/browser evidence) are both required.
- `IMP-0281` Assessment A and B MUST run as two isolated sub-agents whenever a sub-agent/Task tool is exposed. Running them inline in this context is "possible" but is NOT permitted; it is a degraded run. Inline is allowed ONLY when no sub-agent tool exists (or the user declined, on harnesses that ask).
- `IMP-0282` If you degrade for any reason, the report's first line MUST be a banner: `⚠️ DEGRADED: single-context (<reason>)`. A silent degraded critique is a failed critique.
- `IMP-0283` Assessment A must finish before detector findings enter the parent synthesis context. Detector output is deterministic, but it still anchors judgment.
- `IMP-0284` A skipped detector is a failed critique run unless `impeccable detect` is missing or crashes after a real attempt.
- `IMP-0285` Viewable targets require browser inspection when available.
- `IMP-0286` Any local server started only for critique visualization must run in the background, have a recorded stop method, and be stopped before final reporting unless the user asks to keep it.
- `IMP-0287` Do not claim a user-visible overlay exists unless script injection succeeded and the detector ran in the page.
- `IMP-0288` The question is the LAST thing in the response. Write the entire report out first, then ask; nothing follows the question. Prose emitted after a structured question is withheld until the user answers it, so a report written after the question reads as if the critique never ran.
- `IMP-0289` A run that ends with neither the targeted questions nor a literal `Questions skipped: <reason>` line is an incomplete run. The report is not the finish; the close is.
- `IMP-0290` **Resolve the target** to a concrete file path or URL. Prefer a source path over a dev-server URL when both identify the same surface; ports drift, paths do not.
- `IMP-0291` "the homepage" -> `site/pages/index.astro` or `index.html`
- `IMP-0292` "the settings modal" -> the primary component file
- `IMP-0293` "this page" -> the current URL or source file
- `IMP-0294` **Confirm the target slugs cleanly**:
- `IMP-0295` **Read `.impeccable/critique/ignore.md`** if it exists. Drop matching findings silently; it is the only prior-run input critique consumes.
- `IMP-0296` Unless a harness-specific gate below overrides this, spawn A and B as two isolated, parallel sub-agents whenever a sub-agent/Task tool is exposed. This is the default and is mandatory; do not run them inline because it is faster.
- `IMP-0297` "Unavailable" means exactly one thing: no sub-agent/Task tool is exposed in this session (or, on harnesses that ask, the user declined). It does not mean inconvenient.
- `IMP-0298` If and only if sub-agents are unavailable, fall back sequentially: finish and record Assessment A, then run Assessment B, then synthesize, and emit the degraded banner.
- `IMP-0299` Whichever path you take, declare it in the report header (see Report header provenance). Skipping sub-agents without the banner is the most common failure of this command.
- `IMP-0300` **Design specificity**: Is the composition, interaction, and visual language grounded in this product, or could an unrelated product use it unchanged? Make this judgment before seeing detector output.
- `IMP-0301` **Holistic design**: hierarchy, IA, emotional fit, discoverability, composition, typography, color, accessibility, states, copy, and edge cases.
- `IMP-0302` **Cognitive load**: consult the [Cognitive Load Assessment](#cognitive-load-assessment) section below; report checklist failures and decision points with >4 visible options.
- `IMP-0303` **Emotional journey**: peak-end rule, emotional valleys, reassurance at high-stakes moments.
- `IMP-0304` **Nielsen heuristics**: consult the [Heuristics Scoring Guide](#heuristics-scoring-guide) section below; score all 10 heuristics 0-4, marking any heuristic the mode-applicability rule allows as `n/a` instead of forcing a number.
- `IMP-0305` Pass markup files/directories as `[target]`; do not pass CSS-only files.
- `IMP-0306` For URLs, skip CLI scan and use browser visualization.
- `IMP-0307` For very large trees (500+ scannable files), narrow scope or ask.
- `IMP-0308` Exit code 0 = clean; 2 = findings.
- `IMP-0309` If the detector entrypoint is missing or fails to load, report deterministic scan unavailable and continue with browser/manual review.
- `IMP-0310` Create a fresh tab and navigate. Prefer the harness's native/browser-canvas screenshot path before hand-rolling a Playwright/Puppeteer script; only fall back to a custom script when no native browser tool is exposed.
- `IMP-0311` Preflight mutable injection by setting `document.title` and appending a `<script>` tag. Read-only evaluate APIs do not count.
- `IMP-0312` If mutation is unavailable, skip live server, browser presentation, and injection; report fallback signal.
- `IMP-0313` If mutation is available, start `.claude/skills/impeccable/scripts/impeccable live-server --background`, present the browser if supported, label `[Human]`, scroll top, inject `http://localhost:PORT/detect.js`, wait 2-3 seconds, read `impeccable` console messages, then stop the live server.
- `IMP-0314` For multi-view targets, inject on 3-5 representative pages.
- `IMP-0315` Dual-agent: `Method: dual-agent (A: <agent-id> · B: <agent-id>)`
- `IMP-0316` Degraded: `⚠️ DEGRADED: single-context (<reason, e.g. no sub-agent tool exposed>)`
- `IMP-0317` **[P?] What**: Name the problem clearly
- `IMP-0318` **Why it matters**: How this hurts users or undermines goals
- `IMP-0319` **Fix**: What to do about it (be concrete)
- `IMP-0320` **Suggested command**: Which command could address this (from: /impeccable adapt, /impeccable animate, /impeccable audit, /impeccable bolder, /impeccable clarify, /impeccable colorize, /impeccable critique, /impeccable delight, /impeccable distill, /impeccable document, /impeccable harden, /impeccable layout, /impeccable onboard, /impeccable optimize, /impeccable overdrive, /impeccable polish, /impeccable quieter, /impeccable shape, /impeccable typeset)
- `IMP-0321` "What if the primary action were more prominent?"
- `IMP-0322` "Does this need to feel this complex?"
- `IMP-0323` "What would a confident version of this look like?"
- `IMP-0324` Be direct. Vague feedback wastes everyone's time.
- `IMP-0325` Be specific. "The submit button," not "some elements."
- `IMP-0326` Say what's wrong AND why it matters to users.
- `IMP-0327` Give concrete suggestions. Cut "consider exploring..." entirely.
- `IMP-0328` Prioritize ruthlessly. If everything is important, nothing is.
- `IMP-0329` Don't soften criticism. Developers need honest feedback to ship great design.
- `IMP-0330` **Write the body to a temp file** so you can pipe it to the helper. Use the full critique report (heuristic table, design-specificity verdict, priority issues, persona red flags, minor observations, and questions), but stop before the "Ask the User" / "Recommended Actions" sections that come later.
- `IMP-0331` **Pass the structured metadata** through `IMPECCABLE_CRITIQUE_META` (JSON), then run the write command:
- `IMP-0332` **Delete the temp body file** after the write attempt completes, whether the write succeeded or failed. If deletion fails, mention `temp-file cleanup failed: <reason>` briefly in the final output, but do not block the critique.
- `IMP-0333` **Read the trend** for context:
- `IMP-0334` **Append a single line to the user-visible output**, after the report and before the questions:
- `IMP-0335` **Close the run.** Go to Ask the User below and emit the questions, or the `Questions skipped: <reason>` line when the count allows it. The run is not complete until you do. Persistence is bookkeeping and cleanup is not an ending; stopping here leaves the user with a report and no way forward, and leaves `/impeccable polish` with no priorities to inherit.
- `IMP-0336` **Priority direction**: Based on the issues found, ask which category matters most to the user right now. For example: "I found problems with visual hierarchy, color usage, and information overload. Which area should we tackle first?" Offer the top 2-3 issue categories as options.
- `IMP-0337` **Design intent**: If the critique found a tonal mismatch, ask whether it was intentional. For example: "The interface feels clinical and corporate. Is that the intended tone, or should it feel warmer/bolder/more playful?" Offer 2-3 tonal directions as options based on what would fix the issues found.
- `IMP-0338` **Scope**: Ask how much the user wants to take on. For example: "I found N issues. Want to address everything, or focus on the top 3?" Offer scope options like "Top 3 only", "All issues", "Critical issues only".
- `IMP-0339` **Constraints** (optional; only ask if relevant): If the findings touch many areas, ask if anything is off-limits. For example: "Should any sections stay as-is?" This prevents the plan from touching things the user considers done.
- `IMP-0340` Every question must reference specific findings from the report. Never ask generic "who is your audience?" questions.
- `IMP-0341` Keep it to 2-4 questions maximum. Respect the user's time.
- `IMP-0342` Offer concrete options, not open-ended prompts.
- `IMP-0343` Skipping is allowed only when the report listed **fewer than 3 Priority Issues**. Count them; do not judge the findings "straightforward" by feel. At 3 or more, the questions are required.
- `IMP-0344` **`/command-name`**: Brief description of what to fix (specific context from critique findings)
- `IMP-0345` **`/command-name`**: Brief description (specific context)
- `IMP-0346` Only recommend commands from: /impeccable adapt, /impeccable animate, /impeccable audit, /impeccable bolder, /impeccable clarify, /impeccable colorize, /impeccable critique, /impeccable delight, /impeccable distill, /impeccable document, /impeccable harden, /impeccable layout, /impeccable onboard, /impeccable optimize, /impeccable overdrive, /impeccable polish, /impeccable quieter, /impeccable shape, /impeccable typeset
- `IMP-0347` Order by the user's stated priorities first, then by impact
- `IMP-0348` Each item's description should carry enough context that the command knows what to focus on
- `IMP-0349` Map each Priority Issue to the appropriate command
- `IMP-0350` Skip commands that would address zero issues
- `IMP-0351` If the user chose a limited scope, only include items within that scope
- `IMP-0352` If the user marked areas as off-limits, exclude commands that would touch those areas
- `IMP-0353` End with `/impeccable polish` as the final step if any fixes were recommended
- `IMP-0354` Breaking complex tasks into discrete steps
- `IMP-0355` Providing scaffolding (templates, defaults, examples)
- `IMP-0356` Progressive disclosure: show what's needed now, hide the rest
- `IMP-0357` Grouping related decisions together
- `IMP-0358` Confusing navigation that requires mental mapping
- `IMP-0359` Unclear labels that force users to guess meaning
- `IMP-0360` Visual clutter competing for attention
- `IMP-0361` Inconsistent patterns that prevent learning
- `IMP-0362` Unnecessary steps between user intent and result
- `IMP-0363` Progressive disclosure that reveals complexity gradually
- `IMP-0364` Consistent patterns that reward learning
- `IMP-0365` Feedback that confirms correct understanding
- `IMP-0366` Onboarding that teaches through action, not walls of text
- `IMP-0367` [ ] **Single focus**: Can the user complete their primary task without distraction from competing elements?
- `IMP-0368` [ ] **Chunking**: Is information presented in digestible groups (≤4 items per group)?
- `IMP-0369` [ ] **Grouping**: Are related items visually grouped together (proximity, borders, shared background)?
- `IMP-0370` [ ] **Visual hierarchy**: Is it immediately clear what's most important on the screen?
- `IMP-0371` [ ] **One thing at a time**: Can the user focus on a single decision before moving to the next?
- `IMP-0372` [ ] **Minimal choices**: Are decisions simplified (≤4 visible options at any decision point)?
- `IMP-0373` [ ] **Working memory**: Does the user need to remember information from a previous screen to act on the current one?
- `IMP-0374` [ ] **Progressive disclosure**: Is complexity revealed only when the user needs it?
- `IMP-0375` **≤4 items**: Within working memory limits, manageable
- `IMP-0376` **5–7 items**: Pushing the boundary; consider grouping or progressive disclosure
- `IMP-0377` **8+ items**: Overloaded; users will skip, misclick, or abandon
- `IMP-0378` Action buttons: 1 primary, 1–2 secondary, group the rest in a menu
- `IMP-0379` Navigation menus: ≤5 top-level items (group the rest under clear categories)
- `IMP-0380` Long-form articles: one reading path; gather related links into a single block at the end instead of scattering them mid-flow
- `IMP-0381` Documentation sidebars: ≤4 sibling choices visible per level before grouping kicks in
- `IMP-0382` Portfolio and gallery indexes: one decision per screen (which piece to open), not filter, sort, and tag controls all at once
- `IMP-0383` Loading indicators during async operations
- `IMP-0384` Confirmation of user actions (save, submit, delete)
- `IMP-0385` Progress indicators for multi-step processes
- `IMP-0386` Current location in navigation (breadcrumbs, active states)
- `IMP-0387` Form validation feedback (inline, not just on submit)
- `IMP-0388` Familiar terminology (no unexplained jargon)
- `IMP-0389` Logical information order matching user expectations
- `IMP-0390` Recognizable icons and metaphors
- `IMP-0391` Domain-appropriate language for the target audience
- `IMP-0392` Natural reading flow (left-to-right, top-to-bottom priority)
- `IMP-0393` Undo/redo functionality
- `IMP-0394` Cancel buttons on forms and modals
- `IMP-0395` Clear navigation back to safety (home, previous)
- `IMP-0396` Easy way to clear filters, search, selections
- `IMP-0397` Escape from long or multi-step processes
- `IMP-0398` Consistent terminology throughout the interface
- `IMP-0399` Same actions produce same results everywhere
- `IMP-0400` Platform conventions followed (standard UI patterns)
- `IMP-0401` Visual consistency (colors, typography, spacing, components)
- `IMP-0402` Consistent interaction patterns (same gesture = same behavior)
- `IMP-0403` Confirmation before destructive actions (delete, overwrite)
- `IMP-0404` Constraints preventing invalid input (date pickers, dropdowns)
- `IMP-0405` Smart defaults that reduce errors
- `IMP-0406` Clear labels that prevent misunderstanding
- `IMP-0407` Autosave and draft recovery
- `IMP-0408` Visible options (not buried in hidden menus)
- `IMP-0409` Contextual help when needed (tooltips, inline hints)
- `IMP-0410` Recent items and history
- `IMP-0411` Autocomplete and suggestions
- `IMP-0412` Labels on icons (not icon-only navigation)
- `IMP-0413` Keyboard shortcuts for common actions
- `IMP-0414` Customizable interface elements
- `IMP-0415` Recent items and favorites
- `IMP-0416` Bulk/batch actions
- `IMP-0417` Power user features that don't complicate the basics
- `IMP-0418` Only necessary information visible at each step
- `IMP-0419` Clear visual hierarchy directing attention
- `IMP-0420` Purposeful use of color and emphasis
- `IMP-0421` No decorative clutter competing for attention
- `IMP-0422` Focused, uncluttered layouts
- `IMP-0423` Plain language error messages (no error codes for users)
- `IMP-0424` Specific problem identification ("Email is missing @" not "Invalid input")
- `IMP-0425` Actionable recovery suggestions
- `IMP-0426` Errors displayed near the source of the problem
- `IMP-0427` Non-blocking error handling (don't wipe the form)
- `IMP-0428` Searchable help or documentation
- `IMP-0429` Contextual help (tooltips, inline hints, guided tours)
- `IMP-0430` Task-focused organization (not feature-organized)
- `IMP-0431` Concise, scannable content
- `IMP-0432` Easy access without leaving current context
- `IMP-0433` Skips all onboarding and instructions
- `IMP-0434` Looks for keyboard shortcuts immediately
- `IMP-0435` Tries to bulk-select, batch-edit, and automate
- `IMP-0436` Gets frustrated by required steps that feel unnecessary
- `IMP-0437` Abandons if anything feels slow or patronizing
- `IMP-0438` Can Alex complete the core task in under 60 seconds?
- `IMP-0439` Are there keyboard shortcuts for common actions?
- `IMP-0440` Can onboarding be skipped entirely?
- `IMP-0441` Do modals have keyboard dismiss (Esc)?
- `IMP-0442` Is there a "power user" path (shortcuts, bulk actions)?
- `IMP-0443` Forced tutorials or unskippable onboarding
- `IMP-0444` No keyboard navigation for primary actions
- `IMP-0445` Slow animations that can't be skipped
- `IMP-0446` One-item-at-a-time workflows where batch would be natural
- `IMP-0447` Redundant confirmation steps for low-risk actions
- `IMP-0448` Reads all instructions carefully
- `IMP-0449` Hesitates before clicking anything unfamiliar
- `IMP-0450` Looks for help or support constantly
- `IMP-0451` Misunderstands jargon and abbreviations
- `IMP-0452` Takes the most literal interpretation of any label
- `IMP-0453` Is the first action obviously clear within 5 seconds?
- `IMP-0454` Are all icons labeled with text?
- `IMP-0455` Is there contextual help at decision points?
- `IMP-0456` Does terminology assume prior knowledge?
- `IMP-0457` Is there a clear "back" or "undo" at every step?
- `IMP-0458` Icon-only navigation with no labels
- `IMP-0459` Technical jargon without explanation
- `IMP-0460` No visible help option or guidance
- `IMP-0461` Ambiguous next steps after completing an action
- `IMP-0462` No confirmation that an action succeeded
- `IMP-0463` Tabs through the interface linearly
- `IMP-0464` Relies on ARIA labels and heading structure
- `IMP-0465` Cannot see hover states or visual-only indicators
- `IMP-0466` Needs adequate color contrast (4.5:1 minimum) [D06]
- `IMP-0467` May use browser zoom up to 200%
- `IMP-0468` Can the entire primary flow be completed keyboard-only?
- `IMP-0469` Are all interactive elements focusable with visible focus indicators? [H02]
- `IMP-0470` Do images have meaningful alt text? [H04]
- `IMP-0471` Is color contrast WCAG AA compliant (4.5:1 for text)? [D06]
- `IMP-0472` Does the screen reader announce state changes (loading, success, errors)?
- `IMP-0473` Click-only interactions with no keyboard alternative
- `IMP-0474` Missing or invisible focus indicators [H02]
- `IMP-0475` Meaning conveyed by color alone (red = error, green = success)
- `IMP-0476` Unlabeled form fields or buttons
- `IMP-0477` Time-limited actions without extension option
- `IMP-0478` Custom components that break screen reader flow
- `IMP-0479` Tests edge cases intentionally (empty states, long strings, special characters)
- `IMP-0480` Submits forms with unexpected data (emoji, RTL text, very long values)
- `IMP-0481` Tries to break workflows by navigating backwards, refreshing mid-flow, or opening in multiple tabs
- `IMP-0482` Looks for inconsistencies between what the UI promises and what actually happens
- `IMP-0483` Documents problems methodically
- `IMP-0484` What happens at the edges (0 items, 1000 items, very long text)?
- `IMP-0485` Do error states recover gracefully or leave the UI in a broken state?
- `IMP-0486` What happens on refresh mid-workflow? Is state preserved?
- `IMP-0487` Are there features that appear to work but produce broken results?
- `IMP-0488` How does the UI handle unexpected input (emoji, special chars, paste from Excel)?
- `IMP-0489` Features that appear to work but silently fail or produce wrong results
- `IMP-0490` Error handling that exposes technical details or leaves UI in a broken state
- `IMP-0491` Empty states that show nothing useful ("No results" with no guidance)
- `IMP-0492` Workflows that lose user data on refresh or navigation
- `IMP-0493` Inconsistent behavior between similar interactions in different parts of the UI
- `IMP-0494` Uses thumb only; prefers bottom-of-screen actions
- `IMP-0495` Gets interrupted mid-flow and returns later
- `IMP-0496` Switches between apps frequently
- `IMP-0497` Has limited attention span and low patience
- `IMP-0498` Types as little as possible, prefers taps and selections
- `IMP-0499` Are primary actions in the thumb zone (bottom half of screen)?
- `IMP-0500` Is state preserved if the user leaves and returns?
- `IMP-0501` Does it work on slow connections (3G)?
- `IMP-0502` Can forms use autocomplete and smart defaults?
- `IMP-0503` Are touch targets at least 44×44pt?
- `IMP-0504` Important actions positioned at the top of the screen (unreachable by thumb)
- `IMP-0505` No state persistence; progress lost on tab switch or interruption
- `IMP-0506` Large text inputs required where selection would work
- `IMP-0507` Heavy assets loading on every page (no lazy loading)
- `IMP-0508` Tiny tap targets or targets too close together
- `IMP-0509` Read the target audience description
- `IMP-0510` Identify the primary user archetype not covered by the 5 predefined personas
- `IMP-0511` Create a persona following this template:
- `IMP-0512` **Evidence.** Before any other check, verify the required captures exist and every capture is valid. Required: the platform's full viewport set (web: `desktop.png` and `mobile.png`; native: one capture per shipped device class), plus every capture the calling brief names as required, a reported user viewport (`user-<width>.png`) included. Valid: no black or blank regions, content matching what the filename claims (a visit capture showing the About section is invalid), the document top visible where the file claims a full page, dimensions that make sense for the named viewport. A required capture that is absent fails exactly like one that is malformed: a viewport nobody captured is a viewport nobody inspected, and it cannot ship. When any capture fails, the whole review changes shape: return `disposition: recapture` as the first line, then one section, `recapture`, listing each missing or invalid file and what a valid capture of it shows, and stop. Never build a matrix on malformed evidence; a verdict derived from a broken capture launders the breakage into an approval, and the parent owes you a full re-review on valid captures, not a scoring round.
- `IMP-0513` **Persistence.** PRODUCT.md exists. On a comp-led build, `.impeccable/build/state.json` exists and every phase before `review` is `closed` or explicitly `skipped`; an open or failed phase is a material finding. A comp-led config with no state file, or a state whose `comps` phase is neither `closed` nor `skipped`, means the comp round was skipped and the build ran from a world description alone, a material finding that outranks craft; a phase closed with a `forced` record is disclosed as a material finding unless the user downgraded the comp in words the packet quotes; a state file whose `hero.gate.score` sits under 0.72, or a missing state file, means the reproduction ran unproven, a material finding, and `.impeccable/review/hero-repro.png` must exist either way. When DESIGN.md predates this build (an extension or redesign), it matches the built world; on a new world it is written after this review by the documenter, so its absence here is not a finding. When comp-round comps exist under `.impeccable/mocks/`, an approval record exists too: the surface brief naming the approved comp, or an `approved` flag in its sidecar. Comp-round comps with no recorded pick mean the approval point was skipped, a material finding. Files under `.impeccable/mocks/decision/` are exempt: they are the direction round's dealt hand, produced before any comp round, and imply no approval whatever the build path; a code-led build has no comp round at all.
- `IMP-0514` **Fidelity.** Start from the measurement, then judge what it cannot: read `.impeccable/review/diff/final/report.json` (and hero) first; every region scored `missing` or `contradicted` is a matrix row in that state unless the paired crop under `regions/` shows the score is wrong, and you say why; a region scored `match` still gets your eye for lettering character and material, which the numbers do not measure. Then, against your own element inventory of the approved comp, never against the contract's summary of it: topology, reading order, focal scale, overlaps and z-order, density, signature geometry, the primary action's treatment (a CTA the comp physically works, dissolves, or stamps is a signature element; its plain-rectangle rendition is contradicted), navigation items and icons, headline levels and scale relationships. Classify every salient element: match, acceptable adaptation, missing, contradicted, or added without approval. Three rows are mandatory in every matrix. TYPE: the display lettering's character, compression, width, weight, contrast, terminals, against the comp's; a face of a different character is contradicted however the layout matches. MATERIAL: an element rendered as flat CSS or clean vector where the comp shows painted, textured, dimensional, or photographic material is contradicted regardless of placement; medium is part of the promise. GROUND: the page field's value and temperature against the comp's, sampled from pixels on both sides when tooling allows rather than judged from memory, and read as the net on-screen result where a texture or tile paints over the base color; a ground warmer or cooler than the comp's is contradicted however faithfully the layout matches, and drift toward the rendition prior (warm cream on light grounds, blue-black slate on dark) is the direction to hunt. With no approved comp, TYPE and MATERIAL do not lapse: judge them against the contract's OWN-WORLD and the world's real materials, and treat faked physicality (CSS bevels, embossing, stamped-metal or chalk effects imitating a material the page never renders) as contradicted on its face; imitation material is the single most reliable mark of machine-made design.
